# CHAPTER VIII.

## WHOSE SUPREME PRINCE IS MASERIEL
### *He dwells in that region which we call <term>Favonius<note>The Roman name for the West Wind, associated with the beginning of spring.</note></term>, having under him 60 principal leaders with their many servants.*

<image-desc>A square woodcut decorative initial letter 'O'. The interior of the frame depicts a seated figure in a high-backed chair, possibly a scholar or authority figure, surrounded by ornamental scrolling foliage and architectural flourishes.</image-desc>

The eighth station is toward the West, in that place from which the wind called Favonius blows. There dwells a great prince named Maseriel with an infinite multitude of leaders, princes, and attendants. Of these, thirty princes are assigned to this art to oversee daytime operations, and just as many oversee nighttime operations, along with many servants. All of these are appointed to announce and report the secrets of the human arts: Philosophy, Magic, <term>Necromancy<note>Literally "divination through the dead," though in this context, it often refers more broadly to forbidden or hidden knowledge.</note></term>, and all the wonderful and most secret operations which are known to very few men. In these matters, they are prompt and very faithful; they do not return until they have completed everything committed to them. They are quiet and come without noise, as they are commanded—either alone, with many, with few, or even without any visible servants. They terrify no one, except for those who, being uninstructed in the art, presume to call them through the true principles of the art, which no one easily reaches without a teacher. However, so that you may have at hand those whom you call for the operation

You have indeed out of the thirty principal spirits of Maseriel <note>The presiding spirit of the West Wind.</note> twenty-four with seven hundred and twenty servants, of whom the first twelve preside over daytime operations, and the remaining twelve over those of the night. When, therefore, you wish to work anything in <term>Steganography<note>From the Greek "steganos" (hidden) and "graphein" (writing); the art of secret communication.</note></term> through them, turn yourself toward <term>Favonius<note>The Roman name for the West Wind.</note></term>, and having performed the necessary things required by the art, call one of the aforementioned whom you wish, as is proper; and he will come without delay. The conjuration: 

> *Maseriel bulā lamodyn charnoty Carmephin iabrū carefathroyn asulroy beuesy Cadumyn turiel bulan Seuear; almos lycadusel ernoty panier iethar eare pheory bulan thorty parō Venio Fabelronthusy.* <note>This "conjuration" is a null-cipher. The bizarre words are not magical incantations but a container for a hidden message. To the uninitiated, it looks like demonic summoning; to the recipient, it is a coded letter.</note>

When the spirit you have called appears, entrust your secret to him to be carried securely, hesitating not at all, speaking silently to him if you are in the midst of others, as you know how to do.

->*Let the secret of your mind be such:* :-<-

*You have secrets in the occult sciences of philosophy, the magic of the stars, Arithmetical <term>Kabbalah<note>A mystical Jewish tradition that Trithemius adapted into a system of mathematical and alphabetic cryptography.</note></term>, or any other arts which you desire a friend, present or absent far away, to know: but they are such things that it is not safe to commit to letters or to messengers; or perhaps it is urgent that he send secrets to you: or even to commend a philosopher so that he may not be known by others. Call one or two of the above-mentioned spirits: explain the secret: he takes up the burden and will carry it to your friend, and will report back to you what has been entrusted by him.*

# BOOK ONE

D 2

->circum-<-

*Write whatever prayer you wish, or any letters containing no secret.*

> **A**lmighty eternal God, most just rewarder of the good, who willed that your Son become a participant in our race so that he might redeem us, most wretched ones, from diabolical envy; who by his kindness alone took upon himself our incorruptible form from the flower of the virginal womb, with the holy Archangel Gabriel announcing that the most blessed Virgin would conceive by your Spirit and remain a perpetual Virgin, immaculate, brighter than men and more preeminent than the angelic spirits. She gave birth to the almighty King, God and man, the most holy and revered Virgin Mary, entirely ignorant of a man’s company, giving birth without pain, looking upon the crying God and man without sadness, remaining always immaculate, most pure, most innocent, higher than men by the virtue of humility, full of charity, most worthy of all praise, and to be venerated by all: most blessed of the Angels and always to be praised, most radiant Empress, noble, renowned, and truly most splendid: whom your Son admits with most generous worthiness in everything she might ask, and reverently venerates her as a mother, more excellent than all creatures: to whom he is accustomed never to deny anything she requests, but reverently consents to your most holy will, granting all things to her honor, she who will stand for us on the last day, helping us who venerate the illustrious merits of the nourishing Virgin. O God, light of truth to us, we most devotedly pray, pour out your grace and mercy, O God, upon all who invoke you and who humbly seek the remission of their sins, and do not look down upon us wretched ones, but mercifully receive us who trust in your goodness, through the love of the Virgin Mary, rejoicing in God, and of all your elect. Amen. I, Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, wrote this. In the year of the Lord’s Incarnation or birth, 1500, on the fourth day before the Ides of March <note>original: "Iduum Mar. quarto"; this corresponds to March 12, 1500.</note>.

---

The person to whom the letters are sent, upon receiving them and recognizing the "wood" of Maseriel <note>The "wood" or "matter" (lignum) likely refers to a specific cryptographic key or a physical token associated with this class of spirits.</note>, should turn toward the West Wind <note>original: "Fauonium"; the Roman name for the West Wind, associated with the prince Maseriel.</note>, and having secretly performed the preliminary actions required by the art, should call upon the spirit itself that was sent, adding the Conjuration.

# CHAPTER IX.
## THE PRINCE OF WHICH IS CALLED MALGA-
->*ras, who dwells in the west, having under him 30 dukes for daytime*<-
->*actions and as many for nighttime: whose duty*<-
->*is to announce the domestic secrets*<-
->*of friends.*<-

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'N' featuring floral patterns and a small figure or face within the letter's structure.</image-desc>

The inhabitant of the ninth mansion, which is in the equinoctial <term>equinoctial west</term><note>The point on the horizon where the sun sets during the spring and autumn equinoxes; due west.</note> west, is Malgaras, the greatest prince of the west, who has under him 30 dukes who preside over the day, and 30 others who preside over the night, along with their servants. The duty of all these is to report the secrets and private domestic matters of friends to their friends. These dukes are good and most ready to obey the one calling them; especially the daytime ones, since they are more accustomed to associating with men. For the nighttime spirits, although they are good, do not willingly associate with men, but instead flee and detest the light above all else, desiring to hide in the shadows; for that reason, they frequently appear to the one calling them through conjurations in the form of bats. From all of these, we will set down twenty-four of their names in a table, of which twelve for the day and as many for the night are sufficient.

You now have twelve of the dukes of Malgaras <note>The Latin text here reads "Malgarctis," likely a grammatical corruption of the Prince's name, Malgaras.</note> for daytime actions, and as many for nighttime, along with their six hundred and forty servants: and note that Aroyz and Baliel are always solitary travelers; Mitiel, Baras, Aspor, and Deilas can be called in pairs, while the rest are called in threes. When you wish to have three, or two, or one, take care that you do not err in their names. As the table teaches you, so shall you do without change, lest you fall into danger.

->*The Song of Conjuration.*<-

<margin>Rothi</margin>Malgaras ador chamelo buluctiny maresbo bodyr Cadu: mir auiel calinyo tedy pleoryn viordi cate viorba, chameron vesy thuriel vlnauy, beuefy meuo chasmironty naor ernyso, chorny barmo caleuodyn barso thubra sol. <note>While this sounds like a magical incantation, in the context of Steganography, these "magic words" actually function as a code or key to hide a secret message within seemingly nonsensical prose.</note> Once the conjuration has been spoken, be a strong and constant man: those whom you have called will immediately appear visible to you. But if those called from the nighttime spirits do not come immediately, do not on that account stop your work; rather, urge them on with a repeated conjuration until they obey. For they are somewhat lazy and do not willingly come among men, as we have said before.

->*Let there be a secret of the mind, which it pleases to commit neither to man nor to letters.*<-

<margin>S: 18.</margin>In those matters which pertain to family affairs, money, a deposit, a loan, faith, or any business of human conversation, you have a Secret which you desire an absent friend to know far away: but it is of such a nature that it would undergo public scrutiny with the greatest danger or detriment to both [parties]: for that reason, it must neither be trusted to letters nor to men. Therefore, so that you may be secure and respect the conscience of no one <note>Meaning, you need not worry about a messenger's prying eyes or guilt.</note>, turn yourself toward the west, call the duke, and commit the secret [to him]: he goes, performs all things faithfully, and reports back if anything has been committed to you in return.

->*Write whatever letters you wish, in which you fear the sight of no one.*<-

Health and affection, most beloved and singular friend; the bearer of these presents, originating from Roermond <note>original: "Ruremunda," a city in the Netherlands.</note>, is very beneficial and glorious toward our friends; I ask that you receive him humanely, and he will explain his necessity to you. Since he has always been joined to me by an old friendship, I would judge it most sweet, according to my strength, to relieve him and heap benefits upon him. And would that <meta>catchword: I might be able to (possem)</meta>

# STEGANOGRAPHY

I might be able to offer some consolation for his calamities. But since I am (as is clearer to you than the light of day) exhausted by heavy losses, I cannot satisfy my own zeal as I would wish. You, however, who abound in the necessary things of this world, show yourself a joyful friend to him, and show yourself—as you have been accustomed—to be a humane patron to friends in need. I ask you, therefore, to lend him two hundred florins <note>original: "florenos" — a common gold currency in Europe during the Renaissance.</note> under a fixed term, because he has been placed in the greatest necessity, as he himself will show you. I trust that he will restore everything in full when the appointed term arrives. If you do this, I will give you thanks that shall never die, and I will stand by you in more pleasing matters. Act now, so that we may prove your sincere love for us by its effect. Know that whatever you do for him, I wish to be considered as done for me; nor will you have bound a man less grateful than himself to you (believe this firmly), and when the term has passed, he will restore everything most fully. For he is accustomed to keep his promises inviolably and to show himself constant to all men.

Furthermore, since you enjoy hearing new rumors, I wish to write something to you. I have raised a puppy <note>The "puppy" here is a steganographic metaphor. Trithemius is not actually describing a dog, but rather the "spirit" or the method of secret communication he is teaching the reader.</note> (rightly called *Erit* <note>original: "erit" — a Greek-derived name likely meaning "worker" or "contender."</note> by a Greek name) with such great education, learning, and a memory for all things, that he seems to some to have cast off the beast and put on the man. He understands Greek and Latin words; he knows how to seek pardon for his offenses. Whatever I command, he does; and at a nod of my gestures, he reveals the secrets of men. You would see him imbued with such discipline of character that you would judge him more learned than many men. When ordered to open a window, he opens it, and surpassing his own nature by his wit, if forbidden from a thing, he puts it back. He carries the burden placed upon him and presents it. He shows himself a zealous defender of me, plays excellently, and does things altogether incredible to those unaccustomed to them. Farewell. Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Saint Martin in Sponheim, wrote this on the 13th of March <note>original: "3. Id Marty" — the 3rd of the Ides of March.</note> in the year 1500. <note>floral flourish ornament</note>

When the friend to whom these or any other such letters are sent receives them, he understands them to be a sign of the Prince of the West. Therefore, after the customary preliminaries have been performed, he turns himself toward the West and calls the Spirit, calling the invisible messenger to witness, so that he may become visible and tell no one.

->**The Song of the Spirit.**<-

MALGARADAS apro chameron afuty mesary throes Zamedo fogreal paredon adre Caphoron onatyr tirno beofy. Chameron phorly mellon tedrumarsy dumaso duiise Casmiel elthurnpeson alproys fabelronty Sturno panalmonador. <note>While this appears to be a magical incantation to summon the spirit Casmiel, it is actually a cipher. The secret message is hidden within these invented words according to a specific system of letter substitution or extraction.</note> Once the song is completed, the Spirit who was called will be present in his usual manner, revealing to you those things which he received in his commands; he is a faithful messenger with whom you will be able to send back to your friend whatever secret you wish him to know.

# BOOK ONE. CHAPTER X.

# WHOSE SUPREME PRINCE IS CALLED DOROTHIEL,
*inhabiting that region of the world which is called Chorus <note>The Latin name for the Northwest wind.</note> after the wind; and he has under him many dukes and servants.*

THE TENTH station is called Chorus after the wind, in which dwells the great prince Dorothiel, who has under his power and dominion 40 dukes who preside over daytime operations, and just as many who preside over nighttime ones, not counting the almost infinite servants and attendants. Their duty is to announce all secrets which pertain to spiritual matters, and to ecclesiastical <term>gifts, benefices, prelacies, dignities</term> <note>These terms refer to various ranks and property-holding positions within the Church hierarchy.</note>, and the like. ### STEGANOGRAPHY

<meta>catchword: [mo]destum</meta>

...namely, twenty-four dukes for the day and twenty-four for the night, with a number of servants totaling ten thousand, one hundred and sixty. And note, that the operation performed by these is very different from the others, just as we shall speak at length concerning the master. For according to the twelve hours of the day and night, one must operate in this station; four princes always preside over two hours, both in the day and in the night: so that it is not permitted for you to call another, except in the order which concerns the hour. For example, if the operation is in the first or second hour of the day: call whichever you wish from the first four who preside over the day; if in the third or fourth, you should know that a duke from the second four must be called. And so it must be done consequently through all the orders and twenty-four hours, both in the day and in the night.

When, however, you wish to operate in <term>Steganography</term> <note>Trithemius’s term for his system of "secret writing," presented as a system of communicating through spirits.</note> through the spirits of <term>Dorothiel</term> himself: once the generalities customary to the art have been performed, write whatever letters you wish, and turning yourself toward that region of the world which we commonly call <term>Chorus</term> <note>The Latin name for the Northwest wind.</note> after the wind, call one or more of the four who preside over the hour in which you are working:

*Dorothiel casi seor madyion busar pamesy chearianothym baony Camerisy ulyneor peathan adial cadumyr renear thubra Cohagier maslon Lodiano sabeirusyn.* <note>In the context of this book, these "barbarous names" are actually a polyalphabetic cipher. The practitioner would write this "invocation" to hide a secret message within the letters.</note>

Once these things are said, you will see the appearing spirit whom you have called, prompt and cheerful to obey, to whom you may commit your secret safely and faithfully. Look at the preceding table.

->*Let us set down here certain cases: in what way the secret should be committed to letters.*<-

<margin>Roth.</margin> <note>Likely a reference to the cipher key or table "Roth" mentioned on the previous page.</note> You have a business matter, in any spiritual thing whatsoever, to a prince, which is both very difficult and secret, which you think should be entrusted to no mortal nor even to letters; do you wish the prince to know it? You feign a letter of supplication for a poor cleric, or any other kind, to him: you call the spirit, you entrust the secret to him: he performs everything faithfully.

->*You shall give whatever letters you wish, in which you should not fear anything.*<-

I inform Your Grace, Most Illustrious Prince, that <term>Conrad Hummel</term>, the holder of the <term>benefice</term> <note>A church office that provides an income to the holder.</note> at the altar of Saint John the Evangelist, died last night. Lest Your Grace grant the said altar out of haste to either one of the unskilled or the unworthy—of whom I believe a great number will be present (as is custom) waiting for the said altar—but rather reserve it for a more useful man, I pray: although I stand far unworthy that my prayers should seem to bring back any favor in the sight of so great and noble a prince, [I ask] that Your Grace might deign to grant the aforementioned altar to the bearer of these presents, having rejected the more unworthy; for I have known him to be worthy of the best benefices, as he is one adorned with the beauty of character, learned in every way in science and letters, modest...

### CHAPTER XI.

<margin>Roth.</margin>
### WHOSE SPIRIT AND CHIEF IS CALLED USIEL, 
*inhabiting that region of the world which, from the wind blowing thence, we call Subcircius: who has under him 40 dukes by day, and as many by night: who are over Treasure.*

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial letter V with intricate floral and foliage patterns.</image-desc>The eleventh station of the whole world is called **Subcircius** <note>The North-Northwest wind, located between the North and the Northwest.</note> from the wind, namely, which is accustomed to come from that direction. In this region dwells a great prince who is called **Usiel**, having under him 40 princes who preside over the daytime operations, and 40 who preside over the nighttime ones. Their duty is to report hidden and subterranean treasures, and all things which seem to pertain to the nature of treasures. Of these we shall name a few, and as many as are sufficient in this place for the operations of <term>Steganography</term> <note>Trithemius’s system of secret writing disguised as spirit conjuration.</note>: and we reserve the rest with others to be named in their own chapter at the end of this book. And note, that the dukes of this station, when they are called by the operator, are accustomed to come willingly and with the greatest cheerfulness. And although they have many servants: yet I have never seen that they brought any of them with them, at least visibly. If anyone, however, wished to use the ministry of the servants: he could, since they are under the princes, and are compelled to [obey] them in all things.

<meta>This page continues the instructions for the spirits under Usiel. ### BOOK ONE

...perform the rest of what the <term>art</term> <note>Referring to the "Art of Steganography," which Trithemius presents as a system of spirit communication, but which functions as a method for encryption.</note> itself demands, and entrust the secret to the spirit now appearing with the seal of a profound mystery, without hesitating: since all these leaders are good and faithful.

->*Let us suppose that you have such a secret of the mind*<-
->*that must be trusted to no one at all.*<-

Imagine you have found a great <term>treasure</term> in the <term>tombs of the dead</term> <note>Original: "Thecis mortuorum," referring to ancient burial chests or coffers.</note> or in some other place in the earth, or you know for certain it is hidden away: you are not able to carry it out alone, yet you do not trust anyone nearby. You have a faithful friend, but he is far away, to whom you wish to reveal the mystery; but you trust neither letters nor a messenger, lest through haste you fall into public view and danger, and lose your gain. You call therefore a spirit. The spirit comes, he carries the message, he calls the friend. He comes, helps, and participates. Write the letter as you wish, in which there is nothing at all to be feared.

I send you an oration <note>A formal speech or sermon used here as a "cover text" to hide a secret message.</note> which I delivered at the wedding of a certain friend of mine. *God, the Creator of all things and Redeemer of the human race, and Savior of all, lest the human race perish, also instituted the <term>sacrament of marriage</term>: for we read that he joined the woman Eve to the first man, and said:* **INCREASE AND MULTIPLY** <note>Original Latin: "Crescite et multiplicamini," the biblical command from Genesis.</note>: *instituting a law for the preservation of humanity. Afterward, God incarnate deigned to sanctify marriage with a miracle. Those who keep what has been established deserve well of God, but those who take up chaste <term>marriage</term> with a zeal for offspring contribute more to humanity. I would rightly praise you who take up honorable marriage, following the example of our Savior. For while he praised the necessary sacrament of marriage duly instituted with miracles, I shall do so with words: though I may be unmarried, I will nonetheless rightly praise marriage. Indeed, if marriage were taken away, the entire human race would fail and fall. For by the use of marriage, the human race continues to exist, though it be ruinous, weak, and corruptible: yet without marriage, it would not endure. But see to it that the fruitfulness of your marriage be adorned with holy character and conversation, causing no scandal to anyone. Above all, love God, continually devoting yourselves to works of mercy with justice; flee from impiety, cherish righteousness, love the good, always speak praises to God, assist the poor with alms, obey the commands of God. Do not desert the truth, honor the ministers of God in the churches, pay your tithes, give hospitality to strangers, free the innocent, feed the hungry, obey your elders,*

# STEGANOGRAPHY

*...love your neighbors honorably, inflicting no injury at all on anyone. Devoutly cherish the Christian religion; abhor the vain, harmful, and superstitious customs of the heathens. Keep the Gospel teachings and live according to the commandments of God. Flee the pleasures of the flesh, and show yourselves to be honorable and good; happily guarding the purity of the mind, do not defile it. Remember, I pray, what Christ our Lord cried out in the Holy Gospel: **Blessed are the pure in heart** <note>original: "Beati mundi corde," from Matthew 5:8.</note>: namely, those who guard the purity of the soul in a body of clay for the sake of God. Moreover, let purity of heart not be abandoned by constant and humble prayers, for prayer purifies the mind, cools those who are burning <note>Likely referring to the heat of passion or desire.</note>, and puts the temptations of the devil to flight. Hence the holy Apostle Paul teaches you to persist in diligent and devoted prayers, so that you may overcome harmful temptations. We join you in marriage, so that you may build up your lineage propagated by the decree of the law: not so that you should overflow with harmful pleasures. For the pleasures of the flesh kill the soul, and corrupt the noble dignity of the human essence of those to come. Therefore, mindful of these things, know that you are travelers, destined to return to the homeland to which you have been recalled. This present life is very short, and the joy of the world is ended most swiftly. Thus live in marriage that after death, when you are raised up, you may come to the kingdom of heaven. Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, of the order of the Holy Father Benedict, added this on the day before the Ides of March <note>March 14th.</note>, in the year of the birth of our Lord Jesus Christ, 1500.* —

*When [the recipient] receives these or any other letters to which they are sent; once the sign of the great **Usiel** is recognized, let him do secretly as he knows must be done according to the <term>art</term> <note>The "art" refers to the cryptographic system Trithemius is veiling as magic. The recipient would know which letters to extract from the oration to read the hidden message.</note>. Then let him turn toward the **North-Northwest** <note>original: "Subcircius," a classical term for the wind blowing from the north-northwest.</note> and say the **Incantation** <note>original: "Carmen." While presented here as a magical spell, these strings of words in Trithemius’s work are typically null ciphers or substitution keys.</note>: **Vsielafoyr paremon cruato madusyn sauepy mauayr realdo chameron ilco paneras thurmo pea elfoty fabelrusyn iltras charson frymasto chelmodyn.** Having spoken this incantation, the spirit will show himself visibly and reveal the messages entrusted to him: and if anything was entrusted to him by that person, he will report it back to the sender.*

The first ten of these are assigned to daytime operations with 500 servants, and the other ten likewise for the night with their 500 attendants. And you should know that the spirits presiding over daytime operations are much more benevolent than the nocturnal ones, and more prompt to obey. Thus, it is often necessary to compel those of the night with a certain violence, and to repeat the conjuration more frequently. When they are commanded, they bring their servants with them; if they are not commanded, they hide them. When you wish to operate through the ministry of these spirits, turn yourself toward that region which we mentioned; and, having performed the customary preliminaries, say this Conjuration:

> Cabariel onear chameron fruani, parnaton fofiel bryosi nagreal fabelrontyn adiel thortay nofruau pena afefiel chusy. <note>While this sounds like a magical spell, in Trithemius’s system, these are "barbarous names" that actually serve as a cipher for a hidden message. Once the song <note>original: "carmine," meaning a song, poem, or ritual incantation.</note> is completed, if it be during the day, the called spirit will be present immediately without delay. If, however, it be at night and the spirit you called is slow in coming, urge him on by repeating the Song—which they are accustomed to revere greatly—until he comes.

# STEGANOGRAPHY

Suppose you have a FAITHFUL friend, whom you love as yourself, but who is far away. You have learned that an ambush is being prepared for him on his return journey—either on the road or in some other place or manner—by someone more powerful than he. You wish to warn him, as he is unaware; however, a great danger would loom over you if your warning were made public. Therefore, the matter must not be committed to an ordinary letter, lest it be read by someone else, nor should it be entrusted to a messenger, lest it happen to be revealed. So that you may remain secure and your friend may be warned: you call upon the spirit and entrust the secret to him.

You then send a familiar letter to your friend, or a devout prayer, which is designed to appear perfectly innocent.

*I had lent you my book on the deeds of the Lombards and the volume by Bede on chronology <note>original: "de temporibus," likely referring to Bede the Venerable's "De temporum ratione."</note>, which I pray you would return. For it becomes a wise man, and especially a Christian, to keep his word wherever promised. Indeed, to forget favors is considered a most grave crime by both divine and our own human institutions. Do not incur such a sinful guilt. I sent you my volumes so that you might eventually restore them. Truly, I gave the Greek books to you freely; in view of them, you should rightly show yourself both grateful to us and faithful. Farewell, from Sponheim <note>Trithemius was the Abbot of Sponheim Abbey.</note>, on the Ides of March <note>March 15th.</note>, in the Year of our Lord 1500, on the day of Jupiter <note>original: "♃" (the symbol for Jupiter), indicating Thursday.</note>.*

When your friend receives this letter (or any other sent by you), and recognizes the sign of Cabariel, let him turn toward that quarter of the world from which the North-Northwest wind <note>original: "Circius."</note> blows; and having performed the usual preliminary rites, let him read this prayer in silence.

->The Song of Conjuration<-

Cabariel asiar parèmon chiltan amedyn fayr pemadon chulty mouayr sauepor peatha mal frimaston dayr pean cothumo fabelrusyn elfoty chelmodyn. <note>While these words appear to be a magical incantation, in Trithemius’s system, they often function as a null-cipher or a prompt for the recipient to extract a hidden message using a specific key or method.</note> Once this song has been spoken toward the North-Northwest, the angel will immediately appear in visible form, reporting to him the secret entrusted to it; and it will report back to you if he should entrust anything in return.

# BOOK ONE.

The thirteenth mansion <note>original: "mansio," an astrological or magical station</note> is in the North, whose spirit and supreme commander is Rayfiel. He has under his dominion 50 dukes who preside over daytime operations, along with many servants, and an equal number who preside over nighttime operations with their own attendants. Their duty in these matters and traditions, which pertain to death, is to warn the practitioner's friends and to announce secret things. The dukes who preside over daytime operations willingly obey the caller and arrive cheerful, pleasant, and most ready to serve. However, those who preside over the night are somewhat stubborn, as they greatly hate and detest the light. They do not easily obey, especially novices who are less proven in the art, whom they frequently mock to their faces, unless they are so constrained by the practitioner's conjurations that they lack the power to refuse. For they greatly fear conjuration, and it is said they are more bound by conjurations than the daytime spirits; nevertheless, they obey unwillingly. But now, from the total number of all these, let us list the names of a few and the number of their servants—as many as are sufficient for our customary operation.

# OF STEGANOGRAPHY.

### *The Conjuration Verse.* <note>original: "Carmen Conjurationis"</note>

Rayfiel afuano chameron fofiel oncar Vemabi parnothon fruano Caspiel fufre bedatym buliscor pean Curmaby Layr Vaymeor pelarym adorcus odiel Vernabi peatha darsum laspheno deuior Camedonton phorsy lasbenay to charmon druson olnays, Venouym lulefon, peorfo fabelrontos thurno. Calephoy Vem, nabelron bural thorasyn charnoty Capelron. <note>While these appear to be "barbarous names" or magical incantations, in Trithemius’s system, such verses often serve as a cryptographic key or a method of masking the actual communication process.</note>

When the verse has been completed according to the rite and in silence, the summoned spirit will appear. Entrust your secret to him securely; he will carry it and bring back what must be sent. If, however, you see those presiding over night operations delay—as they often tend to do—compel them by manfully repeating the verse, fearing nothing; for they will be forced to revere you as their master.

->*I have a secret, which I trust to no one to be carried,*<-
->*in this manner.*<-

I present a case. I have a certain noble and learned friend, whom I know is to be secretly killed by certain hired assassins. I do not dare to warn him openly, because it is dangerous for me: in a private and more secret matter, I fear lest the letters fall into the hands of others: I do not presume to trust a man to speak by mouth: because he who is with me today, will perhaps stand against me tomorrow. Therefore, I flee to my accustomed art <note>The "art" refers to Steganography or secret writing.</note>, to which whatever I entrust, I do not fear it becoming public, even if it should happen to be intercepted by an enemy.

->*I send familiar letters to a friend, in which I*<-
->*fear no danger.*<-

*Antiquity defines Justice and Philosophy as the greatest ornament of nobility, and impiety and Ignorance as a disgrace. For what is more beautiful than to adorn a noble lineage, which serves in war, with letters and virtues? We find that this man and many other most noble men serving in war have done this both by night and by day: so that they might conquer barbarians with arms, and instruct their own nation in righteousness and letters. By their example, most learned Bumavv <note>Likely a contemporary contact or a placeholder name for the recipient of the secret message.</note>, you do not cease to study the letters of humanity and the good arts, decorated as you are with arms and letters: and the most wise ancient princes formerly were accustomed not to lock up wealth in their treasuries, but rather to bestow it for literary use: judging the study of wisdom to be the most honorable support for those striving toward happiness, by which the bliss of eternal retribution is acquired. Truly, knowledge of the scriptures makes princes venerable to all and most to be feared; Ignorance of the scriptures is called the burial of a living man <note>original: "viui hominis sepultura"</note>, and is accustomed to bring disgrace and horrendous calamity to princes and nobles—*

BOOK ONE.

... [continuing from the previous page]. A man without learning is a *two-legged ass: to be buried alive, or rather, already buried.* <note>original: "Asinus bipes: viuusq3 sepeliendus, immo sepultus." This was a favorite proverb of Trithemius, emphasizing that without education, a person is no better than a beast.</note> It is especially necessary for nobles and those governing the state to be wise and notable in their knowledge of letters, for ignorance of them leads to tyranny. Furthermore, a love of letters commends the illustrious deeds of princes and nobles to eternity. For we see that the ancient philosophers have maintained the memory of their rule, and achieved an immortality of name among all men that is more everlasting through their writings than through their arms. Indeed, letters have rendered the glorious, excellent, and wise ancient tyrants and princes more famous than weapons ever did, granting immortality to their memory and their titles of praise. Add to this that for Christians, the study of letters provides the highest and most honorable assistance in soaring toward Heaven. For through the scriptures, we begin to contemplate the flashing and honorable majesty of divine enlightenments, which would otherwise be inaccessible to us. Therefore, Henry, most learned soldier, may you favor letters, so that you may always live adorned with righteousness. Farewell, from Sponheim <note>The Benedictine abbey of Sponheim, where Trithemius served as Abbot and built one of the greatest libraries of the age.</note>, on the Ides of March. In the year 1500. <meta>flourish mark</meta>

And I would have you know that some spirits presiding over nocturnal operations do not come willingly to the work at night unless they are compelled by the greatest sacraments and conjurations; yet, once they have come and have been sent to a friend, they are accustomed to appear quickly and obey the one calling, unless perhaps the practitioner himself, deceived by some occasion, should neglect something prescribed in the art.

# STEGANOGRAPHY

**Q**uarta decima <note>Fourteenth</note> station is in the North, distant from the beginning of the North by 21 degrees and a bit more. Its supreme Emperor is **Symiel**, who has under him 10 dukes who preside over daytime operations with many servants. Many dukes also preside over the nighttime operations, whose exact number I have not been able to find, and who also have many servants under them. Their duty is to announce the most hidden secrets between friends, which are to be revealed to no man for eternity. However, so that you may work through them if it should be necessary: I will explain to you ten from the daytime and as many from the nighttime, and they will suffice for the present.

| Black | Red | B. | R. | B. | R. | B. | ☿ |

<note>The table labels "Schw." (Schwarz/Black) and "Roth" (Red) refer to the color-coded system Trithemius used for his ciphers. The planetary symbol for Mercury (☿) indicates the spirit's association with communication and speed.</note>

You now have ten princes named for daytime actions with many subordinates, and 10 for nighttime operations also with many substitutes, through whom you will be able to work securely and without danger—provided you are well-instructed in <term>Steganography</term> <note>Literally "secret writing." Trithemius uses the guise of "spirit summoning" to mask his cryptographic methods.</note>. For whoever presumes to approach the operation of this art and its precepts without being well-instructed will either profit nothing or will scarcely escape danger. For the princes do not obey everyone. When you wish to work with this science yourself, once the necessary preliminaries have been properly performed, turn yourself toward the North, and in the manner I have handed down to you, conjure the duke so that he comes through this Song.

->Song to the Spirits<-

# BOOK ONE.

...obey, do not stop for that reason, but press them with the repetition of the song: and they will obey when compelled.

->*For example, let us pose any secret whatever that is to be committed to absolutely no one.*<-

YOU HAVE some most secret business with a friend, which concerns you and him: the disclosure of which would bring perpetual loss or confusion to both of you. Certainly, it is not safely trusted to letters lest they be read by someone, nor is it committed to a messenger, who might divulge it to many. Use our art; thus you will be safe, and your friend will know the secret.

->*Let us form for this purpose whatever short letters please us, which are in no way suspicious.*<-
->*Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim <note>Latin: Abbas Spanheimensis</note> of the Order of Saint Benedict, to the excellent youth Jacob Trithemius, his dearest brother, greetings.*<-

> *Devoting yourself to LETTERS, preserve humility of Heart. Good manners decorate young men, and make those of noble birth more illustrious. The knowledge of letters adorns a youth who loves holiness; without manners, justice will lack honor. A youth <note>original: Adolescens</note> immersed in vanities and vices, a despiser of wisdom, does not love true wisdom, which you should remember is to be sought through virtues. The education of youth to be instructed is decorated by manners, since Heavenly honor is conferred upon the holy arts, and unfruitful knowledge will be tortured with eternal torments. Knowledge profits by virtues and letters; let both be lacking if stained by vices. Spurn pleasures, so that by experience you may understand the truth of wisdom. Farewell.*
<note>To the casual reader, this is a standard moralizing letter. However, in Trithemius’s system, the secret message is often hidden using the first letter of every word, or every other word. The "magic" is actually a "null cipher"—a way of hiding a message in plain sight.</note>

The recipient of these letters, skilled in the art of <term>Steganography</term> <note>The art of hidden writing, from the Greek 'steganos' (hidden) and 'graphia' (writing).</note>, as soon as he sees the character of the prince Symiel, perceives by understanding what must be done: and turning himself, after the necessary preliminaries, toward the North, he says the conjuration, <margin>Conjuration.</margin>

Symiel marlos chameron pyrcohi pean fruary fabelronti gaelto siargoti melaflor hialbra penor olesy Aiulbrani ordu Calmeron omer vemabon. <note>While these words sound like a magical incantation, in the context of this book, they often serve as a secondary key or a ritualistic "mask" to explain why the recipient is behaving strangely while decoding the message.</note> When the song is finished, the spirit sent, appearing, will faithfully fulfill the duty committed to him.

### CHAPTER XV.

## WHOSE SUPREME EMPEROR IS ARMADIEL,
*who dwells in that part of the world from which the North Wind <note>original: Boreas</note> is accustomed to blow, having under him many princes.*

# OF STEGANOGRAPHY

<image-desc>A large square decorative initial 'M' featuring ornate floral and leaf patterns in a woodcut style.</image-desc>

**THE FIFTEENTH MANSION** <note>In this context, a "mansion" refers to a spiritual or astrological dwelling place or office.</note> toward the Northern region is inhabited by **Armadiel**, the great emperor, who holds under his command many dukes and princes with their servants. Of these, one hundred have been refined for works both by day and by night indifferently: so that all are equally in charge of our nocturnal and diurnal actions in **Steganography** <note>The art of "hidden writing." While presented here as magic, it is actually a method of cryptography.</note> according to the will of the operator. Their duty is to report the most hidden secrets of the operator to princes and great men with the greatest fidelity and mystery. From these, we shall name some of the more powerful ones, who are sufficient for our purpose.

| **R.** <note>Likely "Regens" or Ruler/Name.</note> | **S.** <note>Likely "Subservientes" or number of subordinates.</note> | **R.** | **S.** | **R.** | **S.** | **R.** | **S.** | **R.** | **S.** |

| Caluarnya | 20 | Asmael | Mafayr | 40 | 40 | 70 | 70 | 20 | |
| Alferiel | 10 | Iaziel | Oeniel | 50 | 50 | 60 | 60 | 10 | |

You now have fifteen of the spirits of Armadiel in number, with their 1,260 subordinates, who—according to the division of the twenty-four hours into six parts—are accustomed to come to the operator with their dukes when called, if he so wishes, preserving their own order in their coming. Therefore, when you wish to operate anything in Steganography through them, it is necessary that you most diligently observe the division of time according to this art, without which you will profit nothing at all. When these things are known as they ought to be, you shall turn yourself toward the **North**, doing what the art requires.

->**Conjuration.**<-

<meta>This page transitions from the catalog of spirits to a practical demonstration of Trithemius’s cryptographic method. Having established the "spiritual" framework in the preceding chapters, Trithemius now illustrates how to actually encode a message. He frames the exercise as a formal letter of dedication to his patron, Prince Philip, demonstrating that a secret message can be hidden in plain sight within a mundane or courtly text.</meta>

->*Let us create for ourselves, by way of example, a secret of the kind that must be entrusted to no one.*<-

I have a certain secret for my prince which must be communicated to no one at all. I do not dare to commit it to writing, lest the scattered pages be read through negligence or forgetfulness; much less do I wish to announce it through a messenger, lest it happens to be revealed to others through him in some way. Therefore, it pleases me to try the mystery of this art, through which my secret will remain hidden for all time.

->*Let us write a letter, of whatever sort we please, which fears the gaze of no one.*<-

## To the Most Serene Prince, Lord Philip, Count Palatine of the Rhine, Duke of Bavaria, Arch-Steward of the Holy Roman Empire, Prince Elector, Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim <note>Trithemius (1462–1516) was a famous Benedictine abbot, polymath, and pioneer of cryptography.</note>, with prayers and service.

MOST ILLUSTRIOUS Prince, with humble submission, my service. The kindness of your renowned dukedom commanded me to commit certain most hidden matters, which had been explained in words, to writing. I would rejoice, not without devoted exultation, if powers equal to my will were added, to provide a most honorable and suitable place for the favors shown, and to offer the service owed by grace. The novelty of this work <note>Trithemius is referring to his invention of *Steganographia*, or "hidden writing."</note>—which must be undertaken without a preceding path—detains the weakness of my heart, for I know myself to be far unequal to it; for it will involve immense and great labor, and it strikes a great deal of sluggishness into my efforts. Nevertheless, I shall test my strength, intending to obey Your Grace, undoubtedly approaching a most kind judge if the ability to complete it properly is not present alongside my good will: where, with most abundant humanity, the glorious and most munificent prince, lover and defender of letters <note>In the Renaissance context, "letters" refers to literature, scholarship, and the humanities.</note>, gives his judgment; to whose most kind protection the University of Heidelberg <note>original: "Vniuersitas Heydelbergensis"</note>, adorned with many most learned and most erudite men, most fresh in genius, transcends all the schools <note>original: "Gymnasia," referring to centers of higher learning.</note> throughout Germany <note>original: "Alemaniam"</note>. Therefore, most illustrious and most kind Prince, if I have or am anything, I know I am entirely indebted to your kindness: which I shall always most promptly take care to offer with humble submission. And although there may be others wiser, who might adorn this work better or more usefully or more elegantly, in no small—

<meta>catchword: —ly render.</meta>

<summary>Trithemius begins a practical demonstration of his secret writing by composing a formal, public-facing letter of dedication to Prince Philip of Bavaria. He explains that this method allows a sender to transmit a secret that is safe from the prying eyes of messengers or negligent handlers.</summary>
<keywords>cryptography, Johannes Trithemius, Prince Philip of Bavaria, Heidelberg University, Steganography, secret writing, Renaissance scholarship</keywords>

N. <note>The letter concludes Trithemius's demonstration of how a secret message can be hidden within a formal greeting.</note>

Anyone receiving these letters, or any others, who is skilled in this art, [looks] for the Character of Armadiel; once this is known, let him turn himself where the art commands him to turn, and say the song. <note>In Trithemius's system, "turning" and "saying the song" are metaphors for applying a specific decryption key or reading pattern to the text.</note>

->Conjuration.<-

**A**RMADIEL ascan meson Casayr pelodyn, Cauoti chameron therloruy marbeuon pheor Casoyn myruosy lyburmy deon fabelronton. Chubis archamarson. <note>original: "Armadiel ascan meson..." While this looks like a magical incantation, in the context of Steganographia, these "spirit names" and "conjurations" function as the ciphertexts or mnemonic keys themselves.</note>

Once this conjuration has been completed in the proper manner, the sent spirit will appear openly and will faithfully reveal the secrets entrusted to it by the sender into the ear, adding or subtracting nothing. And if anything should then be entrusted to it by him to report back to the practitioner from whom it was sent, let that which is commanded by the art be done; and it will obey.

### CHAPTER XVI.

### WHOSE PRINCE BARUCHAS DWELLS IN THAT region from which the wind called Vulturnus <note>The southeast wind, specifically the one blowing from the direction of the Vultur mountain.</note> is accustomed to blow, having under him many princes and servants, who are messengers over the hidden commissions of lords.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial M featuring leafy flourishes and a classical architectural border.</image-desc>

**T**HE seventeenth mansion, situated toward the southeast wind <note>original: "Vulturnum ventum"</note>, is presided over by the great Emperor Baruchas, who has under his command many princes, dukes, and other spirits. The duty of all of them is to announce the hidden and most secret commissions of princes, nobles, and lords to their subjects or friends. From these I will name 15 for you, who will suffice for any practitioner of Steganography; and they do not have divisions or orders between them for day and night like the others, but all have a commission for everything in general, so that whoever is called at any hour by the practitioner is compelled to come.

You now have 15 named leaders from the princes of Baruchas, the great prince or Emperor, for the operation of <term>Steganography</term><note>Trithemius's coined term for "hidden writing," which he disguises as a system of angel magic.</note>, along with his 7,040 servants and subordinates. Their service is more than sufficient for you for every operation of this art. Therefore, when you wish to call upon any of these spirits, observe the division of time according to the six hours of the day and night; and with your face turned toward the Southeast <note>original: "Vulturnum"</note>, call the one whom the hour concerns. Be most diligent in observing the letters of the servants, without which they never proceed; and take care that you do not err in their number, order, or dominion.

### *The Conjuration.*

Baruchas maluear chemorsyn charnotiel bason ianocri medusyn aprilty casmyrō sayr pean cauoty medason peroel chamyrsyn cherdiel auenos nosear penaō sayr chauelonti genayr pamelron frilcha madyrion onetiel fabelronthos. <note>While this appears to be a magical incantation, it is actually a string of nonsense words designed to serve as a mnemonic or a carrier for a hidden message in Trithemius’s cryptographic system.</note> Once this conjuration has been completed in silence as is proper, one principal spirit will soon appear, whom you have called with the servants assigned to him according to the sequence of time. For example: It has been commissioned to me to survey the orders and monasteries, and to emend and correct things done wrongly. Hence, I desire to send such a secret to a friend among the monks who is far away, which, if published, would bring upon him imprisonment and the hatred of his superior <note>original: "pastoris," literally "shepherd"</note>, and would bring detriment to me, the order, and the common good. Since it must neither be entrusted to a letter nor committed to any man, so that we both may be secure, I call upon the spirit—the minister of this art—and commend the secret to him.

->*I send whatever letters I wish, which I will not refuse to let all men read.*<-

->**Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim <note>original: "Spanheimensis"</note> of the Order of Saint Benedict, to his religious brother Nicholas of Dureckheim, a monk of the Hirsau Monastery <note>original: "Hirsaugiensis"</note> of the same order: sincere charity in the Lord.**<-

<page-num>48</page-num>
<header>STEGANOGRAPHY</header>

Since I know, dearest brother, that you are a lover of the good arts, it delights me to exhort you vehemently that you may wish to persevere in constantly searching out and turning over the secrets of books. These show us the way of happiness and the direct path to return to the fatherland <note>A common Renaissance metaphor for Heaven or the original state of grace.</note> which we lost through sin. To this path, the burning love of our most mild and merciful Savior—hidden from us in the Holy Scriptures—will lead us back, provided we adorn our minds with virtues and exercise ourselves in holy study, which shall without doubt be granted to us. For what could be more healthy or sweet to those wandering in miserable exile than to apply themselves diligently to letters? Through the study of these, our eyes are raised to seek the truth, and our minds are lifted to the desire for future blessedness. Without these studies, the soul is compared to sacrificial victims, immersed in temporal and worldly affairs, never destined to arrive at the knowledge of God. Therefore, you must despise the pleasures and turmoils of the world and devote yourself to letters day and night; for both religion is tempered to be illustrated by virtues, and virtues by the commerce of letters. Thus, one can neither love letters without the ornament of noble virtues, nor can good virtues prevail without the knowledge of the scriptures. Therefore, I exhort you to always give your labor to the study of letters. Farewell from Sponheim, the 16th Calends of April <note>March 17th</note>, in the year of our Lord 1500.

After the person to whom they are sent has received the letters, and the sign of Baruchas has been recognized, and once the necessary preliminaries are completed, let him say this Conjuration in silence while facing the quarter of the Southeast wind <note>original: "Vulturni"</note>, which is nearest to the East.

->*Conjuration of the spirit.*<-

> Baruchas Mularchas chameron notiel pedarsy phroys lamasay myar chalemon phorsy fabelrontho theras capean Vear almonym lierno medusan therfiel peatha thumar nerosyn cral nothiel peson segalry madon scoha bulayr. <note>As with previous examples, this "conjuration" is a series of meaningless words that act as a mask for a hidden message or a key for the recipient to begin the decryption process.</note>

When the conjuration has been spoken according to the precept of the art, the spirit will appear and will bring forth the secret committed to him into the ear without deception.

### CAP. XVII.

**WHOSE PRINCE AND SUPREME SPIRIT** is called *Carnesiel*, and he dwells in the East, having under him many dukes and princes with their servants, whose duty it is to announce all things that are sent toward the East.

<page-num>40</page-num>
# BOOK ONE.

Since human affairs are so numerous and more may arise—matters that must be reported to friends at a distance, for which no prince or spirit among those previously mentioned has been specifically assigned to those stations <note>original: "mansionibus"; refers to the specific roles or "dwelling places" in the hierarchy assigned to spirits in previous chapters</note>—lest any difficulty occur for those working in this art of **Steganography** <term>Steganography: From the Greek "steganos" (hidden) and "graphein" (to write), refers here to Trithemius’s system of secret communication using spirit names as cryptographic keys.</term> in the perfection of their work: after we have made mention of certain specific spirits who preside over specific secrets, we also wish to set forth certain general princes of spirits for reporting general matters. Thus, if something should happen to be reported by someone for which no specific commission is found among the spirits previously mentioned, it may be referred to these, who in their own way have commission over all things; provided, however, that the general does not detract from the specific, nor does the specific (not being clearly enough expressed) refuse the office of the general.

There are, therefore, four great princes who, along with their dukes and subordinates, preside over all secrets to be reported that were not expressed above. The first of these, **Carnesiel**, presides over all things we wish to report toward the East. **Caspiel** toward the South. **Amenadiel** toward the West, and **Demoriel** over those things sent generally toward the North. When, therefore, you wish to report some secret through a spirit to a friend far absent toward the East: call one or more as you wish from the princes of **Carnesiel**, turning yourself during the whole operation toward the eastern region, as you know according to the art. I will describe to you the names of the princes with the number of servants <note>In the table, "R." abbreviates "Regentes" (Rulers/Princes) and "S." abbreviates "Servi" (Servants).</note>, as many as suffice for the present business: and take care, lest you err in anything.

| R. | S. | R. | S. | | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| *Myresyn* | *Benoham* | *Armany* | 6000000000000000 | | | |
| | | | **Ri.** | **S.** | **R.** | |
| *Ornich* | *Arisiel* | *Capriel* | 10 | 30 | 100 | 300 |
| *Zabriel* | *Cumeriel* | *Bedarys* | 10 | 30 | 100 | 300 |
| *Bucafas* | *Vadriel* | *Laphor* | 10 | 30 | 100 | 300 |

You have here 12 of the princes of Carnesiel with a very great multitude of servants, where (note) that great number 6,000,000,000,000,000 belongs to other dukes and princes who generally preside over all daytime matters <note>original: "diurnis"; spirits associated with the hours of the day</note>: and from them a certain number is called, as the operator wishes, with the

# STEGANOGRAPHY

#### Song.

Camesiel aphroys chemeryn mearapofyn. Layr peã noema ouear ma fere crafty caleuo thorteam chamerõ ianoar pe lyn Layr, baduon iesy melios ionatiel delassar rodjuial meron sau ean fabeli on clumarly preos throen benarys fauean demofynon laernoty chamedonton. <note>This "song" or incantation is actually a cipher. In Trithemius's system, the specific letters and sequences of these seemingly nonsensical words hide the actual message to be transmitted.</note> Once these rites are properly completed, the general messenger of secrets whom you called will appear, ready, faithful, and entirely secret.

### If I should wish to secretly warn a friend about an evil man.

Suppose there is a certain man who, under the appearance of honesty, is an evil babbler <term>blatterator: a malicious talker or someone who speaks thoughtlessly and harmfully</term>. Knowing that I have influence with a friend, he asks me for a letter of recommendation to him. I am unable to refuse him, yet I do not wish my friend to be fraudulently deceived by him. I write the letter, praising the scoundrel <term>circumcellionem: originally a member of a 4th-century rebel group, here used as a general term for a deceptive vagabond or dishonest person</term> in a wonderful manner; he reads it, exults, and promises himself an open path for his treachery. I give him the letter and he departs happy; I then call the spirit and send it to my friend so that it may warn him and reveal my secret intent.

### I send a letter of recommendation pleasing to the one being recommended.

->*Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, sends greetings to Johannes Vigilius, Lord Wacker, most distinguished Doctor of both Laws.*<- <note>A "Doctor of both Laws" refers to someone who has mastered both Civil (Roman) law and Canon (Church) law.</note>

*I send the bearer of these presents to you, most beloved Vigilius, a man truly adorned with all virtues, most learned in all writings, and so crowned with beauty of character that I remember having scarcely seen his equal with these eyes—an excellent mathematician, a most sharp philosopher, to be compared with the most lettered men, transcending all the most practiced Germans in wisdom and study. Whence he has deservedly seemed worthy to us of being received with the praises of all lettered and good men: for he is noble and most literate, one who can be useful to all in handing down various doctrines of wonderful things. Farewell, from Sponheim, March 18th <note>original: "15. Cal. April."; the 15th day before the Kalends of April</note>, in the year 1500.* ✠

# BOOK ONE.

When a friend who is skilled in the art of **Steganography** <term>Steganographia: literally "hidden writing"; in this book, it is presented as a system of angel magic used to transmit secret information</term> receives these or any similar letters: once the sign of **Carnefiel** <note>One of the "Princes" or presiding spirits in Trithemius's system, associated with the East.</note> is recognized, let him turn toward the **East**, and having performed the necessary preliminaries <note>original: "praemissis praemittendis"; a ritual formula meaning the required purifications or opening prayers have been completed</note>, let him speak this conjuration:

->Conjuration.<-

> Carnefiel aproysi chamero to pemalroyn phroys Cadur mear mol benadron Vioniel faviron army pean arnotiel fabelron-thusyn throe chabelron sauenear medaloys vear olmenadab cral-ty fayr. <note>Like the "song" on the previous page, this string of words is a cipher. To the uninitiated, it looks like a magical incantation to summon a demon or spirit, but to the "friend skilled in the art," it contains the decryption key or the message itself.</note>

Once the song has been spoken, the dispatched spirit will now appear visibly and will faithfully reveal the secret entrusted to it. <note>In the context of Trithemius's "warm" explanation to his readers, the "spirit appearing" is often interpreted by modern scholars as a metaphor for the hidden message becoming clear to the person who knows the cipher's key.</note>

### CHAPTER XVIII.

### WHOSE SUPREME PRINCE IS CALLED **Caspiel**, whose dwelling is in the **South**, who has under him many princes, leaders, and innumerable servants.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut drop cap letter 'A' entwined with vines and foliage.</image-desc>

IN the South dwells the great prince **Caspiel**, who has under his dominion 200 principal princes or leaders, and 400 counts or sub-leaders, along with a vast multitude of servants. Their duty is to report generally all and every matter toward the South <note>original: "Meridiem"</note> not previously mentioned. Since it is necessary in this art to know certain leaders by name, and because they are so numerous, we shall take care to name only a few out of the many, which will suffice for all our purposes.

| N. | G. | N. | G. | N. | G. | N. | N. |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |

| Chariel | Camory | Ambrî | 200 | 40 | 2000 | 400 | 20 |
| Maras | Larmol | Camer | 200 | 49 | 2000 | 400 | 20 |
| Femol | Aridiel | Oliel | 200 | 40 | 2000 | 400 | 20 |

<note>The table lists the names (N) and ranks or groupings (G) of the spirits. In Trithemius's system, these names often correspond to specific alphabetic keys or shift patterns for the cipher.</note>

You now have 12 of the principal leaders of **Caspiel**, along with counts, sub-leaders, and 100,640 servants, through whom every operation for reporting general information toward the South is performed. When you wish to report something secret to a friend residing in the South, perform those things required by the art, and speak the conjuration.

When the conjuration is completed in the proper manner, you will see the one you called present and ready to obey your commands in all things. All the leaders of this **Caspiel** are very benevolent and willing, but their sub-leaders are somewhat more difficult; nevertheless, they yield to conjurations if the operator is steadfast and fearless, speaking...

->*Let us suppose a certain secret, which must be trusted to no one.*<-

## *It does not matter what kind of letters they are, provided they are entrusted to a spirit.*

> Behold, mortals, the brevity and misery of the present life, and perform a saving penance while you have the time. Woe to you, most foolish lovers of worldly glory, who rashly despise Almighty God and eternal joy. Why do you not love those heavenly feasts which are promised to those who humbly seek God? For you cast away humility and seek the briefest joys of the world; you do not love the excellence of eternal blessedness. You live with contentious zeal, you stain your honesty with wicked actions, and you know not the praises of the High God. Day and night you insist upon worldly actions and temporal gains; always intent on hunting for novelties, you sharpen your memory but neglect to hear the readings; you do not care for the time of grace granted to you, nor the expectation of eternal happiness. Behold, the Judge, terrible to all criminals, approaches. Behold, the Almighty Majesty of the strict account hastens. Behold, the final judgment is established. Behold, eternal lamentations are set before us, and everlasting goods shall be withdrawn from us. The year 1515, the 15th day before the Calends of April. <note>original: "15.Cal.Apr." This corresponds to March 18, 1515.</note> 6

In Trithemius's system, the hidden message is usually extracted by taking specific letters—such as the first or second letter of each word—to form a new, secret sentence.</note>

->**OF WHOM THE SUPREME PRINCE IS CALLED A-**<-

**A**menadiel the Emperor, supreme spirit of the West <note>In Trithemius's system, the four cardinal directions are governed by major spirit-princes; Amenadiel is the ruler of the Western quarter.</note>, has under his dominion 300 more powerful princes or dukes, no fewer than five hundred sub-leaders or counts, and servants, under-servants, and attendants from among the countless, of whom I have found no fewer than 30,000 names—besides others beyond number whose names I have not yet discovered. Through these 12 dukes and princes, you will be able to accomplish everything that must be done by the command of Amenadiel in Steganography <term>Steganography: Trithemius uses the language of "spirit conjuration" as a metaphor for his cryptographic systems; the "spirits" are essentially keys or methods for encoding messages.</term> toward the West. But observe diligently, so that you know when you call one of the dukes, and know how many counts, servants, and attendants he has under him according to the 24 hours of the day and night, which these 1,550 <note>This total likely refers to the sum of the primary hierarchical units mentioned in the heading (300 dukes + 500 counts + 750 others).</note> keep for themselves in their assigned order by equal division. If you err in these matters, you will achieve nothing, and perhaps you might fall into some danger <note>The "danger" Trithemius mentions is a recurring theme in grimoires, though here it likely refers to the total failure of the message to be correctly decrypted if the "astrological" (chronological) timing is missed.</note>. For it is necessary for one wishing to work in Steganography not only to be skilled in our art, but also to be most diligent in all things, because a great danger usually arises from even a small negligence in operation. Therefore, when you wish to work through these spirits, turn yourself toward the West; and having performed the necessary preliminaries, say this conjuration:

### The Conjuration.

AMenadiel aprolsy chameronta nostoy throen melio salayr chemaros noe pean lasy freueon tonatiel peltroyn rathtroy Caser malusan pedon Cranochyran daboy seor marchosyn lauo pedar venoti gestoy phernotiel Cabion. <note>These words constitute the "conjuration." ->*I have a secret that is very hidden and necessary for a friend.*<-

I have a certain secret for a friend, which I trust to be delivered neither by man nor by letters: for if it were to become public, it would create great danger and loss for me. I cannot endure delay: therefore, I call the spirit and I write letters that are in no way suspicious, and which he himself <note>The "spirit," or the unintended recipient.</note> would not be thought to understand, being ignorant of the Latin language: I send the spirit, and he reveals the secret.

### Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, to the Noble and Valiant Knight, Albert Goeler von Ravensburg, Governor of Kreuznach, Greetings. <note>Albert Goeler von Ravensburg (1444–1503) was a contemporary of Trithemius and a member of the lower nobility in the Palatinate.</note>

We believe that our Savior, the Lord Jesus Christ, is coming to judgment, who will render to each according to his works. This we believe, and yet, wretched as we are, we neglect ourselves. Therefore, let us study to <unclear>precede</unclear> <note>original: "præuenire"</note> His face with constant tears, amending our negligences <unclear>which</unclear> <note>original: "quas"</unclear> we have committed, lest that terrible coming of our Redeemer seize us while we are unprepared; for we must all necessarily meet Him to receive the reward of our evils. Why do we now waste the opportune time of salvation granted to us—which the Almighty has conceded to us as something <unclear>so</unclear> <note>original: "tamq[ue]"</note> useful and acceptable? Why do we not emulate those men <unclear>distinguished</unclear> <note>original: "insignes"</note> in character who left us examples of justice, of honest conversation, of humility, of <unclear>chastity</unclear> <note>original: "castitatis"</note>, and of all virtues? Let us act by emulating their names through penance: for the end of our living is at hand. Horrible death will come, which knows not how to <unclear>spare</unclear> <note>original: "parcere"</note>: but it will devour the young men and the maidens, the old with the young. Let us live in mourning, washing away our excesses with continual tears; lest an unexpected death submit us, once seized, to a horrible judgment. Let us amend our past life, praying to God, that after...

### CHAPTER XX.

### WHOSE PRINCE, DEMORIEL, DWELLS IN THE NORTH, having under his dominion 400 major leaders or princes, 600 sub-leaders or counts, with many subordinates and servants, whose number is uncertain.

->*The Song of Conjuration*<-

**Demoriel onear dabursoy Cohyne chamerson ymeor pean olayr chelrusys noeles schemlatyn venodru patron myselro chadarbon veuaon maferos ratigiel personay lodiol camedon nafiel fabelmerusin sosiel chamarchoy syn.** <note>To a casual reader, this looks like a demonic incantation. ->*Suppose such a secret, or any other, is to be*<-
->*communicated.*<-

I have a very close friend, currently absent and far away, with whom I have most secret business which should not be entrusted to anyone at all, nor even safely to letters. So that the secret may remain hidden, I choose to call some Spirit familiar to me from the art. I entrust the secret: he comes, he goes, he acts faithfully, and delivers what I committed to the friend; we are both safe. He also brings back a secret response to me from the friend, which he will tell to no other mortal. However, so that the friend himself understands the Spirit sent to him, it is necessary to forge some harmless letters, fearing nothing, in which he may recognize the sign of the sent Spirit. For the Spirit does not appear unless compelled.

->*Let us set forth the letters, of whatever kind pleases, which*<-
->*fear the sight of no one.*<-

### Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, to Jacob Wimpfeling of Sélestat, Theologian, Poet, and most eloquent Orator, Greetings.

I would very much wish, Jacob Wimpfeling <note>Jacob Wimpfeling (1450–1528) was a famous German humanist and theologian. Trithemius uses real contemporaries as the "recipients" of his letters to make the encrypted messages look like legitimate, scholarly correspondence.</note>, that you would at last satisfy your promise and my expectation of you. You know, however, what you promised, and you are not ignorant of what I expect. I do not want you to live as a monk, but to live with monks. Hasten, let us live together. Behold, the hour of our passing presses upon us, greatly to be feared. Why then do we delay? Why do we [waste] the brief and troublesome [time] of our dwelling...

...our state <note>original: "statum," the conclusion of the phrase from the previous page</note>, do we, unhappy ones, neglect it? Why do we refuse to perform penance for our crimes and excesses in this time of salvation? Let us prepare ourselves for death, because it hastens quite terribly; it calls all the wicked, it spares no one, it respects no one. It denies a hearing to those who pray; it despises the tears of those wailing; it looks down upon the threats of princes, it wipes out the names and glory of kings; it voids the hateful pride of tyrants. Therefore, brother, diligently reflecting on the benefits of the redeemer offered to us, which are very useful, let us make amends for our crimes, shining continually with good character, so that with tears and penance we may be able to appease the strict examination of the judge hurrying to the seat of judgment, who will show himself terrible to the outcasts, and joyful to the chosen. Farewell from Sponheim <note>The Benedictine abbey where the author, Johannes Trithemius, was Abbot.</note>, the fifteenth day before the Kalends of April <note>March 18th</note>, in the year of our Lord 1500.

### Song of Conjuration

**Demoriel ofayr chameron chulty faue porean lusin dayr pean cathurmo fomarson erfoty lamedon iothar bufraym fuar, menadroy chilarso fabelmerusyn.** <note>While this looks like a magical spell, it serves as a "carrier text." In Trithemius's system, a secret message is hidden within these specific words using a pre-arranged cipher or letter-selection rule.</note> Once the song is finished, as prescribed in the art, the sent Spirit will be present and will appear visible to you alone, and to no one else at all; and he speaks the secret into your ear.

## CHAPTER XXI.

->*WHOSE SUPREME RULER AND PRINCE*<-
->*is called Geradiel, dwelling in no specific quarter of the world, but*<-
->*accustomed to being present everywhere as a wanderer.*<-

Besides those emperors, princes, and leaders of Spirits whom we named in the previous chapters—who remain in certain places throughout the visible world—there are also many others assigned who are wandering, unstable, and remain nowhere certain, whom the ancient sages and magi called <term>aetas</term> <note>Original Greek: αήτας, meaning "unstable" or "drifters."</note>, that is, unstable ones, because they fly in the air like flies, without order, without a home, and without restriction. Of these, there are several not useless to us for <term>Steganography</term> <note>The art of "hidden writing." Trithemius presents his cryptographic methods under the guise of summoning spirits to deliver messages.</note>, because they do not require the observance of specific locations, but can be summoned by conjuration in any place.

# BOOK ONE.

The first of these is called **Geradiel**, who does not have any duke or prince beneath him, but only many and almost innumerable servants <note>original: "famulos"</note>, whom he takes with him into service according to the ranks they hold among themselves, and according to the 24 hours of the day and night. Their general duty is to announce all secrets, whatever they may be and from whatever region you desire.

<margin>Hours of the day and night.</margin>

| R. | S. | S. | R. | S. | S. | R. | S. | |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| 2 | 2 | 200 | 100 | 40 | 30 | 50 | 60 | |
| 2 | 2 | 80 | 70 | 100 | 90 | 120 | 110 | |
| R. | 2 | 140 | S. | R. | 150 | 180 | 170 | |
| 2 | 2 | R. | 130 | 160 | | | | |
| 2 | 2 | 2 | 200 | 1000 | 400 | 300 | 60 | 500 |
| 2 | 2 | 800 | 700 | 10000 | 900 | 1200 | 110 | |
<note>This table represents the mathematical distribution of Geradiel’s 15,150 servants across different times. In this system, "R" likely stands for *Regens* (Ruler/Prince) and "S" for *Servi* (Servants).</note>

In this table, you have 15,150 ministers and servants of the great prince **Geradiel**, numbered and arranged in their proper order. You should fully understand their arrangement and the underlying logic for your <term>operation<gloss>a magical ritual or task</gloss></term>, so that you know at what hour they are accustomed to come and with how many servants—or when he himself is not accustomed to come, but instead the servants assigned to that hour. I advise you, however, to take care in your operations to observe the hour in which the prince himself comes in person with the servants of that hour; otherwise, when their commander is absent, they are not always willing to obey the practitioner in everything. Therefore, when you wish to work through this prince, having performed the necessary preliminaries, speak the conjuration.

->Song of Conjuration.<-

**Geradiel onayr bulesar modran pedarbon sazeuo nabor vielis proyn therdial masre reneal Chemarlon cuhadiam almona faclry penoyr satodial chramel nadiatli thorays Vayr pean efridiel cubal draony myar dearsy colludarsy menador atotiel Cumalyn drasnodiar parmy sofiel almenarys fatiel chulty dealny peson duarsy cuber fruony maroy sutiel, fabel merusi venodran praffo lufior lamedó syuaro larboys theory malrosyn.** <note>As with other conjurations in this text, this "song" is a cipher. While it sounds like an ancient incantation, the words are designed to hide a secret message that can only be extracted by a recipient trained in the author's cryptographic methods.</note>

Once the song is finished, the spirit will be present—either the principal one with his servants, or the servants from the number of the hour without the prince, just as you wish according to the hours, as we have said above.
