CHAPTER

->*Whatever secret we wish to entrust, it shall always remain hidden.*<-

**I**f I have any secret, no matter how private, which must be made known to one or more friends who are perfected in the art of Steganography <note>The word literally means "hidden writing." In this context, Trithemius presents it as a form of "angel magic" to hide the fact that it is actually a sophisticated system of cryptography.</note> by means of spirits: I call upon the aforementioned prince at an hour suitable to me and entrust the mystery to him. He receives it, goes forth, and delivers it with complete Steiner-like fidelity. I send a letter for the sake of appearance only, so that men may be deceived; and he to whom I send it knows from the sign written below which spirit he ought to summon, because they do not appear unless they are compelled.

->*I send letters of any kind, not secret ones.*<-

### Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, to all the learned and curious who read this art without a teacher, offers his labor:

> *Amend your lives from the very roots. The strict examination of judgment will suddenly seize everyone (no one is safe), and it will fill the wicked with its bitterness and eternal grief; yet it will not dare to sadden the holy. Wail and deplore your crimes, you most miserable and foolish people. Behold, you reject the name of religion without performing good works. Do you not know that an examination is coming upon the harmful? The final hour approaches: we shall not live long, but we shall all be compelled to pass away horribly and unexpectedly. Therefore, do not neglect your time; honor the grace granted to you through your works. From Sponheim, April 13th, in the year 1500. X.* <note>original: "13. Cal. Aprilis" refers to the 13th day before the Kalends of April, which is March 20th, though Trithemius often uses a simplified dating style.</note>

Once these letters have been received, or any others marked with the character of **Geradiel**, there will be no need to turn elsewhere. Instead, after performing the customary preliminaries of the art, the Song of Conjuration <note>original: "Coniurationis Carmen"</note> must be recited.

->*The Song of Conjuration*<-

**G**eradiel osayl chamerusin chulti pemarsoniel dayr fayr chaturmo les bornatyn erfoty camylor fayr fabelmerodan costy damerson maltey nabelmerusyn. <note>To the uninitiated reader, this looks like a magical incantation. However, in Trithemius’s system, these "magic words" are actually a cipher. By taking specific letters from these words, the recipient extracts the true hidden message.</note> Once this conjuration is completed, the spirits who were sent will appear to you visibly, and they will manifest all things to you, speaking secretly into your ear.

<meta>This page continues the catalog of spirits used in Trithemius’s system of secret communication. While the previous page focused on Geradiel, a spirit associated with sending secret messages through mundane-looking letters, this page introduces Buriel, a far more sinister and nocturnal entity. The transition marks a shift from spirits that can be worked with generally to those that are strictly bound by the time of day and specific, often frightening, appearances.</meta>

BOOK ONE.

## CHAPTER XXII.

### WHOSE PRINCE IS BURIEL OF THE NIGHT, *who shuns the light and dwells in the lakes and pits of the earth: and there is no operation through him except at night.* <note>original: "Lucifugus, & habitans in lacubus & foraminibus terræ: & non sit operatio per eum nisi in nocte." This title warns the practitioner that this spirit is "light-shunning" and tied to the dark places of the world.</note>

**A**fter Geradiel follows Buriel, a prince who is indeed great, but perverse and wicked; he hates, persecutes, and detests all other spirits (especially the princes), but he is no less hated by them in return. He fears the light and flees from it with all his dukes and servants, nor is he ever accustomed to appear except at night; this frequently occurs with great horror and fear for the one performing the work, especially if they are not perfected in the art, or are not constant and brave of mind. He frequently appears in the form of a serpent having a maiden’s head, with a tail and the rest of its body being serpentine, hissing terribly. When summoned in the proper manner, he speaks with human words. He has under him dukes and princes whom he is accustomed to send into service with their attendants and servants, the multitude of whom is almost innumerable. Of these, let us set down the names of some who may suffice for our work.

| Names | S. | R. | S. | R. | S. | R. |
|-------|----|----|----|----|----|----|
| Merosiel | Casbriel | Drusiel | 100 | 10 | 10 | 100. |
| Almadiel | Nedriel | Carniel | 100 | 10 | 10 | 100. |
| Cupriel | Busiel | Drubiel | 100 | 10 | 10 | 100. |
| Sarniel | Futiel | Nastros | 100 | 10 | 10 | 100. |

<note>In these tables, the headers "S." and "R." likely refer to "Spiritus" (Spirits) and their respective counts or rankings within the cryptographic system Trithemius is veiling as magic.</note>

We have in this description of the table 12 of Buriel's princes, with 880 servants, who proceed in their order with their individual dukes according to the 12 <term>unequal hours of the night</term> <note>Also known as "planetary hours," these are calculated by dividing the period between sunset and sunrise into twelve equal portions, meaning the length of an "hour" changed depending on the season.</note>, which we call the hours of the planets, when they have been called by the operator in the proper manner. The Duke is accustomed to appear in the form we have described; however, the servants are frequently seen as monkeys or <term>jesters</term> <note>original: "ioculatores," referring to entertainers, jugglers, or acrobats.</note>, playing and leaping from on high to the ground in a wonderful phantasm. When you wish to work through these princes, do not presume to begin your operation except after sunset, because they do not obey except at night. There is no need to observe a specific direction of the world <note>i.e., the cardinal directions like North or East.</note>; rather, looking at the earth beneath your feet, say this Conjuration:

STEGANOGRAPHY

->*The Incantation.*<-

BVriel mastfoyr chamerusyn, noel pean Ionachym mardusan philarfij, pedarym estlis carmoy boyscharonti phroys fabelronti, mear Laphany vearchas, clarefon, notiel, pador aslotiel, marsy-no reneas, Capedon, thifinafion melro, lauair carpentor, thurneam camelrosyn. <note>This string of words is a magical incantation or "carmen." While the names of the spirits are recognizable, the surrounding words are part of a constructed "angelic" or secret language intended for ritual use.</note> When you have spoken this incantation in silence, always looking toward the ground, the spirits called will be present. But see that you are not terrified: because they cannot harm you, if you are brave and firm of spirit. And note, that these princes or spirits, although generally assigned [to various tasks], are held to announce all nightly secrets during the night: they are especially fit and willing to carry messages into prisons and lakes to captives, and likewise in matters pertaining to carnal love, and the hidden practices of lovers; and to all things which are done at night, whether they be good or bad, because they hate the light.

->*Let us set down a secret, which is to be trusted to absolutely no one, such as:*<-

A secret I might have for a friend residing in this city, which I can signify to him neither by letters, nor by a messenger, lest it become public. Many things stand in my way from visiting the friend myself. I call one of the aforementioned spirits, I commit the mystery to him: he goes, and faithfully announces what I committed: I am safe. <note>Trithemius is using "spirits" as a metaphor for his cryptographic system. To the uninitiated, it looks like magic; to the initiated, it is a method of encoding information.</note>

### I shall fashion letters, of whatever kind I wish, which fear no one, namely, as these are.

*Our Savior Jesus Christ, who redeemed the human race by his blood dying on the Cross, is to be praised by us anxiously and without intermission: he himself will put death to flight, and promised he would deliver eternal life to us who fear and love him. Now therefore let us rise, dearest brothers, from our sins, preparing our lamps with good works: so that we may joyfully meet the redeemer in the hour of the fearful passage, when death shall snatch our souls. We have now plenty of time to fulfill penance, and to adorn the lamps: but in the last hour of death the space for further repenting will be entirely denied to us. Let us consider the very brief glory of the world, since it is vain: it passes most swiftly,*

...most eloquently, and we are corrupted with it every hour. Therefore let us humble our hearts at all times, never failing. In the year 1513, on the Calends of April <note>original: "Cal. April." The Calends refer to the first day of the month; thus, April 1, 1513.</note>.

When he to whom it is sent, being skilled in the art, receives this or any other such letters; having recognized the sign of Buriel, and having performed the necessary preliminaries <note>original: "præmissis præmittendis." ->The Conjuration.<-

BVriel, Thresoy chamerontis, hayr plassu, nadiel, marso, neany, pean, sayr, fabelron, chaturmo, melros, esoty caduberosyn. <note>This "carmen" or incantation consists of "barbarous names of power"—words from an invented or corrupted language intended to compel the spirit to appear.</note> Once the incantation has been spoken, the spirit, invisible to all others but visible to you, will appear and faithfully speak the secret committed to him.

# CHAPTER XXIII.

## *WHOSE SPIRIT AND SUPREME PRINCE*
## *is called Hydriel, and dwells in the waters: having under him 100 dukes,*
## *200 counts, and almost innumerable*
## *servants.*

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut drop cap 'E' featuring scrolling floral patterns within a square frame.</image-desc>

THERE IS another among the principal spirits, a prince who is called Hydriel, and he dwells in waters, ponds, lakes, and marshes, and the sea, and around springs, cisterns, and rivers; having under his dominion at least 100 <term>dukes</term> <note>original: "duces." In Trithemius's system, these are high-ranking spirits governing large groups.</note> or princes, 200 <term>counts</term> <note>original: "comites." Mid-level spirits who serve as subordinates to the dukes.</note> or sub-dukes, and many servants. The duty of these is generally to announce all things both through water and land, whether it be by day or by night. And know, that these princes are quite benevolent and willing in all things, but they do not appear so when they come summoned. For they frequently appear in the form of a serpent, sometimes large, sometimes small; having a very beautiful virginal head with flowing hair. None of them goes alone: but every prince leads with him at least two counts and 80 servants. So that you do not fall into error in the operation, I will set down some names of the dukes for you.

|----------|----------|----------|----|----|-----|-----|
| Mortaliel | Lameniel | Camiel | 10 | 20 | 100 | 200 |
| Chamoriel | Brachiel | Arbiel | 10 | 20 | 100 | 200 |
| Pesariel | Samiel | Lusiel | 10 | 20 | 100 | 200 |
| Musuziel | Dusiriel | Chariel | 10 | 20 | 100 | 200 |

STEGANOGRAPHY

Behold, you have here 12 of the princes and dukes of Hydriel, and 1320 counts and servants. These suffice for your operations in Steganography according to the order of the 24 hours of the day: such that each duke governs 2 hours, with 110 counts and servants in their specific ranks, all of whom you must know thoroughly. When you wish to accomplish anything in Steganography through these princes of spirits: do those things you know must be done according to the art, and recite the incantation.

### The Incantation.

> HYdriel, apron chamerote, satrus pean nearmy chab elon, vearchas, belta, nothelmy phameron, arsoy pedaryn onzel, Lamedo drubel areon veatly cabyn & noty maleros haytny pesary does, pen rasi medusan ilcohi person. <note>This "carmen" consists of "barbarous names"—nonsense words used as a verbal code to trigger the spirit's arrival. In the system of Steganographia, these words often hide a ciphered message or simply serve as a ritualistic focus.</note>

Once the incantation has been spoken, the Spirit whom you have called will be present, together with the servants assigned to him according to that hour. He will be obedient to you in all things, and faithful in everything for which you send him.

<margin>R.</margin> <note>original: "R." Likely standing for *Responsio* (Response) or *Regula* (Rule), marking a practical example.</note>
### Let us devise a secret.

Suppose I have a very hidden secret of any kind to send to a friend, which I cannot safely make known to him through letters or by a messenger. Therefore, so that the secret does not come to light, I call upon the Spirit; he comes, he obeys, I entrust the secret to him, and he carries it faithfully.

<margin>R.</margin>
### Let us draft a letter of any kind.

->Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, to brother Peter Marponius of Gisem, laureate and Monk of Rhenen, sends many greetings.<- <note>original: "S.P.D." stands for *Salutem Plurimam Dicit*, a standard Latin epistolary greeting.</note>

*Brother Nicholas of Irbus, the suffragan Bishop of the most reverend father and most illustrious Duke Adam of Insignisburg of Pantomoran, a most honorable and famous Archprelate and a tireless lover of books, seems most justly to compare the Abbot, Vinilianus by name (because he is one), to beasts—feasting splendidly among the filth of beasts, preferring calves to volumes, just as Reuchlin confirmed.* <note>Johannes Reuchlin (1455–1522) was a famous German humanist and scholar of Greek and Hebrew, and a contemporary of Trithemius.</note> *I ask that you imitate Nicholas, that best and mildest pontiff. Farewell, the 12th day before the Calends of April, 1500.* <note>original: "12. Cal. April. M.D." Using the Roman dating system, this is March 21, 1500.</note> ❦

When you receive these letters, or any others marked with the sign of Hydriel: having observed no consideration of the world <note>This likely means ignoring standard astrological timings or planetary hours usually required in magic.</note>, but having performed only the general...

## CHAPTER XXIV.

### WHOSE PRINCE IS CALLED PYRICHIEL, HAVING under him dukes, princes, counts, and many servants, the number of whom is uncertain.

<image-desc>A decorative drop cap 'P' containing an illustration of a seated figure, possibly a scholar or king, within the bowl of the letter.</image-desc>

PYRICHIEL, a certain supreme prince of spirits, does not seem to us to be entirely rejected by our art, because he is considered to be quite benevolent.

He does not have dukes or princes under him in the manner of the others, but only counts and sub-servants, whose number is uncertain. There are those who say he has dukes and princes under him, but they seem to us to not sufficiently understand his nature and custom; indeed, they have dreamed up counts and servants as being dukes. Moreover, the name of Pyrichiel draws its origin from fire <note>original: "ignis". The name is derived from the Greek *pyr* (fire), suggesting a spirit of the fiery element.</note>, because he is frequently accustomed to be active around fire.

| **R.** <note>Likely standing for *Responsio* (Response) or *Regula* (Rule).</note> | **R.** | | | | | |
|:--- |:--- |:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|
| **Damarsiel** | **Menariel** | 200 | 200 | 100 | 80 | 80 |
| **Cardiel** | **Demediel** | 100 | 600 | 60 | 50 | 50 |
| **Almasor** | **Hurfiel** | | | | | |
| **Nemariel** | **Cuprifiel** | 400 | 30 | 30 | 10 | 10 |

You now have eight of the counts of Pyrichiel with the servants assigned to their service according to the order of the hours. Therefore, when you wish to work through these aforementioned spirits, call one of them, whichever you wish; and when you work again another time, then you shall call the one immediately following: and remember that the same is to be done with the others. Therefore, having first performed the things usually performed in the art generally, speak this conjuration.

STEGANOGRAPHY

### *The Conjuration.*

**P**yrichiel marfoys chameron, nael peanos pury lames iameno famerusyn mearlo canorson theory torfa, nealthis dilumeris maphroy carsul amcor thubra phorsotiel chrebonos aray pemalon layr toysi vadiniel nemor roseuarsy cabti phroys amenada machyr fabelronthis, poyl carepon vemij naslotyn. <note>As with previous chapters, these "barbarous names" are not a known language but serve as a cryptographic key or a ritual focus designed to look like a magical incantation.</note>

When these things have been completed, you will see the Spirits whom you have summoned present: and the principal one is accustomed to appear always in the form of a serpent, having a beautiful head like that of a virgin, with flowing hair. Follows:

### *Let us suppose any secret.*

**L**et me have a secret for a friend who is far away, which I cannot safely send by letter, lest the letters be read if intercepted or certainly seized on the way. I have less trust in a man to carry it; indeed, for I have known that the loyalty in men changes with their fortune. So that all things may be safe, I call upon the Spirit, the friend of the secret: he comes quickly, once the song is heard, and carries the commission faithfully. <note>Trithemius uses the "Spirit" as a metaphor for the hidden message itself, which "travels" securely because it is hidden in plain sight.</note>

### *Let us form the letters as we please.*

**John Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, to Jacobus Draconius, Canon of the Premonstratensian order, greetings and charity.**

***T**HERE is with us a distinguished mathematician and an excellent rhetorician, constantly burning with the study of a most tranquil life. He has with him almost infinite books, and an inestimable treasure of diverse copies: a pleasant and benevolent teacher of the right and good things of life, and of honest society: most expert in resolving doubts. He explains great matters: his name is Orophanius, Burgrave of the kingdom of Bosnia. Farewell. March 21st.* <note>original: "12. Calend. April." This letter is a "cover text." The actual secret message is hidden within the words of this seemingly mundane update about a visiting scholar named Orophanius.</note>

When he to whom they are sent, being skilled in the art of Steganography, has received these or any similar letters marked with the character of Pyrichiel; let him do as he knows should be done according to the art; then, having lit a light, let him say that Conjuration.

### *The Conjuration.*

**P**yrichiel ofayr Chamerosy culty melano dayr fabelron cathurmo pean erfoty meor iathor cabon Frilastro melrusy. Once the song

### CHAPTER 25.

->*WHOSE EMPEROR AND PRINCE is called EMONIEL, who has 100 sub-leaders and princes, and many counts and sub-servants: who hold general office over all things.*<-

The prince of this Chapter, Emoniel, along with his leaders and princes, is wandering and restless, and frequently dwells in the woods; he does not flee from the light, and is benevolent and ready for whatever you might command of him; he gladly appears when called, whether by day or by night. He has under his dominion 100 princes and leaders, and no fewer than 20 counts or sub-leaders, with servants and slaves without a fixed number. From these, we shall name a few who may suffice for our work, reserving the others to be named in their own place with the rest, if there be a need.

| Name | Name | Name | Number | Number | Number | Number |

| *Ermoniel* | *Dramiel* | *Cruhiel* | **10** | **20** | **100** | **20.** |
| *Edriel* | *Pandiel* | *Armesiel* | **10** | **20** | **100** | **20.** |
| *Carnodiel* | *Vasenel* | *Caspaniel* | **10** | **20** | **100** | **20.** |
| *Phanuel* | *Nasmiel* | *Musiniel* | **10** | **20** | **100** | **20.** |

You now have 12 of Emoniel's leaders, with 1,320 of their sub-leaders and servants, who—according to the order of the hours—suffice for every operation of our intention, whether the work be by night or by day. And you should know that prince Emoniel, along with his leaders, is accustomed to appear with the head and body of a virgin, but with a serpent's tail. Therefore, when you wish to work anything in Steganography <note>The art of hidden writing; here, Trithemius uses the framework of "spirit summoning" to describe his cryptographic methods.</note> through the spirits of Emoniel: perform those things which you know generally must be done according to the art; and afterwards read this conjuration, silently naming the spirit to be called.

18.
STEGANOGRAPHY

Once the conjuration has been spoken in the way the art itself requires, the spirit whom you summoned will appear, ready to obey in all things, whatever you might command of him, because he is benevolent and faithful.

->*A secret, of whatever kind you wish.*<-

I have a friend who is everywhere far too trusting toward everyone, who cherishes enemies as friends, from whom he often receives both a bad reputation and loss. He is gullible toward the charms of flatterers, and especially toward one who stays close to his side; he does not realize this person is secretly his adversary, because he sees him as charming in public. I desire to accuse this man, yet in such a way that the counsel is made known to my friend alone: it shall be hidden from all others. <note>original: "Amicum habeo..." This paragraph serves as an example of the "cover text"—the innocent message that a casual reader would see.</note>

->*Let the content of the letter be shaped as you please.*<-

# Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, to the Reverend Father and Lord Nicholas, Bishop of Irburfiensis, Vicar of the Archbishop of Pantomoranus, sends many greetings. <note>The names "Irburfiensis" and "Pantomoranus" are likely pseudonyms or encoded references, typical of Trithemius’s style of addressing fictional or obscured dignitaries.</note>

*When I recall your talent and the honesty of your life, I am most strongly compelled to declare you the most worthy of all Bishops, Reverend and most excellent Father. Indeed, whether I look upon the learning of the noble arts within you, or the innocence of your life, what else could I call you but most just, most learned in all literature, and venerable for your most holy character? You adorn your conduct with works of righteousness and always show yourself to be the best shepherd of souls, and you continually exercise yourself in study, so that you may benefit both yourself and others. Farewell. Given from Sponheim, the 11th of the Calends of April [March 22], in the year of our Lord’s Nativity 1501.*

After the person to whom the letter is sent—being perfectly trained in the art of <term>Steganography<gloss>The "hidden writing" system developed by Trithemius, which uses the appearance of magic to mask cryptography.</gloss></term>—receives it, let him do as he knows must be done according to the art; having performed these things, let him add the Conjuration.

->*The Song of Conjuration.*<-

Emoniellebos chamerothy meor pemorfy dyor medulorfyn fray pean, Crymarsy melrosyne vari chabaryn dayr. Aschre cathurmo fabelron erfoty marduse. <note>This "song" or incantation is actually a ciphertext. In this specific system, the secret message is often hidden in the second letter of each word or through a similar positional key.</note>

When the song is duly completed, the summoned spirit will be present in his accustomed form and will reveal to you, with faithfulness, all the individual secrets that had been committed to him.

BOOK ONE.

### CHAPTER XXVI.

->**THE SUPREME EMPEROR OF THIS ORDER IS CALLED ICOSIEL**, who has under him 100 leaders or princes, 300 counts or subordinates; the number of servants is not yet certain to me.<-

**Icosiel** is one of the more powerful among the <term>aerial spirits</term><note>In the cosmology of the time, these spirits were thought to inhabit the air and could be commanded to carry messages across distances.</note>, a great and mighty Emperor in the secrets of **Steganography** <note>original: "Steganographiæ." Trithemius coined this term to mean "hidden writing"—essentially cryptography disguised as magic.</note>, having under him 100 princes of ducal rank. There are 300 Counts, though I have not yet discovered the exact number of servants. He is called Icosiel because he dwells willingly with men in their houses. For once you have summoned any of his dukes—properly bound by oath according to the art—along with his counts, if you wish for him to remain with you for all the days of your life: assign him a secret and hidden place in your house. Once you strongly command him through the conjuration to stay there, he will obey you joyfully and will always be ready at your command.

| $R$. | $R$. | $R$. | | | | $R$. |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Machariel** | **Larphiel** | **Athefiel** | 10 | 300 | 100 | 30 |
| **Psichiel** | **Amediel** | **Vrbariiel** | 10 | 300 | 100 | 30 |
| **Thanatiel** | **Cambriel** | **Cumariel** | 10 | 300 | 100 | 30 |
| **Zofiel** | **Zachriel** | **Heresiel** | 10 | 300 | 100 | 30 |
| **Agapiel** | **Nathriel** | **Munefiel** | 10 | 300 | 100 | 30 |

Here we have named 15 of the 100 dukes of Icosiel, along with 50 sub-dukes and 2150 servants. These spirits, according to the order assigned to them within the 24 hours of the day and night, are accustomed to come when called by the practitioner for the service of Steganography. They are capable and willing for anything you desire, provided that you are perfectly learned in the art, and remain constant and unafraid during the operation: for they appear in the form and likeness of a serpent, yet having the heads of virgins. <note>This "serpentine form with a virgin's head" is a common heraldic or mythological image, perhaps intended here to test the practitioner's resolve.</note> Therefore, when you wish to work through any of them, perform what must be done according to the art, and add this Conjuration:

> **ICOSIEL aphorsi chamersyn thulneas ianotiel menear peanos**
> **erasnotiel medursan matory sabelron erfonial cathurmos laer-**

# STEGANOGRAPHY

...noty befraym alphayr lamedonti nael cabeltron <note>This is the conclusion of a magical incantation from the previous page; these phonetic formulas are typical of Renaissance "spirit magic" and are intended to be spoken aloud rather than translated literally.</note>. Once this conjuration is complete, the called spirits will appear without delay, to whom you can securely commit your secret to be carried to a friend.

->*Prepare the letters to be sent according to your pleasure. Let the secret be,*<-
->*whatever you wish.*<-

Suppose I have a friend far away who is skilled in the art of **Steganography** <note>original: "Steganographiæ"; a term coined by Trithemius meaning "hidden writing."</note>, to whom I wish to send a great secret and mystery that should not be trusted to anyone else, nor even suggested via ordinary letters. Therefore, so that all things remain secret, I send a spirit as a secret messenger, who may reveal the secret to my friend and keep my business hidden.

*Let us amend our lives, humbling ourselves before the supreme Creator of the universe. Humility reaches Heaven, annihilates the harmful temptations of demons, restrains the ruinous pride of the mind, and powerfully exterminates all the fires of vice. The path of humility deserves to receive a crown and possess a noble kingdom. I establish this path for you: innocence and honesty of character; by guarding these diligently, you shall not sin. This is our only way to accumulate the merit of blessedness: that, giving thanks to God, we may fear Him with a most upright heart, fleeing the tumult of the world. Let us never grow weary of the exercise of humility as we seek the life above. Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim. Written on the 23rd of March, 1500.* <note>original: "10. Cal. Apr. M D." Trithemius often uses these pious, religious passages as "cover texts." To an outside observer, this looks like a simple moral letter, but to the initiated, it contains a hidden message encoded through a specific cipher system.</note>

# BOOK ONE. CHAPTER 27.

## WHOSE SUPREME SPIRIT AND PRINCE IS CALLED Soleuiel, who has under his dominion 200 principal dukes, 200 sub-dukes and counts, and innumerable servants.

Among those spirits who freely converse with men skilled in the art of **Steganography** <note>original: "Steganographiæ"; the art of "hidden writing" or secret communication through spirits.</note>, there is one named **Soleuiel**. He is not the least among the princes of this art, having under his command 200 principal dukes and another 200 sub-dukes or counts. They observe an infallible order among themselves: those who are dukes this year become counts the following year, and they alternate roles, dukes becoming counts and counts becoming dukes. I have not yet discovered the certain number of their servants, who also guard their own ranks among themselves according to the disposition of the hours.

| **Rule** | | **Rule** | | **Rule** | | **Rule** | |
|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|
| Inachiel | Nadrusiel | Axosiel | 20 | 20 | 20 | 200 | 200 |
| Praxeel | Cobusiel | Charoel | 20 | 20 | 20 | 200 | 200 |
| Morucha | Amriel | Murfiel | 20 | 20 | 20 | 200 | 200 |
| Almodar | Prasiel | Penador | 20 | 20 | 20 | 200 | 200 |

Here you have twelve of the princes of Soleuiel, of whom the first six are dukes this year, and the remaining six are counts. In the following year, the latter will be dukes and the former counts, and so on consecutively in alternating turns. I have calculated the number of sub-servants to be 1,840, although there are many more whom I have not yet been able to count. When you wish to work through these spirits, be steadfast and brave in your soul; do not be terrified by their appearance when you see them appearing in the form of **serpents with a maiden's head** <note>This visual—a "Melusine" or hybrid creature—was a common way Renaissance magicians described the psychological or spectral "masks" worn by spirits.</note>. Then, perform what must be done according to the art; once these things are prepared, add this Song of Conjuration.

-> *Song of Conjuration.* <-

*Soleuiel matsoy chametusyn oniel dabry diuiel pean vear, lasmyn eralmoty pedaros drumes, pean vear chameton loes madur noty bassay erxo nadtus peliel thabren th yiso ianothin vear petasy loes peannethyr fabelton bauesy diameron es hiran pumelon meor dabrios crimorfiel peny vear nametoy iyernoti praisoner.* <note>In the context of Trithemius's work, these "barbarous words" are often actually transposed ciphers for hidden Latin or German text, though they are presented here as a spoken magical incantation.</note>

When the song has been properly completed, the spirits called will appear in their accustomed form, to whom you may commit your secret.

-> *The Secret: we shall set it as we please.* <-

A friend far away, previously skilled in the art of **Steganography**,

Furthermore, I consider it necessary to advise regarding certain imminent dangers; but I fear the public, lest by handling another’s danger I make it my own. To avoid this, I entrust the secret neither to letters nor to men, but to a Spirit, whom I have known to be most faithful in all things.

->*Let us form letters as we please.*<-

## Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim of the Order of Saint Benedict, to the Right Reverend Lord Rutger Sycambre, Archbishop of Neuss, sends greetings. <note>Rutger Sycambre (c. 1460–1514) was a fellow monk and friend of Trithemius. While he is addressed here as an Archbishop, this is part of the "steganographic" fiction of the letter.</note>

*Nicholas, the most reverend and studious Bishop of Irburis, will explain to you that the general governor of your ecclesiastical affairs has managed matters poorly, casting into chains and killing by starvation a kind man, the vicar of our lord King Desiderius. We daily expect King Desiderius to break into the diocese as an enemy. But we implore you to bring aid quickly, so that you do not fall into the enemy’s hands. Farewell. March 24, 1503.* <note>original: "9. Calend. April. M.D. 3." The 9th day before the Calends of April is March 24th.</note>

When he to whom these letters are sent receives them (provided he has been previously instructed in the general art), let him do what must be done, adding this conjuration with a steadfast mind, fearing nothing.

->The Conjuration.<-

SOleuiel curtiel chamerusyn saty pemalros dayr ianothy cathurmo parmoy iotran lamedon frascu penoy ilthon fabelmerufyn. <note>In the context of the Steganographia, these "magical" words are often codes. When the first and last letters of words or specific patterns are extracted, they reveal the hidden message. To the uninitiated, they look like a demonic incantation.</note> When this song has been spoken, the Spirit sent by the practitioner will appear visibly, faithfully revealing everything committed to him; and if the practitioner wishes to send anything back, he may entrust it to the same Spirit.

# BOOK ONE.

<note>original: "LIBER PRIMVS"</note>

...we have known, and experienced quite fully. Therefore, among other spirits who favor this art, there is one who has received the name **Menadiel** by reason of his office; he has under him 20 princes and dukes, 100 counts, and many servants, the number of whom is uncertain to me. All of these are faithful in their commissions and are the best messengers in the great affairs of kings and princes, through whom we shall perform wonders.

| | ☉ <note>The Sun</note> | | ☾ <note>The Moon</note> | ☿ <note>Mercury</note> | | |
| :--- | :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :--- | :--- |

| Clamor | Samyel | 20 | 10 | 100 | Baruch | Tharfon |

We now have six of the dukes, and just as many counts named, with 390 servants also assigned to them through their turns and orders. They maintain this order among themselves: in the first turn, two dukes and one count are called; he who is called again in the second turn then joins the order of dukes. In the third turn, he who was first in the first order of dukes becomes a count, and so on consequently. Therefore, when you wish to operate anything through the Spirits of **Menadiel**: consider diligently which duke and which count must be called by you according to the time and the hours; and, with the necessary preliminaries completed, speak the Conjuration.

->*The Conjuration.*<-

**Menadiel marfoy peanos onael chamerusyn theorianothy ofayr melros tudayr penorfyn sachul tarno roseuas peathāafiel morfoy maplcar casmyron storeal marpenu nosayr pelno dan layrshubra elnodion carsephy drumos fabelmerusyn andu pean, purays calbyn nachir loes philuemy casaner.** <note>While presented as a magical incantation, these strings of words in the *Steganographia* are actually a polyalphabetic cipher. The "spirit names" and "conjurations" hide the actual message being sent.</note> When this Conjuration has been duly completed, the summoned spirit will be present—namely, first one count, and two dukes with their servants.

->*A secret is made of whatever kind occurs.*<-

Let there be a secret <term>arcanum<span>A hidden or private matter; in this context, the specific message being encoded.</span></term> for you to a male or female friend, of whatever kind occurs according to the time or variety of affairs, which you do not wish to be made common to anyone else. Call the spirit, entrust the business to him, and fear nothing; he is the most faithful of all, and will fulfill what has been well-committed to him.

# STEGANOGRAPHY.
<note>original: "STEGANOGRAPHIÆ"</note>

You may form letters concerning these matters in whatever way you wish, according to your pleasure.

*Great and eternal are the future rewards for those who observe innocence: that men, rejoicing, may be present amidst angelic praises: to behold that lofty light: the splendor of eternal blessedness, and to see the face of majesty by its own light. Pay heed all of you, and wipe away through tears the past negligences; cast off useless and harmful cares: the deceptions of unstable things. O men of clay, shudder! Seek at last the gain of souls. Be honorable through good morals and virtues: despise vanities: humbly invoke God: do not covet the world, which pours forth bitterness. In the year 1509, on the 9th day before the Kalends of April <note>March 24, 1509</note>. ☿ <note>The symbol for Mercury, serving here as a code-signal for the spirit Menadiel.</note>.*

**M**enadyel murty chamerole dayr pean cathurmo phameron ersoti pray saruepo, fabel merij rean, charon ietlas Meduse. sayr lamerosy nalty merchahon. <note>While these words appear to be a magical incantation, in the context of Trithemius’s cryptography, they serve as a system of phonetic keys to extract the hidden message from the preceding "innocent" letter.</note> Once the incantation has been spoken, the sent spirit will appear visible to the one calling, and will faithfully and secretly report everything in his ear which he has in his commissions.

### CHAPTER XXIX.

->WHOSE SUPREME PRINCE IS CALLED MACARIEL, having under his command many dukes, princes, counts, and servants appointed to various ministries.<-

<image-desc>Decorative historiated woodcut initial M featuring scrolling acanthus leaf patterns.</image-desc>

**M**acariel, also one of the supreme spirits who preside over the operations of <term>Steganography</term> <note>Steganography: From the Greek *steganós* (hidden) and *graphein* (to write); the art of secret writing, which Trithemius disguised as a system of spirit magic.</note>, must not be omitted by us along with his princes and servants, because he is quite useful, prompt, and faithful in all things whatsoever we may wish. He has, moreover, 40 princes, of whom never fewer than 4 are sent into service—namely three dukes and one count with not a few servants. They observe this order among themselves: that by their turns one of the dukes always performs the office of a count, according to the four parts of the year. And concerning all these it is necessary for the practitioner to have full knowledge. But now from these...

# BOOK ONE
<note>original: "LIBER PRIMVS"</note>

...let us name some of the princes, who will be more than sufficient for us in the operation of our Steganography <term>Steganography: Here, the art of hiding a secret message within an innocent-looking text, framed as a magical operation.</term>.

| | | | R. | R. | R. | R. |
|---------|----------|---------|----|----|----|----|
| Claniel | Afmadiel | Gremiel | 40 | 30 | 20 | 10 |

We have now named 12 of the princes of Macariel, along with 400 servants accounted for according to their turns and ranks. Therefore, when you wish to work through them in Steganography (take care that you call no fewer than four at minimum from those named above); after first performing the customary preliminaries, say this Conjuration.

->Incantation<-
<note>original: "Carmen"</note>

> Macariel myrno chamerosy purmy marefyn amos peanam olradu, chaber ianoes fabelron dearsy chadon vlyses Almos rutiel pedaron deabry madero neas lamero dearsy, thubra dorpilto melrosyne draor chalmea near, parmō dearsy charō alnodiel parsaradean, maroy reneas charsō gnole, melrosin te dransō casmar ebrolet, Landrys masfayr therafonte noel amalan. <note>While presented as a magical chant, these words function as a phonetic key to decode the hidden message in the letter below.</note> When this incantation is spoken, the summoned spirits will be present, appearing in various forms, and indeed frequently with a maidenly head, the body and tail ending in the form of a dragon, coiling and uncoiling themselves in a fourfold rank.

->Let the secret be for the prince.<-
<note>original: "Arcanum sit ad principem." This indicates the intended recipient of the secret message.</note>

A prefect of a king or prince, stationed in a land or province, has learned through a most secret report that enemies intend to invade the province shortly. He wants to advise the prince, but he cannot use messengers, because they would be tortured by adversaries on the road to force them to betray the secret; nor can he use letters, since all are opened by the enemy. He therefore calls the spirit, commits the secret to it, and fakes an unrelated letter.

## Let us devise letters that lead to nothing.
<note>original: "Literas fingamus nihil conducentes." These are "dummy letters" intended to look boring or unimportant to a censor.</note>

We ask, sweetest of friends, that you might wish to write back to us regarding your copy of the most noble and wise man Tertullian <note>Quintus Septimius Florens Tertullianus (c. 155 – c. 220 AD), an early Christian author; a request for a theological book would seem unremarkable to a guard.</note>, in view of our old friendship...

<meta>catchword: swiftly</meta>

<meta>This page concludes the instructions for the spirit Macariel and introduces the next major figure in the hierarchy, Uriel. The text continues the method of disguising secret messages within a pious-sounding letter—in this case, discussing church fathers like Bernard of Clairvaux and Tertullian—before transitioning to the cryptographic key (the 'conjuration') and the next chapter.</meta>

...or to send quickly. We all know the Letter of Bernard to the Templars <note>referring to *In Praise of the New Knighthood* by St. Bernard of Clairvaux</note>, and that the glorious name of Catholic holiness in Tertullian was exhibited of old among our people; and we know that noble Latin author did not remain the last in the sacred faith. He was distinguished in his actions, a glorious teacher of divine law, and a most devoted champion of our liberty. Although he is criticized in some matters, he nevertheless wrote honorable volumes explaining our faith. Moreover, he had a zealous wife, a lover of our religion, noble in humility and innocence, and glorious in chastity. Some of his praiseworthy lucubrations <term>lucubrations: scholarly writings produced by intensive study, typically at night</term> of right instruction remain, many sweet and overflowing teachings; brief letters that are not useless, honorably and graciously urging us to the pursuit of eternal glory. Written on the 25th of March <note>original: "8. Cal. April."</note> in the year 1500 by Johannes Trithemius.

When you have received these letters or any others to whom they are sent, being previously skilled in the art of Steganography <term>steganography: the practice of concealing a secret message within an ordinary-looking text or image</term>, once the sign of Macariel is recognized, let the recipient do what must be done according to custom, adding the Song.

->Incantation of Conjuration<-
<note>original: "Carmen Conjurationis"</note>

**M**acariel osayr chamerose chulti pesano dayr fameron cathurmo pean erfoty lamedon souapor castrea masyr. Ianos tharfia, peathan non acri pean etion matramy. <note>While these appear to be magical names, they function as a cryptographic key. The recipient would use the letters of these words to extract the hidden message from the innocent-looking letter about Tertullian above.</note> Once the song has been duly completed, the spirit that was sent will appear visible to him alone.

### CHAPTER XXX.

**B**ut it does not befit us to omit the great prince Uriel, whom we know from experience (our teacher) does not hold the last place of usefulness among the supporters of this art. He has under his dominion and command 10 dukes and princes assigned to this art of Steganography, and 100 counts or sub-dukes who always accompany these dukes, each in his own order, and many servants whose certain number is not yet known to me.

Here we have the named leaders <term>duces: literally "dukes," these are high-ranking spirits in Trithemius's system</term> with their companions and subordinates, whose service is sufficient for the completion of this art of ours for the present time. And note, that those first in the order are the leaders and princes. The second are the Companions <term>comites: literally "counts," acting as secondary officers</term>; they have two ranks among themselves, which one must know above all things. Those who are in the first rank, both leaders and companions, appear monstrous whenever they are called—specifically with a maiden’s head, but with a body and tail in the form of a serpent. Those who are in the second rank are accustomed to appear in a likeness familiar and common to us. Nor do fewer spirits usually come than one leader and one companion following him in either rank. When you wish to work through these spirits, first do according to what you know must be done by the art; afterwards, add this conjuration.

### Conjuration.

**V**riel matfoys lamedonti noes, chameron, anducharpean phusciel arsmony tuerchoy iamersyn nairiel penos raseon loes vear fabelruso cralty layr parlis meraij meaz, thubra aslotiel dubyreanu nauosti masliel pedonyte chemarphin. <note>While this looks like a magical incantation, in the context of Steganography, these "spirit names" and strings of words act as a cryptographic key for the recipient to decode the hidden message.</note> Once the conjuration is completed, the summoned spirits will be present, each in their order, ready and cheerful to obey in all things.

->:. Q<-

### The secret becomes very great.

**S**uppose I have a certain secret <term>arcanum: a profound mystery or a piece of hidden knowledge</term> intended for a prince or a friend, the disclosure of which would bring no small danger to me or harm to him. In order, therefore, that all things remain secret between me and him: I entrust the secret not to a man, nor to letters, but to a spirit:

### The letters may be as desired, fearing nothing.

**B**egin, most beloved brothers, to correct your negligences, wiping away past sins with constant tears, entreating the Savior of all the living in the time remaining: Hear, O Lord, the groans of those weeping constantly: [grant] forgive—

->WHOSE SUPREME PRINCE IS CALLED BY-<-

One still remains of the supreme spirits assigned to us for the work of Steganography: indeed the last in order, but equal to the first in dignity: whose name, by reason of his office, is called Bydiel. He has under his command 20 leaders <term>duces: high-ranking spirits or "dukes" in the hierarchy</term>, 200 counts or sub-leaders <term>comites: secondary officers or "counts"</term>, and many servants and slaves. These leaders and counts maintain a certain order among themselves: and as often as they are called by the operator, two leaders come with 20 counts, and they appear in human form, calm and ready for all things,

### Let my secret be whatever it may.

**I** have a secret, which I desire a friend far away to know, and it is very private: it concerns a hidden matter which would be ruined if the mystery should happen to be made public in any way. Therefore, I entrust it to be carried not to a man, nor to letters, but to spirits alone, whom I know to be both secure and faithful.

# OF STEGANOGRAPHY
### BOOK ONE

**O**f all the elements of our Steganography <note>original: "Steganographiæ nostræ"</note> of which we have spoken in this, our first book: assigning to each its own appropriate symbol <term>character: a unique sigil or cryptographic sign used to represent a spirit or key</term>, and adding also the number and order of the princes, dukes, counts, servants, and attendants in their proper ranks <note>Trithemius uses the metaphor of a spirit hierarchy to organize his cryptographic keys and methods.</note>, so that no one wishing to study this art might fall into error because of the diversity of its operations. Indeed, the great mysteries of this art can be penetrated only by the most studious of men—those, namely, whom a love of secrets and nature moves to deep inquiry, and who, in their desire to attain knowledge, refuse no honest and possible labor. 

However, let it be understood that if lazy men of dull wit—whom neither a love for the secrets of wisdom ignites to study, nor nature assists—are unable to understand this most secret art of our invention, it is not to be imputed to us, but rather ascribed to their own sluggishness, laziness, and malice. For these are men who either have their wits immersed in the pleasures of the flesh and worldly cares, and thus cannot find the time to devote themselves to these matters with diligence, or who, considering themselves already wise or most learned, despise our compositions as if they were unworthy of their reading. 

There will also be some so accustomed to standing by ancient and foreign traditions <note>original: "pristinis alienisq; traditionibus"</note> that whatever they have not learned therein, they judge to be either impossible or superstitious. Nothing is more unfit for the knowledge of this profound art than such men, who either will not or cannot contemplate its highest depths. 

Furthermore, we have determined to provide a few warnings at the end of this book for those who are both fit and willing to study this art (if any such men should exist in the future), so that they may be able to profit by it. First, they must be warned to always keep the art itself hidden <note>original: "occultam"</note>, lest it fall into the hands and knowledge of the wicked, who might commit many and execrable crimes through it. For even if the art itself is truly good in its own right, its practice <term>practica: the actual application or "working" of the system</term> could be turned to evil by the impious no less than it could be led toward the good by the virtuous. 

Therefore, let it remain a secret among the good, for it would be harmful if made public among the wicked. Yet, the good should not despise this knowledge simply because it benefits the evil for their ill ends, just as one does not scorn a sword because it can strike a man down. Next, the students of this art must be warned not to presume to proceed toward working in it, unless they have first [mastered] all those things which are required for the 
->practice<-

<summary>Trithemius concludes Book One by defending his "art" against the lazy and the closed-minded, while issuing a stern warning that these cryptographic secrets must be kept from the wicked to prevent their misuse.</summary>
<keywords>ethics of secrecy, cryptographic symbols, spiritual hierarchy, intellectual diligence, Johannes Trithemius, occult knowledge</keywords>

--- Page 92 ---
<meta>This page continues the closing warnings of Book One. Trithemius transitions from the technical tables of spirits (which function as cryptographic keys) to the specific requirements for the "operator" or practitioner to successfully execute the art without error or personal risk.</meta>

# BOOK ONE

...practical knowledge <note>original: "practicam scientiam"</note>, they have been perfectly instructed. For it does not escape me how great the dangers are to which those who are less skilled subject themselves when they attempt to perform any operation in this matter.

Furthermore, he who is well-instructed and wishes to work through this science <term>scientia: here meaning a structured system of knowledge or art</term> should remember to observe diligently—according to what we have said regarding both the circle and the individual tables and chapters—the differences, locations, names, orders, and offices of the supreme Spirits <note>In the context of Steganographia, "Spirits" serve as metaphors for the cryptographic keys and planetary/celestial timing required to decode the hidden messages.</note>, as well as their leaders <note>original: "duces"</note>, and the total number of counts <note>original: "comites"</note>; what order they observe, and how many substitutes they preside over, so that he may know how to express these things in the conjuration <term>coniuratione: the specific formula or ritualized string of words used to encode the message</term>. For unless the operator of this art guards all these things diligently, he will not be able to succeed, nor will he reach the intended effect of his purpose. Again, let him attend carefully to when each spirit is to be called for which offices or for announcing which secrets, and in which region of the world <note>original: "orbis plagam"; referring to the four cardinal directions</note> that spirit dwells, lest he err in the locations or in any of the details regarding those spirits he wishes to call.

Likewise, let everyone intending to work in this science be advised to take the utmost care to pronounce the words of the conjurations properly, in which the great power of our operation consists. For when an error is committed in the conjurations, the spirits called (or to be called) not only fail to obey, but even resist entirely. Consequently, let him be forewarned not to call one in place of another, but each one in his own order, time, and office, just as has been sufficiently stated by us in almost every chapter of this book. For if, whether by error or any other negligence, he calls one for another, he will gain nothing in his operation, but will instead provoke danger for himself.

In the conjurations, both the spirit who is called and his substitutes should be expressed by name, and the cause of the calling should be established in the mind beforehand. Nor should the act of operation be performed for trivial or profane matters, which one could otherwise safely communicate to another without danger, whether through letters or messengers. For this art is not to be used except for announcing great and difficult things <note>original: "magnis & arduis"</note>, which, if made public, would bring either loss or danger to the practitioner. It is also necessary for every worker in this art to know the natures of the spirits: which are good, which are evil, which are prompt and benevolent toward obedience, and which are harsh and rebellious. Again,

<sig>L 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: who</meta>

<summary>Trithemius provides a stern warning on the necessity of precision and moral gravity when using his "art," detailing how the practitioner must correctly name and categorize the spirits (keys) to avoid failure or danger.</summary>
<keywords>cryptography, conjuration, spirit hierarchy, ritual precision, secrecy, practitioner's conduct, technical mastery</keywords>

--- Page 93 ---
<meta>This page concludes the operational instructions for Book One, providing rules for the practitioner's conduct and the necessity of using physical "characters" or seals. It then transitions into the formal opening of Book Two, which is dedicated to Philip, Count Palatine of the Rhine.</meta>

...wise to know who presides over nocturnal operations and who over diurnal ones. For unless the practitioner knows the natures of the spirits before he begins to work, he will advance only with the greatest difficulty and will easily fall into a state of confusion. Likewise, when the spirits called appear visible before the operator, once the secret word <note>original: "verbo arcano"</note> has been sent forth, he should commit the secret to the leader or count of his mind with appropriate and fitting words in silence. There is no need to speak to the spirit with a loud voice; rather, let him use a lowered one <note>original: "voce... submissa"</note>. 

Great care must also be taken by both the sender and the recipient not to perform anything pertaining to Steganography in the presence of other men who are ignorant of this art, provided they can have access to a secret place. If they cannot be alone, but are forced by circumstance to work among others, let them act so secretly, so prudently, and with such industry that none of those sitting around may perceive anything of the presence of the Spirits. For all Spirits assigned to the service of this art are of such a nature and condition that they utterly detest and flee the tumult of men, and they hate public assembly. 

Furthermore, the operator must be careful not to direct a Spirit without letters, or at least the character <term>character: a symbolic seal or signature used to identify and command a specific spiritual force or cryptographic key</term> of its emperor; for where the spirit does not see that character impressed, it utterly scorns to obey the caller or to carry the secret to anyone. We send letters, however, for a twofold reason, even though we could announce a secret through a Spirit alone without letters: namely, so that we may keep the men carrying the character free from suspicion, and so that we may compel the spirit bound by that character to obey the friend <note>The "friend" refers to the intended recipient of the secret message.</note>.

Although these are sufficient for the announcement of all secrets, however hidden they may be,*

### BOOK TWO.

...the services may abundantly suffice for every worker. Lest we seem to omit anything that could contribute to the perfection of this art, we have decided to join this second book to the first. Since, according to the tradition of certain wise men, individual spirits are assigned to each hour of the day and night for various operations and diverse, wondrous effects, it has pleased us to imitate their order only—not their superstition. We take from them what is conducive to our purpose without injury to the Christian name; the rest, being full of magical arts and contrary to our faith, we shall pass over with contempt. 

Therefore, for each of the hours, both of the day and of the night, we shall proceed by adopting the principal Spirits—the supreme commanders of the art—along with the other officials necessary for our proposed operation. These are assigned through the secret of Solomon (also known as Hermes <note>original: "Solomonis, cognominati Hermetis"</note>) in his volume on Magic. We shall provide both the method and the teaching to all those studious in this art, by which they may, at any hour and with safer counsel, reveal the secret and hidden concept of their minds to their friends. For every prince presides over his Spirits, dukes, counts, and servants like a watchman; at his command, those called by us with a legitimate incantation <note>original: "legitimo carmine"; here Trithemius likely refers to the encoded messages themselves which "call" the secret meaning forth</note> come to us, deliver the secrets committed to them, and show themselves obedient and prompt in all things. 

But since I am to make mention of Spirits once again, I believe I must preface my words once more: nothing in this art of ours is to be considered frivolous; nothing is contrary to the Evangelical tradition or the Catholic faith; nothing at all is taught that is superstitious. For all things that we have said in the preceding volume, or are about to say in the following ones, are supported by natural, lawful, and honest principles. Only the mystery—veiled by foreign institutions and words wrapped in the names of spirits—requires a learned reader. Indeed, we employ the service of the "Spirit" to veil a secret that would harm the wicked if it were made public. And so that no one fails through an error in calculating the hours, let it be known that we have adopted the system of planetary hours <term>planetary hours: a system where the day and night are each divided into 12 hours, meaning the length of an "hour" changes depending on the season and the time of sunrise</term> for both day and night.

# CHAPTER I.

->**SAMAEL, SUPREME COMMANDER, PRESIDES OVER THE FIRST HOUR**<-
->*of the day: he has under his command many dukes, counts, and servants, whom he sends during this hour only when called by a practitioner expert in Steganography.*<-

Of the first hour of the day, which takes its beginning at sunrise, the Spirit and Commander is called Samael. He has under his command many dukes, counts, and sub-servants, who, when called by the practitioner during this hour only, come in the service of Steganography. They deliver the secret committed to them, but they utterly refuse to come outside of their assigned hour. And note, that the dukes and primary princes of Samael are rarely sent by him into the service of this science, for they perform the office of stirring up magical and nocturnal illusions for men.

--- Page 95 ---
<meta>This page continues the catalog of spirits associated with the first hour of the day, specifically focusing on the "Counts" who serve under the Duke Samael. The previous page established that these names are used as a veil for a system of secret writing.</meta>

The **Counts**, however, along with their servants, are specifically assigned to the service of this art. They are frequently sent forth even without the presence of the dukes. Since they belong to the highest order among spirits, they are accustomed to showing themselves as somewhat proud and rebellious toward those who call upon them. They do not easily obey anyone—except those most skilled in the art of **Steganography** <term>from the Greek 'steganós' (hidden) and 'graphía' (writing); the art of secret communication</term>—without significant effort and danger. Indeed, they are insolent and very ready to deceive and mock men; they especially deride and sneer at those whom they find to be less than expert in the art of **Steganography**. However, those they perceive to be bold, constant, and experienced in the art itself, they respect and fear, and they always most promptly obey their commands with trembling and reverence. Therefore, we shall now record the names of those Counts who are necessary for us in the operations of **Steganography** at this hour.

| | | ℵ <note>The Hebrew letter Aleph, used here as a column header for numerical values.</note> | ℵ | ℵ | | |
| :--- | :--- | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
| *Ameniel* | *Bramiel* | 10 | 100 | 1000 | α | γ |
| *Charpon* | *Nestoriel* | 10 | 100 | 1000 | α | β |
| *Darosiel* | *Chremas* | 10 | 100 | 1000 | 10 | 20 |
| *Monasiel* | *Meresyn* | 10 | 100 | 1000 | 20 | 10 |

We now have four of the dukes of the great **Samael** <note>In Trithemius's system, Samael is the presiding "spirit" or chief key for the first hour of the day.</note> and the same number of Counts, with 4,440 servants from the triple order. Their service suffices us abundantly for every operation of the art of **Steganography** pertaining to the first hour of the day.

When, therefore, you wish to work in this art during the first hour of the day, first write the character of the ascending lord on a small scroll <note>original: "schedulam"</note>, then that of the moon; afterward, write those of all the remaining planets according to the usual order, and finally the character of the sign ascending at that same hour. When you have completed this, write this conjuration on the back of the same scroll in silence, keeping quiet.

## THE CONJURATION.

> **SAMAEL** afluar onayr mylco layr madiel cuhiel naniel nabruys fatiel atharbiel nadian naslon, ranyalcoha pemarson. <note>This string of "spirit names" serves as the cryptographic key or carrier text for the hidden message.</note>

Once this Conjuration has been written on the scroll, as previously described, you will arrange the letters to be sent—beautifully, clearly, and distinctly—to the person whom you wish to know the secret, in whatever form pleases you...

...it will suffice, because it does not matter what kind of letters are to be sent (provided they contain no secret within themselves). Then, once the letters have also been written, call one or two of the counts according to their order—if you are working many times in this same first hour of the day—and speak the prescribed conjuration from that same sheet with the strongest intention, fearing nothing. Once this is duly completed, the summoned spirits will immediately appear to you, visible in a familiar and customary form, calm, benevolent, and most ready for all services that occur. When they appear, entrust your secret to the principal one among them, saying: <term>Ameniel</term> <note>In Trithemius's system, these spirit names are often keys to a cipher or a memory palace.</note> I command you, by the occult power of this Conjuration, that you go as quickly as possible to **Albert Goëlei of Ravensburg, the governor in Kreuznach**, and say to him in my name thus and so. Having said these things, add the secret word, which must not be written down: and the spirit will fly away immediately. You should then send the letters, sealed with the character of <term>Samael</term> <note>Samael is the presiding intelligence of this hour; his "character" or seal serves as a signal to the recipient.</note> (as is the custom), by a messenger; these letters should contain no secret and need not fear anyone's inspection.

## Let the secret be of whatever kind occurs:

Whether it be a great secret or a difficult mystery, whatever occurs for the time and is of such a nature that it ought to be communicated to no one: it must not be entrusted to letters, nor to <term>notaries</term> <note>public officials authorized to draw up and attest to legal documents</note>. For in this art—especially regarding the spirits of this second volume—one must not operate for small or trivial causes which could otherwise be announced through ordinary letters.

# You shall fashion letters concerning these matters as you please.

<note>The following text is a "masking" letter. In Trithemius's steganography, the real message is hidden within this pious-sounding prose, often by taking the first letter of every word or every other word.</note>

*The MAJESTY of the Catholic faith, which must be firmly observed by all men, has promised the happiness of eternal life to those serving Christ: Hold fast to the Catholic Christian faith, glorify the Almighty, fleeing the tumult of vanity. No one of wicked works shall have a blessed life. Let the faithful man follow Christ along the path of virtues. Integrity is adorned by good works: and the Catholic name is preserved by Christian fidelity. For a wicked man obscures the faith. Christ hates the reckless, and loves Christians practicing Catholic operations, venerating tranquility.*

->*I, Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, published this on the Calends of April in the Year of our Lord 1,500.*<-

# STEGANOGRAPHY

...send those things which are to be sent first according to the general instruction of the art; and let him recite the prescribed conjuration in the manner and rite which we described earlier, written on the sheet with the <term>character of the ascending lord</term> <note>In astrology, the "lord of the ascendant" is the planet ruling the sign rising on the eastern horizon; here, it likely refers to a specific magical seal or symbol associated with the spirit of the hour.</note>, and the other things expressed. Then, as soon as he has completed the conjuration: <margin>These are red crosses.</margin> he will see the spirit present. Having seen him, let him say fearlessly: † penador auenal † solmenial phanu † fauear caschanti hernoty maduran. <note>These are "barbarous names of power," phonetic strings used in Renaissance magic that were believed to have inherent power regardless of their meaning.</note> Amen. These things being said, let him keep silent, and he will see the spirit approaching him and relay the entrusted secret into his ear, secretly and safely; let him listen to the spirit without fear and with great diligence, so that if anyone is sitting around him, he does not indicate the spirit's presence by either a nod or a word.

## CHAPTER II.

->*THE SECOND HOUR OF THE DAY IS CALLED CEVORYM. ITS*<-
->*prince and supreme spirit is Anael, who has under his command 10 Dukes,*<-
->*Counts, Centurions, and 100 Presiding Spirits. Of the servants and*<-
->*subservients, there is no certain number.*<-

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut initial 'P' depicting a figure, likely a scholar or monk, seated at a desk within an architectural interior, engaged in reading or writing.</image-desc>
The presiding ruler and Emperor of the second hour of the main day is called <term>Anael</term> <note>Often identified in medieval lore as the angel of Venus, associated here with the second hour.</note>, who has under his command 10 Dukes assigned to the service of this art of <term>Steganography</term> <note>Meaning "hidden writing," here referring to the practice of spirit communication used to send messages.</note>, along with Counts, Centurions, and 100 presiding officers over all his spirits; as of yet, I do not have a certain number for the servants. However, the second hour of the day is called <term>Ceuorym</term>, in which changeable operations are performed that are amazing to all men. All the spirits of this Emperor are quite benevolent, pleasant, cheerful, and most ready to obey the one working in the art: provided that he be skilled in the art, brave in spirit, constant, and of good understanding.

In this table, we have placed 3 of the Dukes of Anael, 6 of the Counts, and 330 of the servants, who are sufficient for us for every operation of Steganography in this second hour of the day, serving through their respective orders.

# BOOK TWO

...to work, write on a clean sheet the <term>character of the ascending lord</term> <note>In astrology, the "lord of the ascendant" is the planet ruling the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the operation.</note>, and according to the following order: the other planets and finally the sign of the 12th house, with <term>ink</term> <note>original: *encausto*; here referring to a special ceremonial ink prepared specifically for these steganographic arts.</note> made from the materials of this art. Then write on the back of the said sheet this conjuration in silence, with your face turned toward the sun.

->*Song of Conjuration*<-

**A**nael otiel aproisy rachymas, thulnear layr meuear theor cralnotiel amersoty mouear phroys lierto mear urnesa elty famelron: <note>These are "barbarous names of power," phonetic strings used in Renaissance magic that were believed to carry spiritual weight through sound rather than literal translation.</note>

With the conjuration written, do the other things which you know are to be done according to the art. Then say the Song in silence: and you will soon see the spirit present, cheerful, pleasant, and well-disposed to everything: to whom you may securely commit your secret.

->*We place any secret whatsoever.*<-

**L**et there be a secret of any kind occurring to us at the time: one which neither seems useful to be set down in letters nor to be committed to a notary. Let a leader and one companion, or another of them, be called from the spirits of Anael, as is available. Let the secret be committed to the principal spirit in the proper manner, just as is required according to the precept of the art: and he will carry it faithfully.

->*The tone of the letters should be familiar.*<-

### John Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, 
### to Wilhelm Veldick, Canon of St. Augustine 
### in the valley of Franconia, fellow philosopher, 
### greetings.

**I** *would rejoice to respond to your letters, so may Christ make me rejoice: and I would have sent the volumes you demanded, if I finally had them. For I have been zealous for the greatest and most serious occult writers with a good natural zeal, such as the most ancient philosophers possessed. Moreover, I give you everlasting thanks, my sweetest brother, for keeping the bond of our mutual friendship inviolate. For in this familiar business, which you seem to have managed most diligently with serious modesty, I clearly recognized your loyalty: a deed which I praise, and for which I have thanks that shall never die. Therefore, I would most desire to comply with your petitions, if (Christ knows) I had the things you requested. You have recently made various translations; I saw the Latin ones: of the most excellent Pico della Mirandola...* <note>Count Giovanni Pico della Mirandola (1463–1494) was a famous Italian Renaissance philosopher known for his "900 Theses" on magic, Kabbalah, and philosophy.</note>

*...of most learned men, I have read many most brilliant volumes. I have seen Hippolytus in Greek; whether he has been fully translated, I do not know. The highest Truth and Christ know—from whom no shadows hide anything—that I desire to comply with your request from my heart. I ask you, be zealous for virtue with a zeal for righteousness: despise the honors and praises that will perish. Consider true praise to follow in the imitation of Christ. Do this, I pray, so that you may stand as a priest in your works, and love Christ for all the time you live. A true lover of Christ burns with zeal for justice. True rest for the soul, and knowledge of the Savior and of the pious observer of Catholic tradition, prepares eternal glory for all. May Jesus Christ, the glorious giver of all good things, grant you the love of eternity, making you a faithful priest of Christians. Farewell. March 29, in the year 1500.* <note>original: "4. Cal. Ap. Anno M. D." The Roman dating refers to the 4th day before the Calends of April.</note>

When the person to whom the letters were sent receives them—provided he is skilled in this art—having first performed the general preliminaries, let him arrange the <term>Lord of the Ascendant</term> <note>In Renaissance astrology, the planet that rules the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the specific time of the ritual.</note> with the others on the chart as is customary. Let him speak the prescribed conjuration. Once spoken, the sent spirit will appear visibly; turning toward him, let the practitioner say fearlessly: <term>Fabelmerusyn passoriel liertos ryneas melchus thyrmo nydran vear padroys</term> <note>These are "barbarous names," phonetic vocalizations intended to command the spirit to reveal the hidden message. They do not have a literal translation but function as a sonic key.</note>. With this song completed, the spirit will soon approach mildly and will faithfully reveal the mystery committed to him. However, let every practitioner in this art beware—both the one who sends the spirit and the one for whom it was intended—that they do not presume to work anything in public or among others who are not skilled in this art, because all hourly spirits love secrecy and detest the public; and they would not depart without danger.

...of the one calling, they are least able to resist. For they love secrecy, just like all others who preside over the hours and their operations. I do not know the names of all of them, but only a few, which, having been revealed by the spirits themselves, are reduced into this table and suffice for us.

| **R.** | | **R.** | **R.** | | **R.** | |

<note>original: "R." This likely stands for Ratio (account/proportion) or is a placeholder for the numeric values assigned to these spirit names for cryptographic purposes.</note>

In this table, we have named 4 of the <term>leaders</term> <note>original: "ducibus." In Trithemius's system, spirits are organized into a pseudo-feudal hierarchy of Dukes, Counts, and Servants.</note>, 8 of the <term>counts</term> <note>original: "comitibus."</note>, and of their <term>servants</term> <note>original: "seruitoribus."</note> an uncounted 1730, who suffice for us for all operations of this art in the third hour of the day. Therefore, when you wish in this hour to call a Spirit for carrying some message to a friend: let no fewer than at least two leaders be called, and the same number of counts with their servants. If, however, you wish to call more or fewer, you may indeed do so, but it will be necessary to have a conjuration to the universal emperor, without whose command the Spirits do not violate their order. Therefore, write upon a new piece of paper the character of the <term>Lord of the Ascendant</term> <note>The planet ruling the zodiac sign rising on the horizon at the time of the operation.</note>, and the others according to the order of the planets, with the sign of the <term>twelfth house</term>, in the manner which the institution of the art requires. On the back of the paper, write this Conjuration.

### *The Conjuration.*

*Vequaniel, odiel mefrij reuoiij sotiel mear iamy otiel aslofian yrfoti breotion drearij fabel merusin.* <note>While appearing to be "barbarous names" of spirits, these strings of words are actually a steganographic cipher. The first and last letters of each word, or specific vowels, would reveal the hidden plaintext message.</note>

The Conjuration having been written and those things which are required by the art having been added; say it in a low voice, lest it be heard by anyone: and immediately the called spirits will come, whom when you see, entrust the secret to the more powerful one, whom you will easily recognize by his clothing: because always, he who is first among the other spirits appears crowned.

### *Let the secret be of whatever kind.*

Let the secret to an absent friend be of whatever kind, which should not be committed to letters, lest they be read by someone, nor to a <term>messenger</term> <note>original: "tabellioni." A professional courier or notary.</note>, lest by any occasion or price he be corrupted, or out of fear betray you publicly. Call the spirit assigned to the hour, entrust the secret to him, fearing nothing: he will carry it faithfully.
