<header>OF STEGANOGRAPHY <note>From the Greek *steganographia*, meaning "secret writing." Trithemius uses the guise of magic to teach methods of cryptography.</note>. You shall form the letters as you please.</header>

## Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, to Arnold Bostius, Carmelite of Ghent, fellow philosopher, greetings and love.

*Since I have known you to be a lover of the humanities, a devoted worshiper of the Christian faith, and a great patron of all philosophers, I gladly grant your requests, led by the wonderful pleasure of our friendship. Behold, I am sending you the aforementioned book on the Ecclesiastical writers and Catholic doctors at your urging, so that you may have something with which to delight your mind. However, please send back to me the Codex of Hermes <note>original: "Codicem autem Hermetis." Likely referring to the *Corpus Hermeticum*, a collection of texts on philosophy and magic attributed to Hermes Trismegistus.</note>, and the books of Dionysius <note>original: "libros Dionysii." Referring to Pseudo-Dionysius the Areopagite, whose mystical theology heavily influenced medieval angelology.</note> on Spirits, and the great work of Euencius on the winter questions. May Christ deign to adorn the eyes of our heart with the most fervent zeal of his love: and make us have a love for heavenly honors and, stripped of lowest desires, teach us to despise vain honors, so that we may be able to penetrate the sublime things of art and the great secrets of nature, and that we may have a continuous desire for Philosophy, to purify our affections, by which we may always adorn the Catholic faith with most honorable character. Hold fast to the great word: you are a philosopher, leave all things and you shall find: suspect the hour of death to be near: so that you may meet it prepared. Farewell. March 30, 1500 <note>original: "3. Cal. Apr. M. D."</note>.*

When you receive any letters sealed with the character of Vequaniel <note>A spirit governing the previous hour mentioned in the text.</note>; write the figure of the <term>Ascendant Lord</term> <note>original: "domini ascendentis." A term from astrology referring to the planet ruling the sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific moment.</note>, and the 12th house with the others which are required for the art on a new parchment, and on its back the aforementioned conjuration; and immediately the sent Spirit will appear visible; as soon as you see him, speak this song.

### Song of Conjuration.

**Fameron aprois liernoti stadiucar diuiel fauean Lamerly.** <note>These are "barbarous names of power," characteristic of medieval grimoires. They often contain corrupted fragments of Greek, Hebrew, or Latin, or are entirely constructed for cryptographic purposes.</note> These things having been said, receive the word from the mouth of the Spirit without fear, because he is faithful enough. And take care, lest you perform this in public before others.

->*supreme Spirit of the hour is called Vathmiel, who has under his Command*<-
->*10 leaders, 100 counts, the number of servants is not certain,*<-
->*through whom the operation is performed in the fourth hour.*<-

<header>BOOK TWO,</header>

<image-desc>A decorative square woodcut initial 'Q'. Inside the letter, two figures are depicted: one appears to be a seated scholar or authority figure, and the other stands before him, possibly a student or messenger, in a room with architectural details.</image-desc>

**T**he fourth hour of the day is called Elechym <note>Likely derived from Hebrew *Elohim*, though Trithemius uses it as a temporal name.</note>, whose supreme angel is named Vathmiel. He has under his command ten most renowned leaders <note>original: "duces"</note> appointed to this art of Steganography <term>Steganography: the art of hidden writing. In this book, Trithemius presents it as "angel magic" to disguise its true nature as cryptography.</term>, as well as one hundred counts <note>original: "comites"</note>; the number of servants is infinite. They have the duty of reporting all secrets generally: and they are very good, benevolent, and obedient to all things, whatever they are commanded. However, being lovers of silence, they greatly detest the noise of men. Therefore, take care that you do not attempt to perform any work through them while situated among others who are not skilled in this art: for the conjured spirits would not easily approach, and they would not depart without danger to you. Observe all things that we command you, and you shall have great progress in this science.

| L. <note>L. for Leader (original "D." for "Dux")</note> | L. | L. | L. | L. | L. | L. |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| *Ammyel* | *Emarfiel* | *Iermiel* | **10** | **100** | **100** | **100** |
| *Larmich* | *Permiel* | *Thuroz* | **10** | **200** | **150** | **160** |
| *Masfiel* | *Queriel* | *Vanisiel* | **30** | **200** | **100** | **200** |
| *Ormyel* | *Strubiel* | *Zasuiel* | **10** | **300** | **100** | **160** |
| *Zardiel* | *Diuiel* | *Hermiel* | **10** | **200** | **180** | **100** |

We have in this table five of the leaders of Vathmiel, ten of the leaders indeed, and 1,550 in the number of servants, whose service is sufficient for us for every operation of Steganography during the fourth hour. When, therefore, you wish to work in this hour: write the character <note>A magical symbol or astrological sign.</note> of the ascending lord and of the twelfth house with the others on a new piece of parchment, and on its back, write that Conjuration <note>original: "Coniurationem"</note>, as is appropriate.

->The Conjuration.<-

**V**athmiel, to the matters <note>The following string is a cipher disguised as a magical incantation: "rhearefo raser theotij venosi fayr fatiel casa irfoti verotiel does ro fabel merusin." To a casual observer, it looks like a spell; to an initiate, it contains a hidden message.</note> rhearefo raser theotij venosi fayr fatiel casa irfoti verotiel does ro fabel merusin. **I**f there is a secret of any kind, great and very hidden, which you wish no one at all to know except one person: Call the spirits appointed to the hour, as many as you wish, and entrust the secret to the more principal one. Write [the letters...]

->Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim of the Order of Saint Benedict, to Rutger Vencaius the Sicambrian, <note>Sicambri was a traditional name for people from the region of Cleves.</note> Canon of the Order of the Divine Father Augustine of the Monastery of Gaesdonck, <note>original: "Dametenſis"</note> Greetings.<-

*Those things which you proposed for me to solve, I have resolved, with Christ's help. Whether well or not, you who proposed them shall bring the judgment. I will report to you briefly a stupendous thing that occurred. A certain brother, an old friend and not the least among them, recently told me that a youth was raised from the dead at the invocation of Christ; he reported wonderful and horrible things among those in the underworld, telling of the torments of wicked Christians: namely those who crucified Christ by their perverse actions. He said they were especially guilty because, having forgotten the most kind benefit of redemption, they remained always ungrateful to Christ. He said those who were known to be ungrateful for his favors by not living well suffer horrible things; blinded by sins, they neglected future joys, following the pleasures of the world. Those who were biting with remorse for their committed negligences cried out most bitterly: "We rightly suffer these things, we who were always ungrateful to the redeemer of the human race—Christian in name, but pagan in action. Wretched, we always sought earthly consolations; therefore we receive the deserved retribution of eternal damnation. Alas for us, most unhappy! Why did we offend Christ? Why did we despise the time granted to us?" The resurrected youth told us many other things, which I am now entirely unable to write, being greatly hindered by heavy business. Therefore, brother, let us zealously imitate the footsteps of Christ, always giving thanks to our most mild Savior: praying that he might pardon us for the crimes we have committed. Farewell. March 30th.* <note>original: "3. Calend. April."</note>

When you, who are skilled in this art, receive the letter to which it is sent, observe the lord of the ascendant of that same hour, and the sign of the twelfth house: <note>Trithemius uses astrological terminology here to mask the cryptographic key—likely referring to specific positions or letters in the text.</note> and write their characters with the others on paper, along with other things required for the operation; and say afterward the aforementioned conjuration, and upon seeing the spirit, these words: <term>Cameruſin aproyſi liertothulnear venean maueas fabelron.</term> <note>These "spirit names" are actually a null cipher or a series of keywords where specific letters (often the first or last of each word) reveal the hidden message.</note> Once these things are said, the sent spirit will approach: and he will reveal the secret entrusted to him faithfully and without danger.

<header>BOOK TWO.</header>

### CHAP. V.

*THE FIFTH HOUR IS CALLED FEALECH, AND THE SPIRIT*
*and supreme Commander is named Sasquiel, having under his command 10 dukes,*
*100 counts, and a vast number of servants.*

THE fifth hour of the day is called Fealech, and its spirit is named Sasquiel, a great and powerful Commander, who has under his command, among many others, 10 dukes and 100 counts, along with their attendants. These are assigned to the service of various and highly secret arts, especially this science of ours <note>The "science" refers to Steganography, the art of hiding messages.</note>. It should be noted that the dukes are rarely summoned for operations of Steganography during the fifth hour, as they are not always necessary; the counts, together with the servants assigned to them, are more than sufficient for every operation we require. If, however, anyone wishes to see some of the dukes or utilize their power, he may summon one, two, or more; they will arrive without delay, being quite benevolent and ready to obey those performing the work.

| **D.** | **C.** | **S.** | **n.** | **r.** | **x.** |
|----------|----------|----------|----|-----|------|
| Damiel | Iameriel | Omerach | 10 | 100 | 1000 |
| Araniel | Futiniel | Lameros | 10 | 100 | 1000 |
| Maroch | Rameriel | Zachiel | 10 | 100 | 1000 |
| Sarapiel | Amisiel | Fustiel | 10 | 100 | 1000 |
| Putifiel | Vraniel | Camiel | 10 | 100 | 1000 |
<note>The table headers in the original use Gothic initials: D for Duces (Dukes), I for Iudices or Comites (Counts), and O for Officiales (Servants). The numbers likely refer to the hierarchies of spirits under each name.</note>

From the dukes of Sasquiel, we have already named five; from the counts, ten; and from the number of servants, 5,550. Their service is sufficient for every operation of the present hour. Therefore, when you wish to work through any of the aforementioned spirits in Steganography during the fifth hour of the day: write the character of the ascending lord, the twelfth house, <note>These are astrological terms used here as part of the "ritual" masking the cryptographic key.</note> and the other things required by the art on a clean piece of paper; and on its back, write this Conjuration in the manner you already know.

->Conjuration.<-

Sasquiel adrés rhetroleti rosiel emelto fatu olmeniel it soti sauearnauediel liernoti chame son. <note>This "conjuration" is a series of null words or a cipher key. To the uninitiated, it looks like a magical incantation, but to the recipient, it provides the instructions for decoding the hidden message.</note> When these things are finished and the other necessary items are added, speak the conjuration itself in the proper manner; you will immediately see the spirit you called visibly present, to whom you may safely entrust your secret.

<header>STEGANOGRAPHY</header>

### The Method of Communicating a Secret.

If we have any secret at all for a friend, which we wish no one but him to know, and it is of such a nature that it cannot safely be committed to letters or to couriers: let us call one of the counts of Sasquiel mentioned above <note>In Trithemius's system, "calling a count" means selecting a specific variation of the cipher code associated with this hour.</note>; let us commit the secret to him, and attach letters signed with his character <note>original: "charactere"; here referring to a cryptographic symbol or signature that tells the recipient which decryption key to use.</note>: and we shall be safe.

### The General Content of the Letter, as desired.

## Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, to his religious brother, Nicholas Baselius of Bad Dürkheim, Monk of Hirsau, Greetings.

*You neglect holy obedience and humility, dear brother; you undermine the constitutions of Benedict. Through the reports of everyone arriving here in succession, I have learned that you hold good zeal in hatred: you despise the rule of Religion and Catholic obedience: you are occupied with secular works and arts. Dear brother, what are you doing? You have disturbed us: you favor double-tongued fools: you love hatred, bitterness, quarrels, and contentions; you hate goodness, you are double-tongued, contentious, proud. Fool, what are you doing? The time is at hand for zealously correcting these negligences of yours according to justice; unless you run humbly to us to break the spirit of bitterness with the whips of humility: we are compelled by our office to act against everything that is seditious to the equality of faith and Catholic morals. Farewell from Sponheim, the day before the Calends of April, in the year 1500.*

When he to whom the letters are sent, being skilled in this art, receives them: having recognized the character of Sasquiel, and after the usual preliminaries, let him say the aforementioned conjuration <note>The "conjuration" is a metaphor for the recipient applying the decryption rules to the text.</note>: and immediately the sent Spirit will appear and show himself visibly. Having seen him, let the recipient quickly say these words: **Fabelmerufyn aueual vear plyan cralti penason.** <note>These "magical" words likely serve as a secondary key or a null-string used to identify the start of a hidden sequence within the text.</note> Upon hearing these words, the Spirit will approach and reveal the secret.

<header>BOOK TWO</header>

The sixth hour of the day is called **Genapherym**, and its angel is **Saniel**, great and powerful in his operations. He has under his command 10 dukes <term>duces; high-ranking spirit-chiefs in the cryptographic system</term>, 100 counts, and 5,550 servants assigned to the service of various tasks. These are sufficient for everything we might wish to perform through Steganography <term>steganographiam; Trithemius’s term for secret writing, literally "hidden writing"</term> during this hour.

However, the dukes are rarely summoned, even though they are prompt and very willing to obey our commands. This is because their counts are accustomed to performing most of their duties, as the dukes are often occupied with other magical experiments <note>In the context of this book, "magical experiments" is a veil for complex cryptographic calculations or alternative cipher systems.</note>.

| N. | R. | | R. | R. | N. |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |

| Charuch | Ienotriel | Permon | 10 | 100 | 110 |
| Medasiel | Sameon | Brasiel | 10 | 100 | 100 |
| Nathmiel | Trasiel | Camosiel | 10 | 100 | 100 |
| Pemiel | Xamyon | Euadar | 10 | 100 | 110 |

We have named here 5 of Samiel’s dukes, 10 of his counts, and 5,550 from the number of servants, through whom the work of Steganography is done in the sixth hour of the day. Therefore, when you wish to work through these Spirits, let it be done entirely in a secret place. If you wish to call one or more dukes, it is in your power, although it is not necessary, because the counts are sufficient to convey everything to us. Therefore, write on a clean piece of paper <note>original: "Chartula munda"</note> the customary characters of the planets and signs, and on the back of it, the conjuration:

## Conjuration.

> Samiel afiel thebrean pothijr ersoty mear pornys layr moas, famerofyn. <note>While this sounds like a magical incantation, in Trithemius's system, these specific strings of words often act as a key or a template for the hidden message.</note> Then, after performing the necessary preliminaries <note>original: "præmissis præmittendis"; a common Latin shorthand for standard opening rituals or procedures</note>, recite the same conjuration in the rite you know. Immediately, the summoned Spirit will be present, prompt and eager for everything, whom you may send far or near to a friend to carry the secret.

## The Nature of the Secret.

Whatever sort of secret you have, you can securely send it through the Spirits of Samiel in the sixth hour of the day to anyone skilled in the art of Steganography. This is because for the purpose of conveying all things, they are general and in...

## The form of the letter, as you wish.

*By praying with the most humble lamentations, we shall obtain the grace of forgiveness. And with groans, we shall receive freedom from our transgressions. Men who are constantly repentant will deservedly perceive joy: adorning Catholic piety with works of justice, they will hold the law of kindness for Catholic praise. The most pious Savior will free everyone and forgive the sins of those desiring indulgence and loving the path of the Catholic religion: they will perceive the joys of blessedness. There is good power in the path of freedom for those who keep the law; they will triumph gloriously and obtain bright blessedness by the light of the Trinity. Written in the year 1500, on the Kalends of April <note>April 1st</note>, by Johannes Trithemius, Abbot.*

When the one to whom these letters are sent—being skilled in this art—receives them, and after the necessary preparations are made, let him say the conjuration; and soon the sent Spirit <note>In Trithemius's system, the "Spirit" is a metaphor for the recipient's understanding of the cipher key. "Appearing" refers to the hidden message becoming clear.</note> will appear. When he sees the Spirit present, let him say these words:

> Charmes pormeniel vear safeuij liernoty noty ersy melron. <note>This "magical" phrase is likely a phonetic key or a specific instruction for extracting letters from the cover text above.</note>

Once these things are said, as prescribed in the art, the Spirit will approach closer and will faithfully and secretly reveal the secret committed to him.

## CHAPTER VII.

->WHOSE HOUR IS CALLED HAMARYM, AND ITS SPI-<-
->rit is Barquiel, having under his command 10 dukes and 100 counts;<-
->the number of his servants is not certain to me.<-

The seventh hour of the day is called **Hamarym**, and its angel <note>original: "angelus"</note> is named **Barquiel**. He has under his command 10 dukes <term>duces; high-ranking spirits in the system</term>, 100 counts, and a great and uncertain number of servants. The ministries of all these, by their turns and orders, are abundantly sufficient for us for every operation of Steganography <term>steganographiæ; secret writing</term> in the seventh hour of the day. 

Now, when you wish to work through the spirits of the aforementioned commander in the seventh hour, do not call fewer than at least one duke and one count, who have servants assigned to them, each in his own order. So that you may have at hand the names of some of the dukes and counts through whom you may work, attend to this table:

| Name | | Name | | Number | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Abrasiel | Harmiel | Pasriel | 10 | 10 | 100 |

# BOOK TWO.

Here you now have named 5 leaders <note>Latin: *ducibus*, often translated as dukes in a celestial hierarchy</note>, 10 companions <note>Latin: *comitibus*, or counts</note>, and numbered from the order of servants, 600. The leaders and companions are called by name, and they are quite ready to obey the one calling in all things which pertain to the ministry of this art. Therefore, when you wish to operate through them; write on clean paper the characters of the lord of the ascending 12th house <note>An astrological reference used here as a component of the encryption key</note> with others to be written according to custom; and on its back, you should write that conjuration according to the precepts of the art, as you know.

### The Conjuration.

> LAmedon mosco vrsoty tharuean: dayr Lays emel thebrean rafoty bamerfon. <note>This "conjuration" is a phonetic cipher. When decrypted using Trithemius's methods, it reveals a hidden message rather than a magical spell.</note>

Then, having joined those things which are to be joined according to the custom of the art, say the conjuration itself with a strong intention, as is proper: and immediately the called Spirits will appear visibly <note>This is Trithemius's metaphor for the hidden message becoming legible to the recipient who knows the key.</note>. You should commit the secret not to a companion but to a leader, unless perhaps you called a companion without a leader; which, although I do not deny it can be done, nevertheless, according to the precept of the art, at least one leader is to be called with whatever companions you wish.

### An argument for the secret, as you wish.

Let your secret be whatever it may be to a friend, which you wish to be shared with no other. Therefore, neither letters nor messengers <note>Latin: *tabellionibus*, refers to those who carry or record tablets/letters</note> are to be trusted: because fraud can intervene on both sides. Call from the above-mentioned Spirits as many as you wish: commit the secret to the stronger one, fearing nothing, because they are good and faithful in all things.

### The form of the letter may be as you please.

## Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, to the vigorous soldier Henry 
## of Bunau, a most studious fellow-philosopher, 
## sends Greetings.

I had lent my books to you on the condition of their being restored, most dear soldier Bunau: but behold, you have by no means restored those which you should have sent back in a short time. You act against the law which the Catholic faith has ordained concerning a promise: do not delay,

*...you should not delay in fulfilling what you promised to all: it does not befit a good and honest man, born of noble parents and ancestors, to violate a promise. For you promised to restore [the books] most diligently in a short time. It is a sign of fickleness to defer a promise, as they say, to the "Greek Calends" <note>An idiom meaning "never," as the Greek calendar had no Calends.</note>. Have I not waited long enough with great goodwill? It is most shameful to promise the greatest things while returning the least. Good faith adorns a soldier. What greater crime is there than to deny a promised loan? It does not befit a Christian to be unwilling to keep an agreement. Moreover, the constitution of Christian law commands us to keep a promise, to return what is borrowed: and what you wish to be done for you, you ought also to do gladly for others. I demand my books, which I had lent you at your request. Dismiss those long excuses which you promise for the Greek Calends. You hold my mind with these brief words. Consider your name and the honorable lineage of the Bunau family <note>original: "Bunawensiu~"; likely referring to the noble Saxon family von Bünau.</note>: and return my volumes to me. Farewell from Sponheim, April 2nd, in the year of our Lord 1500.*

*When you have received these letters or any others from him who sent the spirit to you: once the sign of Barquiel <note>The spirit of the 7th hour discussed on the previous page.</note> is recognized, observe the lord of the ascendant <note>A term from astrology referring to the planet ruling the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific time.</note> at that same hour and write its character with the others upon clean paper: and you shall read the conjuration written on the back secretly, as is proper: and immediately the spirit sent will appear; as soon as you see him, say these words to him.*

> *Mefarym burne theor alucas casuean cralti lierto aply charmoys.* <note>Like previous "conjurations," this is a phonetic cipher. To the uninitiated, it looks like a magical incantation; to the recipient, it contains the hidden message.</note>

*When these things have been said, the spirit sent will come closer and faithfully reveal to you the secret committed to him. But see that you are brave, constant, and undaunted in all things.*

### BOOK TWO.

...obedient and subject to Madael <note>A variation of Osmadiel, the presiding spirit of the eighth hour.</note>, the dukes and counts are accustomed to come in various transformations at the whim of the practitioner, just as they have been commanded by the practitioner. But now let us set down the names of some of them.

We now have five dukes and ten counts named in the table, who are sufficient for us for every operation of this hour. When you wish to work through them in Steganography <term>Steganography: Trithemius’s term for his system of hidden writing, which he disguised as "angel magic" to protect the secret from the uninitiated</term>: write on a clean piece of paper with the customary characters and conjurations: and first having added those things which are to be added, speak the written conjuration itself, which is as follows:

->Conjuration.<-

> Osmodael ancor erfoty neas, hays layr Caphrayn thelreas mear pensarsy. <note>While this appears to be a magical incantation, in Trithemius’s system, these strings of words are actually an encrypted message where specific letters or positions convey the hidden meaning.</note>

Once this conjuration song has been spoken, the called spirits will be present, prompt and obedient in all things. You should entrust the secret <term>Arcanum: a secret or mystery</term> to a duke, not a count, unless the duke is absent: because the business should always be commended to the more principal of those intervening.

->A subject of a secret at your pleasure.<-

Let us have some secret or other (which we wish a far-distant friend to know) and of such a kind that we do not want it to come to the notice of anyone else; and this can often happen. Therefore, lest the messenger reveal the mystery on any occasion, or the letters fall into foreign hands, let us work, and through the spirit we shall be secure.

->Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim of the Order of Saint<-
->Benedict, to Lord Conrad, Abbot of Saint<-
->Stephen in Würzburg <note>original: "Herbipolensi"</note>, sends greetings.<-

A rumor has gone out concerning the Abbot of Limpurg, that he has dismissed Brother Johann of Feilsdorf, a faithful man and most skilled in worldly affairs...

->STEGANOGRAPHIA<-

...then: and he has introduced new **Pantimoran** monks <note>original: "Pantimoranos." This is likely an invented name used by Trithemius to sound like a monastic order while serving the underlying cipher.</note>, and appointed them as his own officials. I wonder greatly at such a useless deed. Yet when I recall the custom of the **Limburgers** <note>original: "Lympurgenfium," referring to the monks of the Limburg Abbey.</note>, I know not what to say. For they are ignorant of temporal matters; and so they dismissed a faithful man because he rebuked their deeds. They should have given thanks to our Savior Jesus Christ for the faithfulness of such a brother, who could have revealed their needs to them. Wretches, why do you repel a good and necessary helper? What shall I say? I do not praise the departure of so useful a brother, most skilled in temporal affairs, who would have come to the aid of the Limburgers as quickly as possible. I grieve vehemently that the Limburg monks have been so foolish as to dismiss an excellent man, and have instead appointed as Stewards monks who are worth nothing at all in temporal matters. **Abbot Macharius**, it was not becoming for a man to act thus. First you praised him, and now you have repelled a man so necessary to you, one who should be second to none. I confess, if Christ had given him to me, as becomes a Christian, I would now give the greatest thanks, and I would take care that he never departed. But you, ungrateful to Christ, preferred to dismiss a faithful man—exhausted by the foolish counsel of the wicked—lest there should seem to be any hope of recovery for your abandoned affairs. 

From Sponheim, April 3rd, 1500. <note>original: "Ex Spanh. 3. Non. April. M.D." Trithemius was the Abbot of Sponheim at this time.</note>

When he has received these letters or any others marked with the sign of **Osmadael**, he who is skilled in this art to whom they are sent—having performed the necessary preliminaries—should speak the prescribed conjuration, and soon the sent spirit will appear visibly. When you have seen him, say:

> Menafon aproysy elmano thulneas assierto mauear veneas cralnoti permafon. <note>This string of nonsense words is a cipher. In Trithemius's system, the recipient would extract the hidden message by looking at specific letters within these words.</note>

With these things said, the messenger spirit will manifest the secret <term>Arcanum: a hidden secret or mystery</term> to you.

# CHAPTER IX.

## THE HOUR OF WHICH IS CALLED KARRON, AND THE name of its supreme Angel is Quabriel, having under his command 10 dukes, 100 counts, and the number of servants is not certain.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut drop cap 'N' depicting a landscape with a figure standing near a building.</image-desc>

**N**ine is the hour of the day called **Karron**, and its principal spirit is named **Quabriel**. He has under his command many and nearly innumerable dukes, counts, and servants. From among these, 10 of the dukes and 100 of the counts have been assigned to the ministry of this our art of Steganography <term>Steganography: From the Greek 'steganos' (hidden) and 'graphein' (to write). Trithemius uses the guise of angel magic to teach complex cryptography.</term>, who in turn have many servants assigned to them in various ranks. In the first rank...

In the second [order] 20; in the third 30; in the fourth 40; in the fifth 500; in the sixth 600; in the seventh 700; in the eighth 1,080; in the ninth 90,000; and in the tenth there are 100,000. <note>These numbers represent the vast hierarchy of spirits that Trithemius uses to categorize his cryptographic keys.</note> But rarely are spirits summoned through all the orders; and if they should be summoned, not all, but only some, are accustomed to come with their <term>dukes<gloss>duces</gloss></term> and <term>counts<gloss>comites</gloss></term> according to the command of the Emperor. Now, Raziel the Great said in his book *On Illusions and Transformations* <note>original: "de præstigiis & transformationibus." ### Purpose of the Secret.

Let us have some secret or other for a friend: which we wish to be most hidden, and in no way revealed to anyone except him. Let us therefore call one or two from the aforementioned dukes and counts, according to the instruction of the art, and let us entrust the secret to the more powerful one...

# John Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, of the Religious Order of St. Benedict, to the Religious Father, John Hugonis, Guardian <note>The superior or head of a Franciscan monastery.</note> of the Friars Minor of Saint Francis in Bad Kreuznach, Greetings and charity.

->*Let us form letters as we please.*<-

*Religious and venerable father, I know that you freely grant all fitting things to your friends: therefore I humbly pray that, out of regard for Catholic piety, you do not take it poorly that your zealous brothers have been detained with us for some time with the greatest charity: for it was necessary for them to stay at my entreaty. It was a glorious joy for us: all of us rejoicing without sin. We exulted in the Lord with spiritual joy, giving thanks to Almighty God with good zeal. What, I ask, is more religious than such a gathering? Where nothing idle is said, nothing wicked is desired, may Jesus Christ the Savior of all make us rejoice in eternal glory: Glorious zealots of virtues prefer nothing to the truth. We rejoiced with holy joy, praying that you receive the aforementioned brothers peacefully, and lay aside all zeal of bitterness. Farewell from Sponheim, April 3rd <note>original: "3. Non. April." — The 3rd day before the Nones of April.</note> in the year of our Lord 1500.*

When he who is skilled in this art receives these letters, or any others marked with the character of Quabriel <note>This "letter" is actually a mask for a hidden message. To the uninitiated, it looks like a polite note between clergymen, but to the recipient, it contains a secret message encoded through the vocabulary.</note>: having performed the necessary preliminaries, let him say the prescribed conjuration: which being said, he will soon see the designated Spirit, and as soon as he sees him, let him say these words:
> Meffraym paslotiel vear reneam cralty thio phrozsy ma meat hamorsy. <note>While presented as a magical incantation to summon a spirit, these nonsensical words are actually a second layer of ciphertext or a key to the preceding letter.</note>

These things having been said, the sent Spirit, approaching calmly, will tell you the entrusted secret.

### CHAPTER X.

## WHOSE HOUR IS CALLED LAMARHON, AND its supreme Emperor Spirit is called Oriel, who has under his command many <term>dukes<gloss>duces</gloss></term>, <term>counts<gloss>comites</gloss></term>, and many servants.

->Conjuration<-

->The Constitution of the Secret<-

**I**f we have a friend whom we favor with good will, and we fear he might be supplanted or circumvented by some vagabond <note>original: *circumcellione* — historically a group of wandering schismatics, used here to mean a deceptive person or a "shyster."</note>, we therefore desire for him to be advised. We cannot do so with open words through messengers; nor do we dare to do so through letters, lest they fall into someone’s hands. Therefore, so that we may be safe, and our secret may always remain hidden: let us call at least one spirit from the aforementioned dukes. He comes, obeys, and carries the message faithfully.

# Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, to Henry of Bunau, Knight of the Golden Spur, distinguished Philosopher and Mathematician, sends greetings.

*Since you are most studious of the humanities, you will, I think, rejoice greatly to have found a new companion in your studies. The man who will present this letter to you is a man of great knowledge, a supreme philosopher, a most subtle arithmetician, a most eloquent rhetorician, a most profound mathematician, and excellently learned in every way. He speaks most elegantly, shining with Ciceronian eloquence <note>original: "Tulliana" — referring to Marcus Tullius Cicero, the Roman standard for perfect Latin rhetoric.</note>. Unless I were your greatest friend, I would have kept such a great man entirely for myself, for in using his company, I have experienced him to be a most learned philosopher. I favor you greatly; therefore, receive this philosopher with gratitude, whom I send to you led by singular love. Oh, would that all our students were such as he, who with a timid heart despise noble science. This man assiduously examines the study of philosophy and other sciences, and the highest depths of nature; he continually treats them and penetrates them most sharply. Therefore, Henry Bunau, remember to treat the man with a liberal countenance for the sake of Christ. Farewell from Sponheim, November 2, in the year of our Lord 1500.*

<note>This letter is a classic example of a Trithemian cipher. To the casual observer, it is a recommendation for a traveling scholar. However, to the initiated recipient, the secret message is hidden within the structure of the words themselves.</note>

When the friend to whom these letters are sent, being skilled in this science of Steganography <note>The art of hidden writing.</note>, receives them: let him do all things which are to be done according to the art, and add the prescribed conjuration, as is the custom. And when he sees the sent Spirit present, let him say these words to him, directed to the more powerful among the Spirits:

> Camyn aparsy aslotiel omear reneas vean triamy cralty penason. 

<note>While this sounds like a magical incantation, in the context of Trithemius’s work, these "spirit names" and "conjurations" often serve as further layers of cryptographic keys or instructions for decoding the letter above.</note>

When these words are spoken, the Spirit will immediately come closer and reveal the secret to him.

## CHAPTER XI.

### WHOSE HOUR IS CALLED MANELOYM, AND ITS SUPREME COMMANDER IS CALLED BARIEL, WHO HAS UNDER HIS DOMINION MANY PRINCES, DUKES, COUNTS, AND SERVANTS.

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut initial 'V' featuring a seated figure, likely a scholar or cleric, in a landscape setting with trees and hills.</image-desc>

THE ELEVENTH hour of the day is called Maneloym, and its supreme angel-commander is called Bariel, who, as Hermes the Jew <note>A legendary figure often cited in medieval and Renaissance occult texts, sometimes confused with or related to Hermes Trismegistus.</note> writes, has many spirits under his command. Through these spirits, wonders are performed in the eleventh hour of the day in various experiments. From among these, some are assigned to the operation of Stegano-

The practitioners of Steganography <note>In Trithemius's work, "Steganography" refers to a system of secret communication disguised as a system of spirit summoning.</note> are many, organized according to their ranks. In the first rank there are 10, in the second 20, in the third 30, in the fourth 40, in the fifth 500, in the sixth 600, in the seventh 700, in the eighth 800, in the ninth 900, and in the tenth 1,000. All these are sent into the service of this art in their turns and ranks; among them are dukes and counts with their servants, very many indeed, whose names are not easy to list, nor is it even necessary. It is enough to know the names of a few, for an operator in this art can always learn more names of others from the report of a single spirit. And note that, according to Raziel <note>Raziel refers to the archangel who, in Jewish mysticism and medieval grimoires like the *Sefer Raziel HaMalakh*, was the keeper of divine secrets and the author of a book of magic given to Adam.</note>, all these spirits shape and transform themselves according to the will of the practitioner. Thus, in whatever form he wishes to see them, let him command them before the Conjuration in the manner you have been taught, and they will obey him most promptly in all things. But let us now set forth enough of their names for our purposes.

Here we now have 15 named spirits from among those belonging to Bariel, of whom five are dukes and ten are counts; they have a total of 1,100 servants, which are sufficient for our operations during the eleventh hour of the day in Steganography. Therefore, when you wish to work through these spirits, observe the lord of the ascendant <note>A term from astrology referring to the planet that rules the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the start of the operation.</note> along with the other requirements, as we have said in the preceding sections, and write this conjuration with the others on clean paper, as is the custom in this science.

<header>OF STEGANOGRAPHY</header>

### The Establishment of any secret.

Suppose we have a secret for a friend, or for anyone else skilled in our art, which we can convey neither by letter nor by a messenger <note>original: *tabellionem*, a scribe or notary acting as a courier</note>, because we fear danger in both. We therefore call upon a spirit, entrust the secret to the more powerful one: and in all things we are safe. <note>Trithemius uses the "calling of spirits" as a metaphor for the encryption process.</note>

### The substance of the letter as desired. <image-desc>steganographic flourish/symbol</image-desc>

->Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim of the Order of Saint Benedict,<-
->to Brother Quirinus, Monk of Corvey<-
->in Francia, greetings.<-

> *I am sending my prayer concerning the mother of the perpetual Virgin Mary, which you recently requested, Quirinus: Hail, most kind mother Anna, most radiant parent of the Virgin Mary: you are the most peaceful <note>Original: *paclissima*, likely a contraction for *pacatissima*.</note> helper of all who invoke you: you are a noble refuge for those who seek you: a quiet benefit of holiness. Hail, most gentle advocate of the poor, most beautiful consoler of the mourning: most adorned storehouse of virtues. Hail, Jewess <note>original: *iuda*</note> reviver of the sorrowful: most salutary medicine of the sick: and most celebrated promoter of the faithful Christians: obtain pardon for us as we pray: intercede to mitigate the terrifying judge, Christ, for your servants, so that we may attain blessedness: we ask that through your intercession, by his kindness, he may receive us wretches. Amen. April 6th <note>original: *8. Iduum April.*, the 8th day before the Ides of April</note>, in the Year of our Lord 1500.*

When the one to whom they are sent, being skilled in the art, receives the letter, let him first observe the character <note>A symbolic mark or astrological sign.</note> of the hour attached to the letter: once this is known, let him work through the lord of the ascending hour in which he received the letter, along with the other precepts in the art. Then let him add the prescribed prayer, in the manner and order that is necessary: and soon many spirits will appear. When you see them, these words are to be spoken to the principal one among them: **chamerusin massotiel vear reneas liernoty trismy penason**. <note>These "barbarous names" are likely a cipher representing the actual secret message or the key to decoding the prayer.</note> When these words have been spoken to the spirit who is more powerful than the others—who is easily recognized by his attire and crown and always precedes the others—he will soon reveal the secret in your ear.

## CHAPTER XII.

### OF THE SPIRIT WHO IS THE GREAT EMPEROR AND HAS UNDER HIS DOMINION MANY PRINCES, DUKES, COUNTS, AND SERVANTS; THE HOUR IS CALLED NAYBALON, AND HIS SPIRIT IS BERATIEL.

<image-desc>Ornamental decorative drop cap 'D' at the beginning of the first paragraph, featuring a figure in a landscape within the letter's frame.</image-desc>The twelfth hour of the day is called Nahalon, and its supreme angel is named the Emperor Beratiel. According to Solomon the Jew <note>Referring to the *Key of Solomon*, a famous grimoire or book of magic attributed to King Solomon.</note>, Beratiel has under his command almost innumerable spirits assigned to various operations. From all of these, different spirits in different orders are assigned to the practice of <term>Steganography</term> <note>Trithemius coined this term from the Greek *steganos* (hidden) and *graphein* (to write). While it looks like magic, it is actually a system for secret communication.</note>. In the first order there are 10 dukes and counts; in the second, 20; in the third, 30; in the fourth, 40; in the fifth, 500; in the sixth, 600; in the seventh, 700; in the eighth, 800; in the ninth, 900; in the tenth, 100; in the eleventh, 200; and in the twelfth, 300. All of these, through their turns and orders with an infinite supply of servants, are assigned to the operations of this art; an intelligent and perfect operator of the art can call upon as many of them as he wishes during the twelfth hour of the day. Furthermore, all the princes, dukes, and counts of this hour—according to the opinion of Raziel <note>Refers to the *Sefer Raziel HaMalakh*, a medieval practical Kabbalah text.</note>—assume whatever forms the practitioner desires.

| ℟. | | ℟. | | ℟. | | ℟. |
|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|:---|
| Camaron | Plamiel | Edriel | 10 | 100 | 10 | 100 |
| *Astrosiel* | *Nerosiel* | *Choriel* | 10 | 100 | 10 | 100 |
| Penatiel | Emarson | Romiel | 10 | 100 | 10 | 100 |
| Demarac | Quirix | Fenosiel | 10 | 100 | 10 | 100 |
| Famaras | Sameron | Hamary | 10 | 100 | 10 | 100 |

Here we also have fifteen spirits named from those of Beratiel, of whom five are dukes across as many orders, and ten are counts; to these are joined 1,100 servants for various operations in the secrets of magic. Therefore, when you wish to work through any of them in the final hour of the day, observe the lord of the ascendant <note>The planet that rules the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific time.</note> of that hour and the twelfth house; write the character <note>A magical symbol or sigil used as a "key" in the encryption process.</note> of each, along with other necessary things, on a clean piece of parchment, and on its back write this conjuration:

# STEGANOGRAPHY

### The Invocation.

Berathiel Odiel irsoti rodu dreor ranezo melros ethiel aty nodiel hayres penason. <note>These words are likely not intended to be a translatable language, but rather a phonetic string that serves as a cryptographic key or a ritualistic formula.</note> Then, after performing all the necessary preliminaries according to custom, recite this invocation in the proper manner; and immediately the summoned spirits will appear. Then, you may entrust your secret to the more powerful one, according to the instruction of the art: and he will obey faithfully in all things.

->*The reasoning for any secret whatsoever, according to one's will, as it may occur.*<-

Suppose we have a friend who is far away: we know that danger is threatening him, of which he himself is unaware. We wish to warn him, but for certain reasons we do not dare to do so openly. We therefore call upon the spirits assigned to this operation according to the hour: and we safely entrust the secret to them.

## We seal letters, composed as we please, with the sign of the spirit.

> Everyone who glories in temporal things should by no means be called virtuous, and he who loves transitory things cannot be a lover of Justice: because Catholic integrity detests a man gasping with greed. For the holy religion of the Christian faith forbids the love of the world to its followers: since he who loves worldly joys loses celestial happiness. A Catholic not living in a Catholic manner is proved to have denied Christ: and he who loves temporal things is not said to be a good Catholic. He who loves the Creator seeks the joys of spiritual things. A lover of Christ despises all the pleasures of the world, nor does he care for glory and unstable riches. But a lover of the world hates virtues: he cares nothing for future joys. Alas, what are you doing, Christians, intent on vain things? You think on things that are not honorable, and you act contrary to religion. Do not, I beseech you, wretches, do not love the soon-to-perish vanities of this world. Take care rather to love Christ, so that he may receive us into the heavenly kingdoms. *Johannes Trithemius, Abbot.* <note>original: "Ioan. Trithe. Abb." This entire pious letter serves as a "cover text." The actual secret message would be extracted from this text using a specific pattern (such as the first or last letters of certain words).</note>

When a person skilled in this art receives this aforementioned <term>short prayer</term> <note>original: "oratiunculam"; refers to a brief devotional text used here as a disguise for a message.</note> or any others sealed with the symbol of Berathiel: let him carefully perform all things commanded in the art and recite the invocation. And when he sees that the spirits—who will come without delay—are present: let him say to the more powerful one, who walks wearing a crown: **Famerusyn melysno alny vemoby dreary drymes charlony.** Having said these things, you shall approach closer

The FIRST hour of the night is called Omalharien, and its angel is named Sabrathan, who holds many other Spirits under him, serving various magical operations according to their times and orders, as witnessed by Hermes Solomon the Jew <note>original: "Hermes Salomon Iudæus"; Trithemius often cites legendary figures like Hermes Trismegistus or King Solomon to grant authority to his cryptographic "magic."</note>, most experienced in magic. From these, many have been assigned to the operation of our art by their turns and orders. In the first order there are 10; in the second 20; in the third 30; in the fourth 40; in the fifth 500; in the sixth 600; in the seventh 70; in the eighth 80; in the ninth 90; in the tenth 100. All these are sent into the ministry of secrets through their turns and hidden orders, of which we have neither full knowledge, nor do we speak.

STEGANOGRAPHIA

**I**t will be a great secret and an arduous mystery, which would cause harm if published; and for this reason, it should not be committed to letters or a notary, but rather it should undergo the ministry of the Spirits who preside over the hour in which you are working. It is not safe to summon Spirits for trivial matters; but where human means fail, their help should be sought through the art. <note>Trithemius uses the language of "spirits" to describe his cryptographic system; the "ministry of spirits" refers to the specific encoding rules associated with a particular hour.</note>

### Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim, to brother Nicholas Baselius, Monk of Hirsau <note>Nicholas Baselius was a fellow scholar and close friend of Trithemius at the Abbey of Hirsau</note>, sends greetings. Let him speak the following prayer:

> **A**lmighty God, exalted Creator of all, who through Christ redeemed us Christians, gave the zeal of His love to Catholics, instilling the remission of sins through the light of faith in those who believe. He most generously gave His beloved Son, the gracious light, to free all of us believers from the power of the mournful King of Tartarus <note>original: "regis Tartarei"; a classical reference to the ruler of the underworld or Hell</note> and to destroy the dark prison and the most cruel Devil <note>original: "Zabulum"; a medieval Latin variant for "Diabolum" or the Devil</note>. What can we Christians worthily return for the passions of Christ: we weak men, professing the Catholic faith without works of justice? Therefore, praying most devotedly, we praise You, our Redeemer, with the highest praises, saying: praise to You, eternal light, most kind Savior of all, who redeemed us with Your blood. Confirm our frailty with the zeal of Your love. Save us, sweetest Jesus Savior, and pour into our hearts the zeal of the Catholic faith. Your zeal is holy: it works compunction in the hearts of those seeking You, O most kind Christ Savior. O eternal Light, sweetest Jesus: illuminate the darkness of Your servants with Your usual goodness. Eternal Jesus Savior, save our souls: confer eternal joys upon us. Christ, hear Your humble servants, and forgive the sins of all those who pray. Savior, save us miserable ones, though we are most unworthy. Grant good zeal to us who humbly ask, Amen. Written the 18th day before the Calends of May <note>April 14th</note>, in the year of our Lord 1500.

### CHAPTER XIV.

->**OF THE HOUR CALLED PANEZVR, AND ITS ANGEL**<-
->*whose supreme commander is called Tartys, who has under him many princes, dukes, counts, and servants.*<-

The second hour of the night is called Panezur, and its angel is named Tartys. He holds under his command many princes, dukes, counts, and under-servants, whom he is accustomed to send in their turns and orders when they are called to the various ministries of those who work in the hidden secrets of nature. Of these, twelve orders are assigned to our science of <term>Steganography</term> <note>original: "Steganographię"; the art of hidden writing. Trithemius uses the metaphor of "spirits" to represent different cryptographic systems or substitution tables.</note>; these are sufficient for us for every operation. For in the first order there are 10; in the second, 20; in the third, 30; in the fourth, 40; in the fifth, 50; in the sixth, 60; in the seventh, 70; in the eighth, 80; in the ninth, 90; in the tenth, 100; in the eleventh, 1,000; in the twelfth, 100,000.

However, it rarely happens that an operation is performed through all of these Spirits, because there is no need to be occupied with the higher ones: since the lower ones are abundantly sufficient for us for all things. Unless someone, well-instructed and entirely perfect in the art, should wish to see the princes of all the prescribed orders and know their names: such a person could summon them all in order.

S T E G A N O G R A P H Y

Here we have named from the dukes of Tartys 6, and from the counts 12; the servants are also numbered, distributed through their turns and orders to the number of 1,520. These are sufficient for us for the experiment of this art in this hour. And note that (according to Solomon and Raziel <note>Trithemius references the *Key of Solomon* and the *Liber Razielis*, famous medieval grimoires, to lend his cryptographic system an air of traditional authority</note>) all these dukes and princes of Tartys are benevolent and most prompt in obedience; and in whatever form they are commanded to appear, they freely assume it. Therefore, when you wish to operate in this science: observe the lord of the ascendant <note>The planet ruling the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> with the other things which we have diligently prescribed: and write all things which are to be written with this conjuration.

### Conjuration.

*Tartys chrybes faziel ytsoti haelnot dreor aduear asy mearo Veny fatu pemerson.* <note>As with previous chapters, this "conjuration" is a string of nonsense words that likely serves as a key or a signal to the recipient that a hidden message follows.</note> Then, when all things have been most diligently completed, and the conjuration read in the due manner as is required: the called spirits will soon be present, ready at your command.

### *Let the secret be, whatever occurs to us, great and difficult.*

**A** secret that we wish to intimate to a friend through spirits should be of whatever kind occurs to us according to the time and variety of business: provided that it is not trivial, but of such a nature that it cannot otherwise be safely announced.

## Johannes Trithemius to the Holy Nuns on the Mount of Saint Rupert <note>The Rupertsberg monastery near Bingen, famously founded by Hildegard of Bingen</note>, who adorn the Catholic faith with radiant Virginity, wishing them to merit eternal happiness through their continuous and most holy religious zeal.

*By the providence of the mercy of the eternal king, we send to you a man long-awaited for a great time, one of great experience in temporal affairs, born in Raunenstat, the reverend and for a long time most zealous Abbot of the monastery of Violsdorp, who has freely resigned that same Abbey. I approached him, just as I had promised you, at the monastery of Lüneburg, in which he was serving Christ, occupied with continuous prayers and the readings of the holy scriptures, together with the monks of the same monastery: and I requested that he might come down to your convent, and undertake the care of temporal things, and become your procurator and cellarer; the Abbot of Limburg, with his usual instability, in his soul*

*...changed, I was able to release him from that agreement. With Christ inspiring him, he finally consented, although timidly: because he fears serving nuns, who are very easily changed. He fears not without merit, since as experience has taught us, sometimes nuns have returned to their stewards <term>Oeconomis</term> <note>Managers of the monastery's temporal and financial affairs</note> for their good deeds a reward that is not praiseworthy. The feminine mind is suddenly changed by a zeal of bitterness. But with Christ granting it, I know he will be useful to you. He is a Christian, he is a monk, he fears the Savior, and he is of good understanding. Therefore, accustom yourselves to restrain womanly changeability so that you may remember to be constant: receiving him with holy honor and sincere charity, laying aside all levity of the womanly mind: especially since he can help you and recover the state of temporal affairs in your monastery which has collapsed, with the help of Christ the Redeemer, who is jealous for charity. Farewell, from Sponheim, the 17th day before the Kalends of May <note>April 15th</note>, in the year of our Lord Jesus Christ, 1500.*

# BOOK TWO.

*I have made a great Catalog of ecclesiastical writers <note>original: "DE eccleſiaſticis ſcriptoribus grandem feci Catalogum"</note>, which I recently presented to your brotherhood to be seen; and concluding it too suddenly, I confess that I passed over several most famous men. This was not because I envied their names the benefit of the Catalog, as a certain swollen critic <note>original: "penſator tumidus," literally "swollen weigher," likely a derogatory nickname for a critic</note> falsely charges, reviling the Catalog with most foolish words, thinking that I failed to mention his people out of envy alone—which is completely false. Furthermore, I wrote and affirmed that the conception of the most holy Mother of God was always most beautiful and without the blemish of original transgression <note>original: "ſine nauo originalis exceſſus," referring to the doctrine of the Immaculate Conception</note>. This, the swollen critic, wishing to rebuke it in his swollen way, said that the most radiant Virgin was stained by the guilt of original sin (with the most vain rashness against Catholic observance). It is a rash thing to wish to defile the most blessed Virgin. However, the most renowned doctors of Cologne, wishing to root out the opinion of that insane little friar <note>original: "inſani fraterculi"</note> which is contrary to the truth, compelled him, when summoned, to revoke his vain fables, asserting that the most blessed Virgin—and confirming that it must be believed by decree—was most pure, and at no time (however brief) was subject to original foulness. I wished to write these things to you because you love the truth, so that you may avoid those who reproach the most blessed Virgin. Farewell, May 16, in the Year of Our Lord 1500.*

The one to whom the letter is sent, being skilled in the art, should receive it and attend to the sign of the principal Spirit, so that he may know whom he ought to call upon according to the turns of the hour. Then let him observe the lord of the ascendant <term>Lord of the Ascendant</term> <note>In traditional astrology, the planet that rules the sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> in the very hour that he receives the letter, along with the rest of the disposition of the heavens through the twelve houses of the Zodiac, omitting nothing at all of those things which have been commanded. Having spoken the conjuration, when he sees the Spirit present, let him say the following words: *chameruſyn maſlotiel vean reneas cralty thyrmo venear penarſon.* <note>While these appear to be magical words of power, in Trithemius’s system they often function as a series of instructions or nulls in a cryptographic code</note> When these things have been said to the principal Spirit, he will presently draw closer and reveal the secret committed to him into the ear.

# OF STEGANOGRAPHY

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut drop cap 'Q' containing a human face in profile surrounded by stylized foliage and scrollwork.</image-desc>
**T**he fourth hour of the night is called **Ramerzy**, whose angel is named **Iefischa**. He has under his command many princes, dukes, counts, and ministerial spirits: who, like those we described in the previous chapter, are distributed by turns and twelve ranks. In the first rank there are 10; in the second, 20; in the third, 30; in the fourth, 40; in the fifth, 50; in the sixth, 60; in the seventh, 70; in the eighth, 80; in the ninth, 90; in the tenth, 100; in the eleventh, 1,000; and in the twelfth, 10,000. From these twelve ranks, spirits are sent to us in their turns for service in the operations of the art of **Steganography** <note>While it sounds like magic, Trithemius used "Steganography" to mean secret writing; these "spirits" are part of a complex system for encoding messages.</note>, of which we shall now name a few.

| Aleph | | Aleph | | Aleph | | Aleph |

| Armosiel | Rayziel | Lamediel | 10 | 100 | 100 | 1000 |

Here we have now named 6 dukes and 12 counts from the spirits of **Iefischa**, to whom are assigned 7,260 <note>The Latin text reads "72. 60."</note> ministerial spirits who are sent in their turns and ranks to serve the one practicing the art. Now, these princes, according to the saying of Solomon the Philosopher <note>King Solomon was traditionally credited with the power to command spirits in medieval and Renaissance occultism.</note>, are multiform: and they assume whatever shapes they are commanded. Therefore, when you wish to work through them in the fourth hour of the night, observe the lord of the ascendant <note>A technical term in astrology referring to the planet that rules the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon.</note> and the whole configuration of the heavens, and do everything according to what we prescribe for you, writing everything on a clean slip of paper <note>original: "schedulam mundam"</note>, and on its back, this conjuration.

->*The Conjuration.*<-

<image-desc>Decorative initial 'I' featuring scrollwork patterns.</image-desc>
**I**efischa osiel mear pathyr lays mean theor dreochis fazan moab lofeas ersoti breo pornys tayr penarson. <note>In the context of this book, these "barbarous words" of the conjuration are actually a cipher. The recipient would extract the hidden message using a specific key, often by taking certain letters from each word.</note> Then, after adding what needs to be added, when you have spoken the conjuration itself in the proper manner, form, and order: the spirits will appear visible in whatever form you commanded, ready and willing for all things.

->*Let the secret be as you please.*<-

Whatever secret comes to you for the time being, which you wish someone absent to know in this fourth hour of the night: you can safely commit it to the Spirits: <note>Trithemius uses "Spirits" as a metaphor for his cryptographic system. To the uninitiated, it looks like a magical invocation, but it is actually a method for encoding text.</note> provided it is such a thing that you cannot safely communicate to him through letters or messengers. For those things which we can communicate without danger through ordinary letters or messengers, we should by no means communicate by the ministry of Spirits. But whoever attempts to call upon Spirits for trivial matters and secrets which can be communicated without danger through letters or messengers, will without doubt suffer loss and inconvenience with danger. <note>This is a warning typical of the period, suggesting that "misusing" the art (or the cipher) carries heavy consequences.</note>

->*The content of the letter as you please.*<-

->To the venerable father in Christ, the Steward of **Limburg**,<-
->the religious lord named Johann **Weissdorffen**,<-
->Johannes of the Fold, <note>Latin: *Ouilis*. This is a play on Trithemius's own name; he was the Abbot of Sponheim.</note> humble Abbot of **Sponheim**,<-
->wishes the joys of Heaven.<-

*The nuns of Saint Rupert <note>The convent of St. Rupert near Bingen.</note> desire you as their governor with great eagerness, and they ask you with humble benevolence to undertake the governance of the temporal affairs of that monastery. To serve the devout virgins of Christ is praiseworthy and meritorious. You have a most famous visitor for the spiritual affairs of that same monastery, who holds you in the greatest honor: a man grave in morals, adorned with virtues, cautious in counsel, beloved by all, well-reputed in his monastic religious life, and notable to all men (a life which is deservedly most pleasing to Christ), the excellent Abbot of the monastery of Saint John. He is most learned, most studious, and most taught in the writings of both Christians and gentiles, <note>Latin: *gentilium*. This refers to the classical Greek and Roman authors.</note> who notably exceeds the wisdom of all men by far. He knows best how to rule the flock entrusted to him and is a zealous guardian of the virgins of Christ, whose cloister he keeps most firmly. Do not fear to serve such honest nuns under him, for he knows how to govern the spouses of Christ according to the monastic law with the best discretion: he who, by virtue and zeal for the Christian faith, will gladly help you in necessity. Since you have such a clear and kind pastor as a friend, I ask that you gladly assume the governance of such religious virgins. Christ will reward your labor with eternal joys.*

...the visitor for the spiritual affairs of the nuns, Stanislaw. When the person to whom these letters are sent, being skilled in this art, receives a message marked with the *letischa* character <note>Letischa is the name associated with the specific cipher/spirit of the fourth hour.</note>; let him first observe the lord of the ascendant of that hour with the entire figure of the sky <note>This refers to drawing up an astrological chart or horoscope for the moment the letter is received.</note>, and let him describe it on a clean sheet of paper. On the back of it, he should write the prescribed conjuration, which, once he has said it and performed all necessary prerequisites, and sees the Spirits sent to him standing present: let him say to the one higher in rank as follows: **chamerusin aphroys aslotiel mean teneas vear tryamo cralti penafon.** <note>While these words appear to be a magical incantation, in Trithemius’s system, they often serve as the cryptographic key or the encoded message itself.</note> When these things have been duly spoken to the principal Spirit, he will approach closer and reveal the secret committed to him into the ear of the recipient.

## CHAPTER XVII.
### WHOSE HOUR IS CALLED SANAYFAR, AND WHOSE
### supreme angel and commander is called Abasdarhon, who has
### under his dominion many dukes, counts, and
### servants.

<image-desc>Decorative initial letter 'Q' featuring dense foliage and scrollwork within a square frame.</image-desc> THE FIFTH hour of the night is called **Sanayfar**, whose first angel and supreme commander is called **Abasdarhon**. He has under his dominion many princes, dukes, counts, and ministers, distributed through twelve orders and successions just like the previous ones: whom he is accustomed to send into the service of various arts and experiments to those practitioners who are duly instructed. In the first order there are 10; in the second 20; in the third 30; in the fourth 40; in the fifth 50; in the sixth 60; in the seventh 70; in the eighth 80; in the ninth 90; in the tenth 100; in the eleventh 1,000; in the twelfth 100,000. And through these, every operation of Steganography is performed in the fifth hour of the night.

->BOOK TWO.<-

Samilen Nameton Habalon 100 100 100 100

Here we have already named 40 of the spirits of Abasdarhon. The first twelve of these in their ranks are dukes, while the remaining 12 are counts; and there are two from each order, namely one duke and one count. We have 3,200 assigned ministerial servants, whom they use in turns in various magical operations. However, Solomon and Raziel <note>Trithemius refers here to the legendary authors of grimoires like the *Key of Solomon* and *Liber Razielis*, who were considered the ultimate authorities on medieval spirit magic.</note>, wise men most experienced in magical matters, say that the princes of Abasdarhon are accustomed to appear in whatever form they are commanded by the operator: which, having frequently experienced ourselves, we confess to be supported by the truth. 

Therefore, when you wish to work through them in Steganography <term>Steganography: Here, the term refers to Trithemius's system of "hidden writing" where a message is supposedly delivered by spirits, but is actually encoded in a carrier text.</term> in the fifth hour of the night: observe the lord of the ascendant <note>The planet ruling the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the specific time of the operation.</note>, and write both it and the entire disposition of the Heavens <note>An astrological chart or square representing the positions of the stars and planets.</note> on a clean slip of paper with the other things we have taught, and on the back of it, write this conjuration.

### Conjuration.

**ABasdarhon morca lafias tharuean buel dreschin tayr moaber efoty layr pornys theori mean afar penason.** <note>While these appear to be magical names, in Trithemius’s system, these words are actually a cipher that contains the encoded instructions for the recipient.</note> Then, having added what needs to be added, as soon as you have spoken the conjuration itself, the spirits whom you called will appear to you, ready for everything you command them.

### Method for a secret that occurs.

Whatever secret you have, which you otherwise cannot make known without danger to a friend living far or near, commit it to the more powerful of the spirits with the ritual and form which is appropriate according to the art, fearing nothing: because they are benevolent and faithful.

## Let the letters be formed as you please.

*The Catholic faith saves no one who despises the fire of divine love. To believe and yet not have the zeal of divine love contributes nothing at all to the redemption of the soul. However, many people are found now boasting in the Catholic faith, but few have the works of Christian faith: for an idle faith dies. Many believe, but very few observe the commandments of God. Alas, the zeal of believers is weak: a stronger one even unbelievers can have, and many do. The gentiles and men who do not believe our Savior was made man, the son of the Almighty, shall be tortured with the pains of Hell. They shall be tormented...*

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for communicating via the spirit Abasdarhon, the ruler of the fifth hour of the night. It concludes a "cover letter" designed to look like a pious sermon while providing the specific ritual for the recipient to extract the hidden message.</meta>

# STEGANOGRAPHY

*...however, with heavier punishments, all those who believe that the Son of God came into the world and was made man out of zeal for human redemption, yet do not adorn the Christian faith with holy works: being always entangled in sins, they prefer the honors of the world to eternal blessedness: zealously performing impious actions, and staining the purity of the faith with depraved morals in defiance of obedience. But the lovers of God, who have a right zeal for the Christian faith, prefer nothing to God; they imitate Christ, flee from sins, and despise the riches and honors of the world. Now, therefore, pay heed to these words, you who glory in the faith: love God, and you shall have the reward of blessedness by being zealous for justice. If you love the vanities of the age against the honor of God, you shall receive the prize of those who are zealous for iniquity: the horrors of hell. Written on the 15th of the Calends of May <note>original: "15. Calend. Maij." This corresponds to April 17th.</note>, in the Year of our Lord 1500.*

When the one to whom the letter is sent, being skilled in the art, receives the letter marked with the character of Abasdarhon: let him immediately consider the lord of the ascendant <note>The planet ruling the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the operation.</note> and the entire disposition of the heavens. Let him observe and write this, along with the tenor of the conjuration and other necessary things, upon a clean slip of paper; and having added those things which are to be added, and having spoken the said conjuration, when he sees the spirits that were sent standing before him, let him say to the more powerful one these words: **Chameron massotiel caseuij renean pornas thilmeuij penason.** <note>These "magical" words are likely part of Trithemius's cryptographic system, where such strings of nonsense or foreign-sounding names act as a key to unlock the message.</note> When these have been spoken, the spirit will soon draw near and faithfully reveal the secret committed to him into the ear of the operator.

## CHAPTER XVIII.

->WHOSE HOUR IS CALLED THAAZARON, AND<-
->*His Angel is called Zaazenach, presiding over many spirits.*<-

THE SIXTH hour of the night is called Thaazaron, and the name of its Angel is Zaazenach, who has under his command princes and leaders, counts and ministerial servants almost beyond numbering, who preside over various magical operations through their turns and orders. In the first order there are 10; in the second 20; in the third 30; in the fourth 40; in the fifth 50; in the sixth 60; in the seventh 70; in the eighth 80; in the ninth 90; in the tenth 100; in the eleventh 1,000; in the twelfth 10,000. Moreover, when you wish to call them, in whatever form pleases you, they are ready and entirely obedient, just as the aforementioned wise men testify, and as we ourselves have experienced.

->*The procedure for any secret that may arise.*<-

**A**ny secret whatsoever that occurs to us, which we can safely intimate to a friend neither through messengers nor through letters: in the sixth hour of the night, let us commit it to one of the prescribed leaders—or even the companions, if a leader is not present, which can often happen—in the manner and order in which the command exists in this art, fearing nothing at all: because all are good and faithful in everything which has been committed to them by the operator, and they omit nothing.

STEGANOGRAPHIA

*Be mindful of your promise which, while looking up to Christ and receiving the Catholic faith, you offered by solemn vow for eternal joy, to which it [the promise] will be sent ahead: and to those going, perhaps for a long time? <note>original: "an fort. diu?"</note> Without a doubt, temporal pleasures and the joys of the present life are promised [to be cast aside] in view of the future eternal rest by those who despise them, and even more, by those who love God. We know that the best rewards are promised to the lovers of God; but for the lovers of the world and for reprobate Christians, God has prepared the most bitter pains of hell. He who has faith, but neglects to have divine love, is unfaithful, and presumes upon faith in vain. O Zealot of divine love, who does not cease to daily adorn the Christian faith with devoted and good works, exercising himself humbly in the arts of piety, farewell. The 14th of the Calends of May <note>In the Roman calendar, this corresponds to April 18th.</note>, in the year of the Lord’s incarnation 1500.*

When he who is skilled in the art has received letters marked with the character of the Spirit of the hour: having first observed the <term>figure of the heavens</term> <note>An astrological chart or map of the planetary positions at that specific time.</note>, let him write it down along with the other necessary things: and then, having completed everything pertaining to the operation, and having spoken the prescribed conjuration, when the Spirit has appeared visibly, let him say: *Chamerusi ferion notiel aseuomy rean, badian, laso sear vaobry hastoripeson.* <note>This "conjuration" is a string of encoded words. In Trithemius's system, these are often null-ciphers where the actual message is hidden within the structure of the words.</note> Having said these things, the Spirit will faithfully and most secretly reveal the secret committed to him into the ear of the one operating.

### CHAPTER XIX.
### WHOSE HOUR IS CALLED VENAYDOR, AND WHOSE angel is named Mendrion, having under his command princes, dukes, counts, and attendants almost beyond number.

<image-desc>Decorative initial 'S' at the beginning of the text, featuring white floral and leaf patterns on a black background within a square frame.</image-desc>

The seventh hour is called **Venaydor**, whose angel and supreme Spirit is called **Mendrion**, who has under his command many other Spirits, such as princes, dukes, counts, and attendants; who are assigned to various operations in magic. Out of these, many in number have been assigned by their turns and orders—princes, dukes, and counts with their servants—to <term>Steganography</term> <note>From the Greek *steganós* (hidden) and *graphia* (writing). Trithemius uses the guise of spirit summoning to mask a complex system of cryptography.</note> and its operations in the seventh hour of the night. In the first order there are 10; in the second 20; in the third 30; in the fourth 40; in the fifth 50; in the sixth 60; in the seventh 70; in the eighth 80; in the ninth 90; in the tenth 100; in the eleventh 1000; in the twelfth 100,000. All of these are called by turns and come to the operation.

->*The Secret May Be of Any Kind.*<-

In announcing a secret to a friend, whether near or far away, who is already skilled in this art of spirits, it matters little what kind of secret it is: provided it is not trivial or the sort of thing that could otherwise have been safely delivered through messengers or letters. We also have no concern for the characters <note>original: "Literarum"; referring to the specific script or alphabet used.</note>, of what kind they may be: provided they include the <term>character</term> <note>A unique magical symbol or seal associated with a specific celestial being.</note> of the principal angel of that hour in which the operation is performed: and no secret at all [will be hidden...]
