OF STEGANOGRAPHY
EIGHTH GATHERING

->Let us form letters as we please.<-

## Abbot Johannes Trithemius sends greetings to the monk Nicolaus Baffelius and transmits this prayer of Saint Nicholas.

> *The creator, reformer, and maker of all things created the first man Adam, to whom he joined a most beautiful wife named Eve, the mother of all living: prefiguring the Catholic Church to be joined to pastors, shining beautifully with the ways of righteousness, and destined to give birth to sons of good works—men who receive the kingdoms of heaven, which shall remain without end, through humility. Christ redeemed us through his kindness, humbling himself for our sake. It is therefore necessary that we love the most kind one, who gave us the Catholic faith: those who truly love it will rightfully obtain angelic happiness. Let us all therefore honor our almighty creator every day, conversing in true religion and living Catholically. Lord Jesus, most kind savior, who mercifully redeemed us all from the pit of death, kindly attending to our fragility and infirmity, conform us to the Catholic faith: so that after this transitory life we may deserve to have the rest of eternal consolation and happiness forever, Amen. April 18 <note>original: "14. Cal. Maij"; using the Roman calendar, the 14th day before the Kalends of May is April 18th</note>, in the Year of our Lord 1500.*

When the person to whom these letters are sent, being skilled in our art, receives them sealed with the character of <term>Mendrio</term> <note>The presiding spirit of the seventh hour of the night</note>: let him observe the <term>lord of the ascendant</term> <note>The planet ruling the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at that specific moment</note>: and let him write both it and the entire disposition of the heavens on a clean sheet of paper, and on its back, the conjuration. Then, having added all those things which are to be added, and having recited the incantation, as soon as he sees the Spirit standing before him, let him say these words to the first one: *Chamerulyn merion nodiel burmy raueto badria sauepo elay reas penafon.* <note>These words are likely a phonetic cipher used to transmit a hidden message, disguised as a magical incantation.</note>

When he has sounded these words to him who appears most prominent among the other spirits: that one, approaching calmly, will soon reveal the secret committed to him.

# BOOK TWO.

<image-desc>A decorative woodcut drop-cap initial 'O' depicting a scholar or philosopher in a room with a window, possibly observing the heavens or working at a desk.</image-desc>

The eighth hour of the night is called **Xymalim**, and its angel, the first and supreme Commander, is called **Narcoriel**. He has under his command many other spirits: princes, dukes, counts, and <term>servants</term> <note>Latin: *ministeriales*; lower-ranking spirits who execute specific tasks</note>, who are called according to their ranks for various effects by those who work in the secrets of philosophy. From all of these, there are twelve ranks of those who serve <term>Steganography</term> <note>A term coined by Trithemius meaning "hidden writing." While presented here as magic, it is a system of cryptography.</note>. In the first rank there are 10; in the second, 20; in the third, 30; in the fourth, 40; in the fifth, 50; in the sixth, 60; in the seventh, 70; in the eighth, 80; in the ninth, 90; in the tenth, 100; in the eleventh, 1,000; and in the twelfth, 10,000. All these spirits observe their ranks so strictly that no one can work safely through them unless he has been fully instructed in all matters. For whoever does not observe the proper order both exposes himself to danger and will be frustrated in the desired effect.

| $\mathcal{R}.$ <note>Likely an abbreviation for *Regentes* (Rulers) or *Rectores*</note> | $\mathcal{R}.$ | $\mathcal{R}.$ | $\mathcal{R}.$ | | | |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Cambiel | Amelzon | Xaneryz | 100 | 100 | 1,000 | 1,000 |
| Nedarym | Lemozar | Iastrion | 100 | 100 | 1,000 | 1,000 |
| Astrocon | Xernifiel | Themaz | 100 | 100 | 1,000 | 1,000 |
| Marihel | Kanorsiel | Hobrazym | 100 | 100 | 1,000 | 1,000 |
| Dramozyn | Busanotz | Zymeloz | 100 | 100 | 1,000 | 1,000 |
| Lustihen | Iamedroz | Gamsiel | 100 | 100 | 1,000 | 1,000 |

Here we now have twelve named dukes and six counts, appointed through all twelve ranks in a hidden arrangement; they have under them servants numbering thirteen and two hundred <note>The text uses Roman numerals "x i i i. & c c.", totaling 213</note>, who are sufficient for us for every operation of the eighth hour of the night. Therefore, when you wish to work through them in Steganography during the eighth hour of the night: observe the <term>lord of the ascendant</term> <note>The planet ruling the zodiac sign that is rising on the eastern horizon at the start of the operation</note>, and write both that planet and the entire arrangement of the Heavens <note>A horary chart or astrological map of the moment</note> on clean paper, and on its back, along with the other usual customs, write this conjuration:

### Conjuration.

**Narconiel aples pornya nauelo meas. Triomé ilneas azy fan lafias my bression ersoti penason.** <note>This "conjuration" is a coded message. In Trithemius's system, these seemingly nonsensical Latinate words contain a hidden plaintext revealed by a specific decryption key.</note>

Then, after adding the things that are customarily added, and having spoken the conjuration, the spirits called will appear in the form you commanded them to take, ready for all things.

# STEGANOGRAPHIA

### The Secret Method.

The secret which we wish a distant friend to know, if we trust neither letters nor messengers: let us entrust it to the more powerful of the spirits of Narcoriel in the eighth hour of the night, fearing nothing at all, because they are all faithful and most ready for obedience.

### Let us compose letters as we please.

> *O most kind Jesus, I give thanks to your most holy mercy, who by your blood reconciled the human race to the heavenly father, hanging raised on the Cross in a pitiable state. For our sake, you subjected the flourishing youth of your most noble body to harsh wounds, so that you might bring a dead man back to life. Therefore, let us Catholics give what thanks we can to our Redeemer: groaning and praising the memory of his passion by honoring the Savior of our race in the Christian manner. Have mercy on me, most clement Jesus, fountain of mercy, abyss of piety, glory of the saints, light of the heavenly citizens: and pour into me the sweetest taste of your most holy love. Rescue me from all my calamities. Grant me true joy of heart, that I may exult in your praises, glorifying you: because by the pain of your wounds you have healed me. What shall I return to your mercy, my Redeemer, my life-giver? I suffer with your labors and fervent tears, blessing the most holy wounds of your body. O Savior, most kind Jesus, I give immortal thanks, asking for the forgiveness of my crimes. In the year of our Lord 1513, on the 13th day before the Kalends of May <note>April 19, 1513</note>, I, Johannes Trithemius, wrote this prayer. <note>original Greek: ἐγὼ ἰωαννὴς τριτέμιος</note>*

When the one to whom they are sent—being skilled in this art—receives the letters (of whatever kind they may be) marked with the <term>character</term> <note>A specific occult symbol or sigil associated with a spirit</note> of Narcoriel: he should observe the <term>lord of the ascendant</term> <note>In astrology, the planet that rules the sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific moment</note> at that same hour, and he should write both that planet and the entire disposition of the heavens upon a clean parchment with the other details, adding all necessary things according to custom and the spoken conjuration. When he sees the spirit standing before him, let him say these words to the more powerful one: **Medaro Cassotiel va reneas thafny thyrmo thea penafon.** <note>These nonsensical-sounding words serve as a cryptographic key or "magical" command to trigger the revelation of the hidden message.</note> Once these things have been said according to the instruction of this art, the principal spirit will presently draw near and will reveal the secret word.

<header>BOOK TWO.</header>

## CHAPTER XXI.

### THE HOUR OF WHICH IS CALLED ZESCHAR, AND
*its <term>spirit|Trithemius uses the term 'spirit' to describe the intelligence or messenger assigned to a specific time, serving as a metaphor for the keys used in his cryptographic system</term> is named Pamiel, having many princes under him.*

The ninth hour of the night is called Zeschar, and its supreme Commander is the Angel named Pamiel. Like those who came before him, he has under his command many other spirits—both good and evil—including princes, dukes, counts, and attendants, who are assigned according to their turns and ranks. In the first rank there are 10; in the second 20; in the third 30; in the fourth 40; in the fifth 50; in the sixth 60; in the seventh 70; in the eighth 80; in the ninth 90; in the tenth 100; in the eleventh 1,000; and in the twelfth 100,000. However, in our <term>Steganographic|The art of hidden writing; here presented as a system of 'angel magic' where the names of spirits act as keys for a cipher</term> operations, we do not require all of these, as a few are sufficient for us.

Here we now have eighteen of Pamiel’s princes: who, in their turns, are sometimes dukes and sometimes counts. He who does not know their ranks will labor in this art in vain. They have 1,320 ministers assigned to them for the various turns and moments of the hour, with whom they work marvelous effects. When, therefore, you wish to work through them in the ninth hour of the night in Steganography, observe the lord of the <term>ascendant|The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the specific moment the work begins</term> of that same hour, as well as the entire state of the heavens; and write everything on clean paper, and on the back of that same paper, write this conjuration.

Then, having completed everything prescribed in the art, speak the conjuration itself, taking the utmost care not to omit anything which belongs to the perfection of the work...

For all those whom we have named are benevolent, good, prompt, and willing to obey the will of one who operates correctly, and they are faithful in the matters committed to them.

### *The Theme of Secrets.*

Whatever secret occurs to us, which otherwise we cannot safely make known to a friend either by letter or by messengers, we first entrust to the spirits appearing before us, and we are secure. Moreover, the one who is more powerful among the others is distinguished by his clothing and crown.

## Let the letters be formed at will.

> *Wail, you mortals who now laugh: for in many ways and most ungratefully you have despised the kindness of our glorious Redeemer with the heaviest of sins and horrendous crimes. Behold the Turk <note>original: "Turcus"; refers to the Ottoman Empire, a common source of existential dread in 15th-century Europe</note>, the lethal enemy of Christians, shall move his army from Hungary; he will slaughter men in the manner of beasts. He has crossed the Danube, fierce and inflamed with the zeal of impiety as if by fire. Therefore mourn, most wretched ones, you who are about to die in a horrendous slaughter; you shall die in the zeal of bitterness, because your sins will overtake you. You have despised the laws of God your most kind Redeemer. Therefore you shall be delivered to most cruel enemies, who will devour you like locusts. We have heard by report how cruelly the Turk has invaded Hungary, Bohemia, and Poland: he has destroyed the strongest cities; women and virgins consecrated to God, who held their chastity with the zeal of holiness, he will most foully defile, horribly butchering any Catholic men, prowling everywhere like cruel beasts. You will soon feel his horrible cruelty: for he will suddenly disturb Germany with his most warlike army, unless a general defense of the princes resists without delay. Therefore, groaning, take up humility: amend your ways: pray to God with tears, that he may grant you remission of your sins, and free us from the enemies who most thirst for our blood. Written on the 13th day before the Kalends of May <note>April 19th</note>, in the Year of our Lord 1500. Johannes Trithemius.*

Upon receiving these letters, or any others whatsoever secured with the sign of Pamiel, let the one skilled in this art to whom they are sent observe and write down the lord of the ascendant, and the whole figure of the heavens <note>Trithemius uses astrological terminology as a metaphor for the decryption keys</note>, just as we have instructed in

### CHAP. XXII.

## *WHOSE HOUR IS CALLED MALCHO, AND ITS*
## *Angel is called the Emperor Jafguarim, who has under his Command*
## *princes, dukes, counts, and many ministers.*

<image-desc>Decorative initial 'D' with ornamental vine and leaf motifs inside a rectangular border.</image-desc>
The tenth hour of the night is called Malcho, and its
Emperor, the supreme Angel, is called Jafguarim, who
has under his command many princes, dukes, counts,
and ministerial spirits, assigned to wonderful and di-
verse magical operations: just as Solomon, surnamed Hermes <note>original: "Salomon cognomento Hermes"; this refers to the tradition of associating King Solomon with Hermes Trismegistus, the legendary founder of Hermetic philosophy</note>, testifies in his secret book of Magic. For the purpose of Steganography <note>The art of hidden writing or secret communication</note>, 100 dukes and 100 counts, along with many ministers, are assigned according to their turns and ranks, such as:

| R. | | X. | | R. | | X. |
| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
| Zapheriel | Chameray | 30 | 10 | 100 | 100 | 300 |
| Emarziel | Hazaniel | 30 | 10 | 100 | 100 | 300 |

Although many dukes and counts are assigned to the operation of Steganography in the tenth hour of the night: nevertheless, since a few are sufficient for all our needs, we have listed by name three from the dukes and three from the counts in this table: adding the number of their servants legally distributed by turns and ranks. Anyone wishing to know more of their names should call upon one of the aforementioned spirits and question him about each one. When you wish to operate through these spirits in the tenth hour of the night: observe and perform everything that has been commanded, writing everything most diligently on a clean sheet, and on its back, write this conjuration in the manner prescribed.

### The intention of any secret whatever..

Let there be any secret whatever. When you cannot notify a friend, whether they are nearby or far away, of that secret through letters and messengers: appoint one or two of the spirits of Jafguarim, once summoned, as your couriers: entrust the secret to them, doubting nothing.

## Let letters be formed as you please..

> *This is the prophecy of Nicholas Baffelsus of Irbufia, Holy Bishop, which he saw in the Spirit. And he said: Thus says the Lord to the shepherds of the Church. Soon you shall perish, for a very great calamity shall scatter your wide dominion and sprinkle all Europe. Woe to the Ishmaelites <note>A historical term often used to refer to Muslims or the Ottoman Turks in this period.</note>, who break the covenants of the tabernacle: because they seize the pastures with envy and rashness. Behold, the dominion which you hold shall be broken, unless you hasten a greater penance: and you shall be handed over to be torn apart. Alas, the whole Church is sprinkled with the worst morals: hypocrisy has invaded its ministers: flee, do not look back, you shall be desolated, and you shall be dragged by dogs: they shall sprinkle the whole earth with blood. Seek penance, inhabitants of the earth. Benjamin the youth shall experience disturbances. Woe to the inhabitants of Gennesaret, which is Europe: because for the multitude of their sins they shall perish under the hands of the Bulgarians. The prophecy of Nicholas the Bishop ends. April 20th <note>original: "12. Cal. Maij"; the 12th day before the Kalends of May.</note>.*

When he to whom they are sent, being skilled in the art, receives any letters whatever pre-marked with the sign of Jafguarim: let him do all things which are commanded according to the art, and observe the order of the planets, and write what is to be written. And once the conjuration written above has been spoken: as soon as the sent spirits appear, let him say to the first among them: **Chamerusin othriel arnotiel solais elty. Naeles proy vear fato cralnoti, penason.** These things having been said, the spirit, approaching immediately, will faithfully and calmly reveal the message entrusted to him.

BOOK TWO.

## CHAPTER XXIII.

### ***THE HOUR WHICH IS CALLED AALACHO, AND its supreme emperor angel who is named Dardariel: who has under his command many dukes, counts, and spirits.***

<image-desc>A large decorative woodcut initial 'V' set within a square frame with ornate floral and foliate scrolls.</image-desc>

The eleventh hour of the night is called Aalacho, and its supreme emperor Spirit is named Dardariel, who holds under his command many most powerful princes and dukes: who in turn preside over counts and <term>attendants<ctrl46>ministeriales: minor spirits who serve the higher ranks</term> almost infinite in number. Moreover, these dukes are exceedingly benevolent, cheerful, and pleasant: and they delight in conversing with men. They are also most obedient to those who are well-instructed in the <term>Art<ctrl46>The "sacred art" or "magic art" of communicating with spirits through specific rituals and ciphers.</term>. And note, that the number of these dukes is infinite, because almost the whole air is filled with them, and they cannot be counted. Both Solomon and <note>original: "Salomon Hermes." Referring to the legendary King Solomon and Hermes Trismegistus, the mythical founder of Hermetic philosophy; both were considered the ultimate authorities on spirit command in the Renaissance.</note> Hermes say—and we ourselves have experienced—that among the dukes, counts, and attendant spirits of Dardariel, many are good, useful, and most honorable. These always desire to benefit men, constantly urging them by a certain invisible guidance toward what is good, toward honesty, peace, concord, toward the integrity of faith, toward the love of God and the keeping of His commandments, toward <term>contempt for the world<ctrl46>contemptum mundi: a common medieval philosophical theme of valuing spiritual life over material desires</term>, toward the desire for heavenly happiness, and toward every good thing. These are good and most holy spirits who love, worship, and possess God; they delight to be with holy and God-fearing men because they are assigned to them as protectors by God, the Creator of all. 

Others, however, are evil and most wicked <term>apostates<ctrl46>Those who have abandoned their faith or original holy state, referring here to fallen angels.</term> from God and enemies of all good things, who continually tempt mortals and lie in wait to trap men. And whatever good the holy spirits establish for the honor of God, these evil ones always strive to destroy and revoke. Indeed, the air is full of these malignant spirits: they run to and fro among men invisibly, always inciting them to evil, and they drag the unwary with them to hell. Through these evil spirits of Dardariel, infinite <term>sorceries<ctrl46>maleficia: specifically harmful magic or "black" magic meant to cause injury.</term> are performed, not only among Christians but even among the infidels: and they especially deceive <note>original: "mulierculas," literally "little women." In this period’s medical and theological context, it often pejoratively referred to women deemed "weak-willed" or "superstitious."</note> vulnerable women who are more prone to malice. And whoever wishes to persist in such wicked deeds through them will be powerful in evils above all other evildoers and worthy of eternal fire. We, however, who [teach] not evil, but good and—

<sig>R 3</sig>
<meta>catchword: teach:</meta>

Therefore, from the good spirits of Dardariel, we have named fourteen dukes here, and seven counts <note>The table lists the dukes in the first column and the counts in italics in the second column.</note>, who are sufficient for us for every operation of <term>Steganography<gloss>Secret writing; here referring to the magical system of sending hidden messages through spirits.</gloss></term> for the eleventh hour of the night, along with their many and almost infinite servants. Thus, when you wish to operate through any one or several of them, perform all things that are prescribed in the art of Steganography; and after you have completed all things, recite this conjuration with focused attention.

### Conjuration.

Dardariel pirno nadeuym pornis melto, nachir pheon pliros cuali estafri thyrmano, Oniel maniel, vear raby cralnoti Vemy Throe orbasiel asar rauean, Purgiel near iano masiel atlay. Naseui myrsos modias merchul noti penason. <note>To the uninitiated, this sounds like a magical incantation of "barbarous names." In Trithemius's system, however, these words are actually a transposition cipher. Each word or letter corresponds to a letter in a secret Latin message intended for the recipient.</note> Once this conjuration is completed with due attention, the summoned spirits will stand by with benevolence.

### The Secret to be Entrusted.

All the aforementioned dukes and counts are good and faithful, harming no one at all who loves God in truth. Therefore, entrust to them whatever you wish to announce in the 11th hour of the night to a friend who is absent, whether near or far, hesitating in nothing according to the rules of the art.

->Johannes Trithemius, Abbot of Sponheim: To Cornelius Gerard<-
->of Gouda, Canon of the Order of Saint Augustine<-
->near Leiden, sends greetings.<-

# BOOK THE SECOND.

Our Lord Jesus Christ, the gentlest savior of our souls, promised us the happiness of eternal consolation if we always observe his commandments with a pure heart. You, therefore, most dear brother, keep the commandments of God and of the holy rule <note>Referring to the monastic Rule, likely the Rule of St. Benedict, given the author's background.</note> diligently: because unless you keep all things that the omnipotent God has commanded you inviolate until death, you will perish by eternal death. For this reason, I have handed down to you the form and law of religious and holy <term>conduct<gloss>original: "conuersationis." In a monastic context, this refers to the "conversatio morum," the way of life or outward behavior of a monk.</gloss></term>. Take care, therefore, and be diligent, that you do not give your heart to be possessed by any mortal creature through the desire of love: because no one comes to those supreme joys except he who despises the world for the sake of God. 

Again, therefore, through these letters of mine I exhort you, as a most dear friend: observe for yourself my warnings which I have made. For I judge myself happy when I have written something good at this sacred time, especially for the purpose of edifying the manner of living well, by which you may always be made better. For this reason, I write to you again now for your own good: because I fear your danger no less than my own. But I pray: always be vigilant, progressing daily in the fear of God, and living innocently, harming no one, and persevering entirely in your holy purpose. Do not trust in time, nor in fortune, nor in age: because they are nothing but smoke. Truly I love you sincerely in Christ Jesus, praying that he himself may arrange that I also be loved by you, and that I, who am absent from you in body, may always be present in spirit. Hence, as I have memory of you continually in my prayers: I vehemently pray that you do not forget me. Farewell. April 20th <note>original: "12. Calend. Maij." In the Roman calendar, the 12th day before the Kalends of May is April 20th.</note>, in the year 1500.

### Conjuration.

> Sarandiel marso porno ioniel schendiel iano Nati chilpres iosafchar meon prissi dyon volayr penason. <note>original: "Sarandiel marso porno..." This string of seemingly nonsensical words is the "conjuration." To the uninitiated, it looks like a magical incantation; to the recipient, it acts as a key to decode the hidden message that follows.</note>

Once the charm has been spoken, the summoned spirits will immediately stand ready for all tasks.

### Arrangement for announcing the secret.

When you see the spirits standing before you in a strange form: do not be afraid, because they are good and benevolent, harming no one; and they almost always hide their monstrous form—lest they frighten anyone—appearing with a most beautiful face. Securely entrust to them whatever you wish a friend to know during this hour of the night.

## Let letters be formed as you please.
<note>This heading introduces the "cover text." The user would write a pious or philosophical letter like the one below, and the actual secret message would be hidden within its structure according to the rules of the twelfth hour.</note>

> *O wretched man, and truly miserable, why do you not pay heed to the instability of all worldly things? And why do you not reflect that you will die soon? Remember what the first man earned <note>A reference to Adam's fall and the subsequent introduction of mortality.</note> because he violated the commandment. Would that our soul might die the death of the righteous, just as Balaam <note>A reference to Numbers 23:10: "Let me die the death of the righteous, and let my last end be like his!"</note> desired, so that by despising present pleasures and the vain honors of the world, we might be immaculate, guarding ourselves from all sin. But we do not care, until at last we fall into the horrific chains of death, to be tormented forever. I judge that man happy who foresees these things—*

## CHAPTER XXV.

### IN WHICH WE SHALL TEACH THE GENERAL METHOD AND FORM
#### *of approaching this art of Steganography, and of operating within it without danger, with the greatest utility.*

<image-desc>Decorative woodcut drop cap 'Q' featuring floral motifs and a seated human figure within a squared border.</image-desc>Whoever desires to approach the knowledge of this our secret art; whoever wishes to work through it wonders and things advantageous in many dangers: first it is necessary that he be adorned with virtues, and of a clean conscience, and of good will toward God, toward himself, and toward his neighbor: lest he be inclined to harm anyone, nor seek shameful dealings. Then it is necessary that he be somewhat imbued with the good arts of letters, and especially in the <term>science of the stars<gloss>The historical study of both astronomy and astrology, which were not yet separate disciplines.</gloss></term>; that he might know the general movements, courses, discourses, changes, orders, natures, positions, risings, settings, and effects of the stars, signs, and planets; because without a competent knowledge of these, no one can have access to the depths of this art. Consequently, it is necessary that he have a teacher perfect and experienced in the art: because we believe it is not possible to come to this knowledge without a teacher, except for a few and those most learned in many things, and especially skilled in magical matters. However, it is necessary that the teacher be not only proven and experienced in knowledge, but also faithful, upright, and God-fearing: because the purer he is with God in good conduct, the more secure he will be in the operation of this knowledge; for the Spirits obey virtues—

And after the master of this art has explained to his disciple the conjuration of all the spirits, their order and sequence; let him open to him the most secret mysteries <note>original: "arcana secretissima"</note> of this science, which are neither written down nor to be written; forbidding him under a sworn oath to reveal them to anyone as long as he lives, except to those worthy and sworn into the art; nor should he write them in any way or for any reason. Then let him teach him the natures, names, locations, numbers, offices, and properties of all the <term>Spirits (the entities or planetary intelligences who supposedly carry the hidden messages)</term> who are assigned to this art: how, when, and where work is to be done through them: and the remaining mysteries that pertain to the perfection of this art, about which nothing must be written.

# HERE BEGINS THE THIRD BOOK
# OF THE STEGANOGRAPHY OF JOHANNES
## TRITHEMIUS, ABBOT OF SPONHEIM, TO THE MOST SERENE
### Prince and Lord Philip, Count Palatine of
### the Rhine, Duke of Bavaria, and Prince-
### Elector of the Holy Empire.

#### PREFACE.

<image-desc>A large ornamental drop-cap 'P' featuring a detailed floral and vine motif within a square frame, marking the beginning of the preface.</image-desc>

*After we have completed the first two books of our art with the help of our Lord Jesus Christ, the Savior of the souls of the faithful, in which we have described our Steganography as best we could: it is necessary for us to join this third book to those, and to couple even more wonderful inventions to those already established. I found in a certain book by an ancient philosopher, who is called <note>Menastor is an obscure figure, possibly a pseudonym or a fictional authority cited by Trithemius to provide historical weight to his claims.</note> Menastor, that it is possible by a certain art to make the concept of our mind known to a friend, however far away he may be, within twenty-four hours, without words, without books, and without a messenger, in the most perfect, extensive, and secret manner. Since I was driven by a most vehement desire for knowledge, I began to wish to test the truth of his words: and after much labor, I finally found that science to be proven by experience. The secret of this kind of art is immense and wrapped in such great obscurities that it is not easily accessible to anyone. For Menastor shrouded the mystery too obscurely and in too few words; he used such great power to keep the secret hidden that after him, no one could yet be found who dared to make his discourse clearer with any explanation. I, however, fearing that such a wonderful work might be despised by the unskilled because of its excessive obscurity, or eventually burned in a fire—since I have long been expert in it through many trials—wished to commit it to writing in clearer letters, so that with God's help, it might become somewhat accessible to learned men and those most studious in magic; and yet remain hidden at all times from the ignorant <term>Rapophagi (literally "turnip-eaters," a derogatory term Trithemius uses for the uneducated or those with "dull" intellects)</term>, and in no way known to their obtuse understanding.*

*Menastor said: There are seven planets, over which seven Angels preside, and to them are subject twenty-one spirits, through whom secrets are announced. Let us set forth their names by means of a table.*

--- Page 151 ---
<meta>This page transitions from the general spirit tables found on page 141 to the chronological and hierarchical theory behind planetary governance. Trithemius explains how specific angels rule historical epochs—such as the Flood or the Exodus—and then narrows his focus to the specific spirits used in his system of "steganography," beginning with the first planetary angel, Orifiel.</meta>

<header>OF STEGANOGRAPHY</header>

These are the seven Angels of the planets according to the tradition of the ancient wise men, each of whom leads the world for three hundred fifty-four years and four months in their turn. Orifiel is the Angel of Saturn, who from the beginning of creation governed the world for 354 years and 4 months. After him Venus, then Jupiter, after that Mercury, and then Mars, then the Moon, and finally the Sun. Each is described as having held dominion over the world for 354 years and 4 months under their respective Angel as written in the common table. Therefore, the sages say that at the time of the flood <note>The Great Flood of Noah.</note> Samael, the Angel of Mars, served the command of the government; Gabriel, the Angel of the Moon, during the time of the confusion of tongues <note>The Tower of Babel, where humanity's single language was fractured.</note>; and Michael, the Angel of the Sun, during the time of Israel's departure from Egypt. And indeed, if anyone places faith in the aforementioned assertion, they will easily be able to calculate the changes of the times and the governance of the Angels from the origin of the world down to their own age in the prescribed order by themselves. However, pursuing those things which serve our purpose, we do not doubt that there are 21 other spirits subject to the seven planetary Angels mentioned above—that is, three to each—according to the instruction of our <term>art</term> <note>Trithemius uses "art" to refer to his system of steganography, which blends cryptography with the invocation of spirits.</note>: through whom we work the effect of our intention. We shall observe the customary order of the planets, beginning from Saturn, the highest of all, down to the Moon: first describing the operation of each principal angel, and then the subservience [of their followers]: and joining the chapters in order.

### CHAPTER I.

## CONCERNING ORIFIEL, THE FIRST AND HIGHEST ANGEL

->Of Saturn, and the operations that are performed through him.<-

<image-desc>A large decorative woodcut initial 'S' entwined with vine-like foliage and floral motifs.</image-desc>

Saturn, higher and more supreme than all the planets, is slow in its natural motion and cold because it is remote. It is difficult and heavy; from the point of its beginning [it takes] 650 years to the point of its first station, staying in any sign for 30 months. By its own natural motion it is moved 626 <note>These figures refer to complex astrological calculations of the planet's orbit and "stations" where it appears to stop or move retrograde.</note>. Its first and highest Angel is called, according to the opinion of the ancients, Orifiel, who has under him three other principal Spirits, namely Sadael, Poniel, and Morifiel, of whom we shall speak in their proper place and order. Through this Orifiel, the great Angel of Saturn, many and diverse operations are performed in <term>Steganography</term> <note>While the word literally means "hidden writing," in this context it refers to Trithemius's method of sending secret messages through mental or spiritual influence.</note>. Through him we are able

### THIRD BOOK

We are able to make the secret of our mind known to a friend who knows the <term>art</term> <note>The "art" refers to Trithemius’s system of Steganography, which he claims allows for secret communication through spiritual means.</note>, wherever he may be: by letters, if it pleases; or even without letters and to do many other wondrous things, which no one else understands who is unskilled in this science of ours. Therefore, wishing to perform anything in <term>Steganography</term> <note>From the Greek 'steganographia,' meaning 'hidden writing.'</note> through this Angel, especially on the day of Saturn <note>Saturday.</note>, and in those matters, businesses, and causes which pertain to Saturn: first, it is necessary that you know his various and diverse motions. And various first: pure, proper, mixed, direct, retrograde, and confused. And in all these, it is necessary to know not only the general rules handed down by the astronomers (for although they are most helpful to this science of ours, they are not entirely sufficient), but also the particular ones. 

As a foundation, therefore, with the tables and rules of the planetary motions presupposed—which it is absolutely necessary for the practitioner of this art to know—we have added special rules and tables of <term>punctual motions</term> <note>Precise, point-by-point calculations of a planet's position in the sky.</note> through each individual chapter of this work, without the observation of which no one will be able to work in this art. 

And note, that the day of any planet is divided into four equal parts, the first of which (that is, three <term>unequal hours</term> <note>In medieval timekeeping, "unequal hours" were calculated by dividing the period of daylight into 12 parts; thus, a summer "hour" was longer than a winter "hour."</note>) the principal angel of that planet holds: and the remaining three parts his substitutes follow in order. 

For example: On the day of Saturn, Orifiel, his first angel, possesses three unequal hours from sunrise, which are called the planetary hours; his first substitute, Sadael, holds the second three. Poniel, the second, holds the third three; and Morisiel, the third angel of Orifiel, holds the fourth three hours. During the first three hours of the day, whether of Saturn or the other stars in matters appropriated to Saturn, one must work through Orifiel; during the second through Sadael; during the third through Poniel; and during the last three through Morisiel. And all operations receive either a prosperous or a weak effect according to the motion of Saturn. 

But now let us set down the
->punctual table.<-

# STEGANOGRAPHY <note>original: "STEGANOGRAPHIA." The title of the work, meaning "hidden writing."</note>

Having learned the mean motion <note>In historical astronomy, "mean motion" refers to the average speed of a planet, as if it moved in a perfect circle.</note> of Saturn for the second day, degree, and hour from the common tables, you should know that it must first be carefully observed whether the planet is direct or **retrograde**; whether it is pure, proper, mixed, or confused. However, you will not find this information in common tables. Therefore, we have arranged the prescribed table in which you can find the starting and ending point of its motion at any hour, with the degrees divided simultaneously into minute particles. Thus, for any given minute, it should be divided into two, three, or four hourly parts according to the proportion of **pure or confused motion**—that is, by observing the conjunction and separation of other planets.

For it is impossible, either through the stars or through the **Spirits of the stars**, for you to achieve the effect of this art unless you know how to precisely arrange your operations according to the proper qualities of the planets, once the **punctual motion** <term>punctual motion: A specific term in Trithemius's system referring to the exact, minute-by-minute numerical value used as a cryptographic key.</term> is known and most diligently observed. For in the various degrees, signs, days, hours, minutes, seconds, thirds, and fourths, the effects of the planets—as far as this science is concerned—vary in different ways. Indeed, in every degree, we have observed and found 25 different changes; hence it seemed necessary to us to divide every degree into that many principal parts.

However, this division does not take place through all degrees, but only in those in which the stars conducive to this art for us arise, by which there is an operation for signaling everything we wish to those who are absent, through the **ministry of Spirits without letters** <note>This is a classic "steganographic" mask. Trithemius claims to send messages via spirits, but "without letters" likely refers to his method of encoding information into numbers or other non-alphabetic symbols.</note>, as we shall explain. Therefore, the first table of the division of degrees of Saturn's motion, which we provided earlier, serves perpetually for the first quarter of the day or night in matters pertaining to the operation of Saturn and its beginning; the second table serves for the second three hours, the third for the third, and the fourth for the fourth. Next, we have appended the **punctual order** of the motion of Saturn itself in its ascent, namely when it is in one of the first four signs, which are **Aries, Taurus, Gemini, and Cancer**. We will also arrange a table for the remaining eight signs

# OF STEGANOGRAPHY
## On the various movements of the planets, and the interpretation of the Tables.

Although this chapter requires us to provide a specialized treatise on the movements of Saturn and its associated spirits, and how to perform steganographic operations through them, we should not be forced to repeat every apt instruction in every single chapter. The diligent reader is therefore advised to remember what we say in this particular chapter for those that follow. There are various movements for each of the planets, and these are divided into many other subtle categories. What we call "pure and proper" movement is complex and varied by many extremely fine divisions. 

**Direct, retrograde, mixed, and confused**—these movements vary by nearly as many points as the planets diverge from their starting positions along the celestial circle. What shall I say of the way these planets align with one another in "conjunction"? For, as Ptolemy says in the *Centiloquium* <note>original: "Gentiloquio"; a collection of one hundred astrological aphorisms traditionally attributed to the astronomer Claudius Ptolemy</note>, there are one hundred and twenty possible planetary conjunctions: specifically, 21 pairs, 35 groups of three, 35 groups of four, 21 groups of five, 7 groups of six, and 1 single conjunction of all seven. We shall discuss these in their proper place. 

As for the other movements, we cannot set a definite limit because of their infinite variety; they shift daily as points increase or decrease, confusing those who are ignorant of the art. However, to the full extent of our observations, we have identified more than 300,000 divisions, which frequently alter the outcome of the operations in this art. Unless a practitioner is highly skilled in all these matters and possesses a perfect knowledge of the "mean motions" <term>mean motion: the average speed of a planet, calculated as if it moved in a perfect circle rather than an ellipse</term> of the planets, as well as the most minute divisions of points (derived from degrees, minutes, seconds, thirds, and fourths most subtly divided into equal parts), they will easily fall into genuine error and will hardly escape grave danger.

To provide complete instruction in this science, we have therefore arranged three tables. In the first of these, we have reduced the motion of Saturn—calculated according to four parts of both day and night, specifically its "pure and proper" motion—to a foundational numerical value <note>original: "radicem"; literally "root"</note> and aligned it with the true point of whatever sign is rising on the horizon <note>original: "ascendentis"</note>. Starting from the first degree of Aries at point 641, and continuing through Taurus ♉, Gemini ♊, and Cancer ♋, the entire Zodiac is covered in the second

<meta>catchword: table</meta>

<summary>Trithemius explains the astronomical and mathematical complexity underlying his cryptographic system, noting that he has calculated over 300,000 variations of planetary motion to ensure the system's accuracy.</summary>
<keywords>Saturn, planetary motion, conjunctions, Ptolemy, Centiloquium, zodiac signs, mean motion, astronomical tables</keywords>

# BOOK THREE

...we have included in the table. Then, in that same second table, we have arranged the movement of **Saturn** in any of the twelve signs for all quarters of the day and night. This is calculated down to the <term>minutes<note>In astronomical terms, minutes are subdivisions of degrees used to pinpoint a planet's exact location.</note></term>, so that at any hour—once the degree of the <term>ascendant<note>The specific degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at a given moment.</note></term> in which Saturn is located is known— the <term>root<note>original: "radix"; the base numerical value or starting point used for further astronomical calculations.</note></term> of that specific point may be clearly revealed. Once a calculation is finished for a particular person (though I know not if our age could ever reach such precision), the table must be started again from the very beginning.

In the third table, we have reduced the sevenfold conjunction of Saturn with the other planets—which is unique—to the proportion of a single point. Through this, all 120 possible conjunctions are easily reduced to a point. In this same table, the "pure movement" of all planets is recognized through the punctual division of the beginning, progression, and distance from a point in any movement—provided that the <term>mean movement<note>The theoretical average speed of a planet, as if it moved in a perfect circle at a constant speed.</note></term> is perfectly understood beforehand. 

For without a knowledge of the mean movement, it is impossible for anyone to approach the practice of this science. Furthermore, even if you understand both the mean and the "true" movement with total perfection, it will benefit you nothing in the knowledge of this science unless you fully understand the three prescribed tables for all points of division in the movements of the planets (taken equally from unequal degrees) down to every single minute detail. But now, let us proceed to the operation itself.

Once all these things are known and perfectly understood as we have described, and with God’s help, we shall say: when you wish to operate in this deepest of <term>speculations<note>In this context, a "speculation" refers to a theoretical or mystical investigation.</note></term>, you must first know the rising, exaltation, and setting of all the stars of the **eighth sphere** <note>The realm of the "fixed stars," situated beyond the orbits of the seven classical planets.</note>, through which the operation is performed, and how far each star is distant from the others. 

Now, the stars through which we work to announce any secret to someone who is absent—whether by words or by writing, and without a human messenger—are seven hundred in number. We have described their names, locations, risings, settings, elevations, distances, and their forward and backward motions in a special book; it would be far too laborious to repeat those details by inserting them here again. 

Therefore, having observed the star in all respects as is proper, and having determined the point of the ascendant in which **Saturn** was located at that same hour (if the operation is being performed through him), consider most diligently the quality of his movement,

# STEGANOGRAPHIA

...whether it be <term>direct or retrograde<note>In astrology, 'direct' motion is the standard forward movement of a planet, while 'retrograde' is the apparent backward movement. These conditions were thought to strengthen or weaken the planet's influence.</note></term>, pure or mixed, proper or joined with other impediments of <term>malefic aspects<note>original: "aspectuum malorum"; specific angular relationships between planets—like squares or oppositions—believed to cause negative or difficult effects.</note></term>, distinct or confused. Then, write upon a clean sheet of paper the point of the beginning of motion in that same sign, both of **Saturn** itself and of the other planets joined to it, calculated most subtly and properly from the common tables and those we have provided. Carefully observe how much it has progressed, how much it is elevated, or how much it is depressed. You shall do the same for the other planets joined to Saturn or to the one through which the <term>operation<note>original: "operatio"; here referring to the ritual process of preparing the secret message.</note></term> is being performed. 

In a similar way, observe most diligently by which <term>aspect<note>The geometric angle between planets. Trithemius lists the four standard ones: trine (120°), square (90°), conjunction (0°), and sextile (60°).</note></term> the planets regard each other: whether it be trine, square, conjunction, or sextile. Because within these lies the greatest power of this operation. Also, above all, you should observe in which quarter Saturn is located, not only regarding the day or night, but also regarding the sign in which it resides, and regarding the years from the beginning of the world when it was first created in its own <term>house<note>In astrology, a 'house' is a specific zodiac sign where a planet is said to have its strongest influence; for Saturn, this is traditionally Capricorn and Aquarius.</note></term>; for all these things are necessary. 

And note that when **Orifiel**, the angel of Saturn, rules the world in his order, his reign will last for 354 years and four months. At that time, every operation of this most profound science which pertains to the works of Saturn and is performed through him is easy, and will achieve a clear effect without great labor. The same must be observed regarding the other planets. 

Whoever, therefore, having learned the principles of this art, wishes to operate through it without difficulty, should operate through that planet whose principal angel is found by easy calculation to be governing the world at that time. Therefore, having calculated everything most diligently and being about to proceed to the operation, observe which angel of Saturn presides over that <term>quarter<note>original: "quartæ"; a division of the day or night used to determine which spirit is active.</note></term> of the day or night; and write his name along with the name of the star through which you wish to operate, which it is necessary for you to see. By the art we have handed down, you will be able to see the necessary stars at any time, whether by day or by night. 

Next, write on the same small sheet the other things which you know to be most secret, using <term>ink<note>original: "encausto"; likely referring to specialized or invisible inks described in the earlier books of Steganographia.</note></term> prepared for this purpose according to our custom, as you know. Afterward, place that slip which you have written before you on the table, and write on another sheet the concept of your mind—what you wish the absent person to know—and wrap these two slips together, and place them in a more prominent location before you on the table or a desk. And

# BOOK THREE

...say the conjuration to the <term>Spirit<note>In Trithemius's system, "Spirits" are synonymous with the planetary angels who act as messengers for the operator’s thoughts.</note></term>, through whom you work, which is appropriate to him. Or, if you would prefer to announce a secret through a Spirit—or even without a Spirit—entirely without writing, let that be left to your own judgment. For the <term>operation<note>original: "operatio"; the ritual or technical procedure of the steganographic system.</note></term> is of the same nature, whether announcing a secret to a friend with a Spirit or without a Spirit, with only a few things changed, as we shall discuss in the proper place. Now we shall arrange the differences between the operations of Saturn and his Angels according to the four principal quarters of the day, with the conjurations appropriate to each in order; these are to be used when a secret is being communicated through a Spirit.

### *Concerning the first Angel of Saturn, who is the principal, and is called Orifiel, who governs the first quarter of Saturn.*

<image-desc>Decorative initial letter P with foliate and floral ornamentation.</image-desc>
The first and principal angel of Saturn, as we have said in the preceding sections, is called **Orifiel**. Along with the other angels of the planets in their respective orders, he rules the <term>orb<note>original: "orbem"; the celestial sphere or the historical age governed by a specific planet.</note></term> for 354 years and four months. He holds the first part of the cycle in both the day and the night. Through him, the operation is performed from the rising of the Sun during the day, and from the setting of the Sun at night, up to and including the third hour in all matters pertaining to Saturn, and on his specific day and night, as we have mentioned. 

When, therefore, you wish to announce something to an absent person during the first quarter of Saturn without words and without letters, you must call upon no other Spirit of Saturn than **Orifiel**, because through him you will be able to communicate everything most perfectly. First, consider in which zodiac sign this Saturn is located, and at which degree; observe whether it is <term>direct or retrograde<note>Direct motion is forward; retrograde is the apparent backward motion of a planet, often considered a time of weakened or inverted influence in astrology.</note></term>, and with which planets it forms a <term>conjunction<note>When two celestial bodies appear in the same area of the sky.</note></term>. 

Let us set an example for this first part: Behold, Saturn is now in the 25th degree of Taurus, on the 28th day of the month of April in the present year, which is the year of our Lord 1500. Now, therefore, let us multiply 25 by 24, which makes 600. Let us divide these by the four equal quarters: 150 will remain for each quarter. Let us calculate all the degrees of Saturn within the sign of Taurus, which are 30. Let us multiply 30 by 25 in total, and it makes 750. Let us discard the one hundred superfluous degrees from the middle of the third and fourth quarters, and 650 will remain. Let us now take the point of Saturn’s motion from the table for the present day and...

Once these things are known, our <term>operation<note>The "operation" refers to the specific ritual or technical method Trithemius uses to "send" a message through the planetary angel.</note></term> will be easy.

Having most diligently considered these things, it is established that **Orifiel**, the Angel of Saturn, is separated from Saturn by 25 degrees and 15 minutes; and he is toward the East, at a distance of 625 gradations from the first point of Saturn's motion, and 25 minutes from the end of the motion. Once these are known, make an image out of wax, or paint on new paper a new figure of Orifiel in the likeness of a bearded and naked man, standing upon a bull of various colors, holding a book in his right hand and a <term>pen<note>original: "calamum"; a reed pen or stylus used for writing.</note></term> in his left. While you are making this, say:

> Let this image of the great Orifiel be made full, perfect, and fit to announce the secret of my mind to <note>N. filio N.</note> [Name], son of [Name], my friend, securely, faithfully, and completely. Amen. <note>original: "Fiat hæc imago magni Orifielis plena, perfecta & apta nunciare mentis meæ secretum N. filio N. amico meo secure, fideliter & integre, Amé." The "N." serves as a placeholder for the names of the sender and recipient.</note>

Write your name on the forehead with <term>ink<note>original: "encausto"; specifically a prepared ink or pigment.</note></term> tempered with oil of roses, and on the

### BOOK THREE.

V 2

...on the breast, write the name of your absent friend, saying:
> <note>original: "Hæc eſt imago N. filij N. cui per Angelum Saturni Orifielem nunciatur mentis meæ conceptũ, Amen."</note> This is the image of [Name], son of [Name], to whom the concept of my mind is announced through Orifiel, the Angel of Saturn. Amen.

On the forehead of the image write **Merion** and on the breast **Troefda**. Then join both images together, saying:
> In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen. Hear, Orifiel, prince of the Star of Saturn, and by the power of almighty God I adjure you, listen: I command and mandate you by the power of this, your image, that you announce to [Name], son of [Name], this my intention—<note>At this point, the practitioner explains the specific message they wish to send.</note>—as quickly, securely, secretly, and faithfully as possible, omitting nothing of those things which I wish him to know, and which I have commended to you. In the name of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit, Amen.

Then wrap the images, thus joined together, in a clean cloth washed with white-water <note>original: "aqua albiſine" – likely a reference to distilled or ritualistically purified water</note>, and place them in a portable vessel which the wise men of India call a <term>pharnat alronda<note>A technical term used in this system, likely a non-existent or "barbarous" name intended to sound exotic and ancient.</note></term>. Cover it carefully on top with scraped leather, and place it at the entrance of some closed dwelling, wherever you wish, for the space of 24 hours. Without any hesitation, your desire will be perfectly fulfilled within those 24 hours: your absent friend will know your intention in its most perfect form, exactly as you spoke it over the image (no matter how long the message was). Whatever you wished him to know from you, he will know in those 24 hours perfectly and most secretly, so that no man can ever perceive or know this without your will or that of your friend. This is a great secret, which none of the wise men before us dared to commit to writing. Moreover, if that friend of yours wishes you to know something of his own affairs, he can make you more certain with the same spirit within the same 24-hour period, provided he is well-perfected in this art.

You, however, once the 24 hours have elapsed, lift the images from the place where they were put and store them away. You can work through them at any time belonging to Orifiel, not only for that specific friend but for any others whatsoever, merely by changing the name of the friend to the name of the person you wish to know something. And note, that it is not necessary for the images themselves to be painted beautifully or with great care; however simple they may be, it matters not, provided they have some proportionate likeness so that they are recognized as images of men. For one who wishes and is able to paint them beautifully: it will neither hinder nor help the operation.

### Concerning the second Angel of Saturn, who is the first under Orifiel and is called Sadael, presiding over the second quarter of Saturn.

**T**he second Angel of Saturn is called Sadael, who is established as the first under Orifiel in the order; he holds the second part after his prince, both in the day and in the night, in the works and times of Saturn—that is, the fourth, fifth, and sixth <term>unequal hours<note>Also known as "planetary hours," these divide the period of daylight and the period of darkness into 12 hours each. Unlike our modern 60-minute hours, their length changes depending on the season.</note></term>. Therefore, when you wish to announce something to an absent friend within these three hours—to be delivered within twenty-four hours, without letters, without words, and without a messenger—do so through each and every thing we described in the preceding operation. You must observe the point of the second motion of Saturn in its degrees toward the sign in which the planet itself is moving, and the nature of that motion, and where the <term>Spirit moving the star<note>In the medieval worldview, celestial bodies were thought to be guided in their orbits by divine intelligences or spirits.</note></term> is located: how far it has receded, how much it is elevated from the center of its motion, and its distance from any point of the entire circle or sphere. To achieve the knowledge of these things, use this table.

<image-desc>A cryptographic or astronomical data layout. The main section consists of six columns of numbers with headers R. and S. for different unequal hours. Large curly braces group pairs of columns. Between these columns are specific annotated values. To the right of the first large table is the astrological symbol for Saturn and the letter R. A smaller two-column table appears at the bottom left.</image-desc>

<margin>As above.</margin>
<margin>Note well.</margin>
In place of the angel Sadael, found by considering the fixed star at the point of Saturn's motion, make two images (in the same manner we described in the preceding operation) of Orifiel with the inscriptions, <term>invocations<note>The Latin 'contestationibus' implies a formal calling upon spirits to witness or fulfill an oath.</note></term>, and other details mentioned there. Once these are completed, place the images upon five small branches of the herb *thrasnota* <note>original: "herbæ thrasnotæ." This likely refers to a specific plant used in these rites; in the context of Trithemius, it is often a coded or specialized name for a botanical element used in the construction of magical charms.</note> outside the house in a secure and secret place, and say over them: In the name of the † Father and the † Son and the † Holy Spirit, Amen. Just as I have placed these two images of the angel Sadael and [Name], son of [Name], my friend, joined together upon these five moving branches, so I command you, Sadael, by the power of Orifiel your great prince, that you carry the secret of my mind, which I have entrusted to you for explanation, without delay to the knowledge of my aforementioned friend. Once these words are spoken, leave the images in that same spot for twenty-four hours; and what you desired shall come to pass: and your friend shall know everything you spoke over the images, most perfectly and secretly. Likewise, if you wish to know how any absent person is doing, and their true state within twenty-four hours, no matter how far away they are, you will be able to know it in this way. **AND ALL THINGS THAT HAPPEN IN THE WORLD, BY OBSERVING THE CONSTELLATION, YOU WILL BE ABLE TO KNOW THROUGH THIS ART.**

### Concerning the third Angel of Saturn, who is the second under Orifiel, and is called Pomiel, presiding over the third quarter of Saturn.

The third angel of Saturn is called Pomiel, who is second in rank under Orifiel, the principal angel of Saturn; he presides over the seventh, eighth, and ninth <term>planetary hours<note>Unequal hours calculated based on the position of the planet Saturn.</note></term> of Saturn, both by day and by night, in all matters pertaining to the work of Saturn. His location—since he moves with his order toward the fixed stars at fifteen degrees from the East, arranged by tables in his hours—you shall find in this way.
